# hep-ex [[Back]](#toc)

### [1] [Updates on the DEAP-3600 experiment and steps towards the ARGO experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27372)
*Susnata Seth*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: DEAP-3600, the largest single-phase liquid argon dark matter detector (3.3 t), sets leading WIMP-nucleon cross-section limits (>30 GeV/c²) and probes superheavy multi-scattering DM; recent work improves LAr property modeling, position reconstruction, and α-background suppression; next-generation 300-t ARGO detector is under development within GADMC, informed by neutron background simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve sensitivity to weakly interacting massive particles (WIMPs) and exotic dark matter candidates (e.g., superheavy, multi-scattering DM) by mitigating dominant backgrounds (especially α-induced) and enhancing reconstruction fidelity in large-scale liquid argon detectors.

Method: Analysis of DEAP-3600 data to refine liquid argon scintillation properties and 3D position reconstruction algorithms; hardware upgrades for α-background suppression; Monte Carlo simulations of radiogenic neutron backgrounds and mitigation strategies for the future ARGO detector design.

Result: Improved understanding of LAr scintillation response and position resolution; reduced α-background contamination; validated neutron background models informing ARGO’s radiopurity and shielding design; world-leading spin-independent WIMP exclusion limits above 30 GeV/c² on argon.

Conclusion: DEAP-3600 has advanced the state-of-the-art in single-phase LAr dark matter detection through empirical calibration, background control, and analysis innovation; its lessons directly enable the scalable, high-sensitivity ARGO experiment as part of the GADMC roadmap.

Abstract: The DEAP-3600 experiment, with an approximately 3.3 tonne liquid argon (LAr) target, is currently the world's largest single-phase LAr dark matter detector. It is located 2 km underground at SNOLAB, Canada, one of the most radiopure underground laboratories. With excellent pulse-shape discrimination against low-energy beta decays and precise position reconstruction, DEAP-3600 has set the most stringent WIMP-nucleon spin-independent cross-section exclusion limits for masses above 30 GeV/c$^{2}$ on argon and provided leading sensitivity to superheavy, multi-scattering dark matter candidate. Here we report the recent advances in understanding LAr properties and position reconstruction techniques using DEAP-3600 data along with hardware upgrades to mitigate residual challenging $α$-backgrounds for WIMP search. As a part of Global Argon Dark Matter Collaboration (GADMC), the next-generation ARGO detector, featuring a 300-tonne fiducial LAr mass, is under development to significantly enhance sensitivity to rare dark matter interactions. Simulation-based studies of radiogenic neutron backgrounds and their mitigation strategies provide essential input to this design and will be described here.

</details>

Zheng,T. Zhou,X. Zhou,Y. Zhou,V. Zhovkovska,L. Z. Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo,Q. Zou,D. Zuliani,G. Zunica*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports the first measurement of CP asymmetry in B_s^0 → D_s^+ D^- decays and the most precise measurement to date for B^0 → D_s^- D^+ decays, using 9 fb⁻¹ of LHCb pp collision data; both results are consistent with CP conservation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test CP symmetry in charmless hadronic B decays, particularly in the underexplored B_s^0 → D_s^+ D^- channel, and improve precision on the known B^0 → D_s^- D^+ asymmetry.

Method: Analysis of proton-proton collision data collected by LHCb, corresponding to 9 fb⁻¹ integrated luminosity; extraction of combined CP asymmetries A_CP via flavor-tagged, time-integrated yield comparisons between particle and antiparticle decays.

Result: A_CP(B^0 → D_s^- D^+) = 0.0009 ± 0.0053 (stat) ± 0.0040 (syst); A_CP(B_s^0 → D_s^+ D^-) = 0.103 ± 0.053 (stat) ± 0.010 (syst); both consistent with zero within uncertainties.

Conclusion: No evidence of CP violation is observed in either decay mode; the B_s^0 result is the first of its kind, and the B^0 result sets a new precision benchmark—supporting the Standard Model expectation of small CP violation in these tree-dominated transitions.

Abstract: Measurements of the combined CP asymmetries in $\kern 0.18em\overline{\kern -0.18em B}^0 \to D_s^- D^+$ and $\kern 0.18em\overline{\kern -0.18em B}_s^0 \to D_s^+ D^-$ decays are made using proton-proton collision data collected by the LHCb experiment, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 9fb$^{-1}$. The measurements are found to be \begin{aligned} A_{CP}(\kern 0.18em\overline{\kern -0.18em B}^0 \to D_s^- D^+) &= 0.0009 \pm 0.0053 \pm 0.0040, \\ A_{CP}(\kern 0.18em\overline{\kern -0.18em B}_s^0 \to D_s^+ D^-) &= 0.103\phantom{0} \pm 0.053\phantom{0} \pm 0.010, \end{aligned} where the first and second uncertainties are statistical and systematic, respectively. This is the first measurement of this asymmetry in $\kern 0.18em\overline{\kern -0.18em B}_s^0$ decays, and the most precise measurement to date for $\kern 0.18em\overline{\kern -0.18em B}^0$ decays. Both measurements are found to be consistent with CP symmetry.

</details>

R. Zhou,X. Y. Zhou,Y. X. Zhou,Y. Z. Zhou,A. N. Zhu,J. Zhu,K. Zhu,K. J. Zhu,K. S. Zhu,L. X. Zhu,Lin Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,J. H. Zou*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First observation of Λc+→nπ+η decay with 9.5σ significance; branching fraction ratio measured; no evidence for Λc+→na0(980)+ intermediate state; Transformer-based deep learning used for background suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To search for the previously unobserved charmed baryon decay Λc+→nπ+η and probe possible intermediate resonant structures (e.g., a0(980)+), thereby testing QCD dynamics and SU(3) flavor symmetry in charm baryon decays.

Method: Analysis of 6.1 fb⁻¹ e⁺e⁻ collision data at √s = 4.600–4.843 GeV collected by BESIII; invariant mass reconstruction of nπ⁺η final state; sophisticated Transformer-based deep learning model for signal-background discrimination; full treatment of statistical and systematic uncertainties, including reference branching fraction uncertainty.

Result: First observation of Λc+→nπ+η (9.5σ); ℬ(Λc+→nπ+η)/ℬ(Λc+→Λπ+η) = 0.155 ± 0.031(stat.) ± 0.012(syst.); absolute ℬ(Λc+→nπ+η) = (2.94 ± 0.59(stat.) ± 0.23(syst.) ± 0.13(ref.))×10⁻³; upper limit ℬ(Λc+→na0(980)⁺)×ℬ(a0(980)⁺→π⁺η) < 8.4×10⁻⁴ (90% CL).

Conclusion: The observation confirms a new decay mode of the Λc⁺ baryon and provides constraints on non-resonant vs. resonant contributions in charm baryon hadronic decays; the absence of a0(980)⁺ signal disfavors significant coupling of Λc⁺ to na0(980)⁺, informing theoretical models of scalar meson participation in weak decays.

Abstract: By analysing 6.1 ${\rm fb}^{-1}$ of data collected at center-of-mass energies between $\sqrt{s}=4.600$ and 4.843 $\rm GeV$ with the BESIII detector at the BEPCII collider, we observe the decay $Λ_c^+\to nπ^+η$ for the first time with a statistical significance of $9.5σ$. The ratio of branching fractions $\mathcal{B}(Λ_c^+\to nπ^+η)/\mathcal{B}(Λ_c^+\to Λπ^+η)$ is measured to be $0.155\pm0.031_{\rm stat.}\pm0.012_{\rm syst.}$ Taking the world average of $\mathcal{B}(Λ_c^+\to Λπ^+η)$ as reference, the absolute branching fraction is calculated to be $\mathcal{B}(Λ_c^+\to nπ^+η)=(2.94\pm0.59_{\rm stat.}\pm0.23_{\rm syst.}\pm0.13_{\rm ref.})\times10^{-3}$. The intermediate process $Λ_c^+\to na_0(980)^+$ is also searched for in the $π^+η$ invariant mass spectrum. Since no significant signal is found, the upper limit on $\mathcal{B}(Λ_c^+\to na_0(980)^+)\times\mathcal{B}(a_0(980)^+\toπ^+η)$ is set to $8.4\times10^{-4}$ at 90\% confidence level. A sophisticated deep learning approach using a Transformer-based architecture is employed to distinguish signals from prevalent hadronic backgrounds, complemented by thorough validation and systematic uncertainty quantification.

</details>

Zhelezov,S. Z. Zheng,X. Z. Zheng,Y. Zheng,T. Zhou,X. Zhou,V. Zhovkovska,L. Z. Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo,D. Zuliani,G. Zunica*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First observation of the doubly charmed baryon Ξ_cc^+ via Λ_c^+ K^- π^+ decay using LHCb Run 3 data (13.6 TeV, 6.9 fb⁻¹), with >7σ significance; mass measured as 3619.97 ± 0.83 ± 0.26^{+1.90}_{−1.30} MeV/c²; mass difference with Ξ_cc^{++} is −1.77 ± 0.84 ± 0.15^{+1.90}_{−1.30} MeV/c².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To discover and characterize the doubly charmed baryon Ξ_cc^+, a key prediction of the Standard Model whose existence and properties test QCD in the heavy-quark regime and inform baryon spectroscopy and production mechanisms.

Method: Analysis of proton-proton collision data from LHCb Run 3 at √s = 13.6 TeV; reconstruction and selection of Ξ_cc^+ → Λ_c^+ K^- π^+ decays; invariant mass fitting with background modeling; systematic uncertainties evaluated including lifetime-dependent mass bias.

Result: Observation of Ξ_cc^+ with statistical significance >7σ; precise mass measurement; first determination of mass difference between Ξ_cc^+ and Ξ_cc^{++}; confirmation of production in pp collisions at 13.6 TeV.

Conclusion: The Ξ_cc^+ baryon is unambiguously observed for the first time, marking the inaugural new-particle discovery with the upgraded LHCb Run 3 detector and providing critical input for understanding doubly heavy baryon dynamics and QCD predictions.

Abstract: The first observation of the doubly charmed baryon $\itΞ_{cc}^+$ is reported through its decay to the $\itΛ_c^+ K^-π^+$ final state, with a statistical significance exceeding seven standard deviations. The observation is made using proton-proton collision data collected in 2024 with the LHCb Run 3 detector at a center-of-mass energy of 13.6 TeV, corresponding to a total integrated luminosity of $6.9\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$. The $\itΞ_{cc}^+$ mass is measured to be $3619.97 \pm 0.83 \pm 0.26 \,^{+1.90}_{-1.30}\,\mathrm{MeV}/c^2$, where the first uncertainty is statistical, the second is systematic, and the third is due to the unknown $\itΞ_{cc}^+$ lifetime, which is assumed to lie in the range 15-160 fs with a baseline value of 45 fs. The difference between the masses of the $\itΞ_{cc}^+$ and $\itΞ_{cc}^{++}$ baryons is determined to be $-1.77 \pm 0.84 \pm 0.15 \,^{+1.90}_{-1.30}\,\mathrm{MeV}/c^2$. This is the first observation of a new particle made with the LHCb Run 3 detector.

</details>


### [5] [Searching for the Dark Photon with PADME](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28685)
*Kalina Dimitrova*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: PADME experiment at Frascati searches for dark photons via positron-electron annihilation and missing-mass reconstruction; this paper details analysis methods, background composition, and rejection strategies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To search for the dark photon—a hypothetical gauge boson mediating interactions between visible and hidden sectors—using a high-sensitivity missing-mass technique in positron–electron annihilation.

Method: Missing-mass analysis of e⁺e⁻ → γA′ events, where beam positrons annihilate with target electrons; the visible photon is detected in an electromagnetic calorimeter, and the dark photon mass is reconstructed from beam energy and measured photon energy/momentum.

Result: Development and validation of analysis techniques, characterization of dominant backgrounds (e.g., radiative Bhabha scattering, cosmic rays, beam-related backgrounds), and implementation of multivariate and kinematic cuts for background suppression.

Conclusion: The described analysis framework enables sensitive dark photon searches in the 1–23 MeV/c² mass range; background rejection is robust and consistent with Monte Carlo expectations, supporting physics results from PADME’s data-taking campaigns.

Abstract: The PADME Experiment at Laboratori Nationali di Frascati is designed to search for the Dark Photon, a hypothetical gauge boson responsible for the interaction between the visible and the hidden sector. PADME explores the process of annihilation of beam positrons with the electrons in a fixed target, employing the missing mass technique: in case the annihilation results in the associate production of one visible and one Dark photon, the first can be registered by the experiment's electromagnetic calorimeter and the Dark Photon mass can be reconstructed knowing the beam energy. This paper presents the analysis techniques that are being employed for the PADME data, as well as the background composition and rejection procedure.

</details>


### [6] [Inclusive jet cross section in $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s} = 200$ and $510$ GeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28695)
*The STAR Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports double-differential inclusive-jet cross sections in pp collisions at √s = 200 and 510 GeV measured by STAR at RHIC, probing the poorly constrained low-x gluon PDF via gg/qg processes; jets are corrected for underlying event using an off-axis cone method, and results are compared to NNLO pQCD predictions with various PDF sets to constrain the gluon PDF and support QGP studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain the gluon parton distribution function (PDF) in the proton at low Bjorken-x (x_T), where it remains poorly determined by TeV-scale colliders, by measuring inclusive jets—dominated by gg and qg processes—in pp collisions at RHIC energies.

Method: Measurement of double-differential inclusive-jet cross sections as a function of jet transverse momentum (p_T) and pseudorapidity (η) using the STAR detector; jet p_T corrected for underlying event contributions via an off-axis cone method; comparison to next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO) pQCD calculations with multiple modern PDF sets.

Result: Inclusive jet cross sections at √s = 200 and 510 GeV over |η| < 0.9, covering 0.07 < x_T < 0.5 (200 GeV) and 0.03 < x_T < 0.31 (510 GeV); quantitative comparisons to NNLO theory show sensitivity to gluon PDF parametrizations, especially in the low-x_T region.

Conclusion: These high-precision jet data provide new constraints on the gluon PDF at low x, improve tuning of Monte Carlo event generators, and serve as essential baseline measurements for jet quenching studies in heavy-ion collisions aimed at characterizing the quark-gluon plasma.

Abstract: Jets are collimated clusters of particles formed by the hadronization of partons following a hard interaction. In proton-proton ($pp$) collisions at the Relativistic Heavy Ion Collider (RHIC), jet production is dominated by $gg$ and $qg$ partonic processes, allowing us to directly probe the gluon parton distribution function (PDF) in the proton in a way complementary to deep inelastic scattering. In this paper, we report the double-differential inclusive-jet cross sections as a function of jet transverse momentum, $p_{\rm T}$, and pseudorapidity, $η$, at center-of-mass energies $\sqrt{s} = 200$ and $510$~GeV, from $pp$ collisions studied with the STAR detector. The jet $p_{\rm T}$ is corrected for underlying event contributions by applying an off-axis cone method. At mid-pseudorapidity, $|η| < 0.9$, the kinematic coverage of our data extends to $0.07 < x_{\rm T} \text{ (}= 2p_{\rm T}{} / \sqrt{s} \text{)} < 0.5$ and $0.03 < x_{\rm T} < 0.31$ at $\sqrt{s} = 200$~and 510 GeV, respectively, where the gluon PDF is poorly constrained by the TeV-scale $pp$~($p\bar{p}$) colliders. The inclusive jet cross sections are compared to the next-to-next-to-leading order perturbative quantum chromodynamics calculations using several recent PDF sets as inputs. These results will further constrain the gluon PDF, help tune Monte Carlo generators, and provide critical reference data needed to study the quark-gluon plasma.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [7] [Symmetry analysis and exact solutions of multi-layer quasi-geostrophic problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26959)
*Serhii D. Koval,Alex Bihlo,Roman O. Popovych*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs an extended symmetry analysis of the multi-layer quasi-geostrophic model, deriving its conservation laws, Hamiltonian structure, Lie invariance algebra, and full point-symmetry pseudogroup; it classifies subalgebras and carries out systematic Lie reductions, yielding uncoupled linear PDEs (e.g., Helmholtz, Klein-Gordon, Bessel), whose solvability depends on spectral properties of the vertical coupling matrix; exact solutions—including Rossby waves, hetons, and dipolar vortices—are constructed and validated against real oceanographic data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically uncover the full symmetry structure and integrable substructures of the multi-layer quasi-geostrophic (QG) model—a fundamental framework for geophysical fluid dynamics—enabling derivation of new exact solutions with physical relevance to oceanic and atmospheric phenomena.

Method: Extended Lie symmetry analysis: computation of maximal Lie invariance algebra and complete point-symmetry pseudogroup; classification of one- and two-dimensional subalgebras; codimension-one to -three Lie reductions; spectral analysis of the vertical coupling matrix; Hamiltonian formulation and conservation law derivation via variational and Noether methods.

Result: First complete description of conservation laws and Hamiltonian structure for general multi-layer QG; explicit forms of Lie symmetries and pseudogroup; reduction to uncoupled linear PDEs (Helmholtz, modified Helmholtz, Laplace, Klein-Gordon, Whittaker, Bessel, linearized BBM); families of exact nontrivial solutions including stationary/travelling Rossby waves, baroclinic eddies, hetons, and localized dipolar vortices; validation against three-layer ocean data.

Conclusion: The multi-layer quasi-geostrophic model admits rich symmetry structure enabling systematic reduction to integrable linear subsystems; spectral properties of the vertical coupling matrix govern solution behavior; the derived exact solutions provide physically interpretable, data-consistent descriptions of key geophysical coherent structures.

Abstract: We carry out an extended symmetry analysis of the multi-layer quasi-geostrophic problem. This model is given by a system of an arbitrary number of coupled barotropic vorticity equations. Conservation laws and a Hamiltonian structure for the general case of the model are correctly described for the first time. Using original methods, we compute the maximal Lie invariance algebra and the complete point-symmetry pseudogroup of the model. After classifying one- and two-dimensional subalgebras of the Lie invariance algebra, we exhaustively study codimension-one, -two and -three Lie reductions. Notably, among invariant submodels of the original nonlinear model, we obtain uncoupled systems of well-known linear equations, including the Helmholtz, modified Helmholtz, Laplace, Klein-Gordon, Whittaker, Bessel and linearized Benjamin-Bona-Mahony equations. Integration of these systems significantly depends on spectral properties of the model's vertical coupling matrix, which we also revisit in detail. As a result, we construct wide families of exact solutions, including rediscovered representations of stationary and travelling baroclinic Rossby waves, coherent baroclinic eddies, hetons and localized dipolar vortices. We illustrate the physical relevance of obtained solutions using real-world geophysical data for a three-layer ocean model.

</details>


### [8] [Semiclassical shape resonances for magnetic Stark Hamiltonians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27196)
*Kentaro Kameoka,Naoya Yoshida*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves a one-to-one correspondence between shape resonances of 2D magnetic Stark Hamiltonians and eigenvalues of a reference operator in the semiclassical limit, yielding Weyl law and asymptotics for resonance real parts near potential well bottoms, using complex distortion via exterior complex translation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish spectral properties—particularly existence, distribution, and asymptotics—of shape resonances for two-dimensional magnetic Stark Hamiltonians under semiclassical scaling, where a constant magnetic field couples with a scalar potential that is a perturbation of a linear potential and possesses wells.

Method: Complex scaling (specifically, complex translation outside a compact set) to define a complex-distorted Hamiltonian; spectral analysis linking resonances (as complex eigenvalues of the distorted operator) to discrete eigenvalues of a suitably constructed reference operator; semiclassical microlocal techniques to derive asymptotics and counting laws.

Result: Existence of a bijective correspondence between shape resonances and eigenvalues of a reference operator; validity of the Weyl law for the number of resonances; precise asymptotic description of the real parts of resonances near the bottom of a potential well.

Conclusion: Shape resonances of the 2D magnetic Stark Hamiltonian in the semiclassical regime are fully characterized by the discrete spectrum of a reference operator, enabling quantitative semiclassical spectral asymptotics including counting and localization of resonance energies.

Abstract: We study shape resonances of two-dimensional magnetic Stark Hamiltonians in the semiclassical limit. The magnetic field is assumed to be constant and the scalar potential is a perturbation of a linear potential. Under the assumption that the scalar potential has potential wells, the existence of a one-to-one correspondence between shape resonances of the Hamiltonian and discrete eigenvalues of a certain reference operator is proved. This implies the Weyl law for the number of resonances and the asymptotic behavior of the real parts of resonances near the bottom of a potential well. Resonances are studied as complex eigenvalues of complex distorted Hamiltonians, which is defined by the complex translation outside a compact set.

</details>


### [9] [Quantum Hall States response to toroidal geometry deformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27319)
*Bruno Mera,José M. Mourão,João P. Nunes,Carolina Paiva*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses geometric quantization, complex-time Hamiltonian evolution, and generalized coherent state transforms (gCST) to analyze how integer and fractional quantum Hall states—specifically Laughlin states—respond to toroidal geometry deformations, distinguishing flat (non-periodic quadratic Hamiltonians) and non-flat Kähler (bi-periodic, τ-preserving Mabuchi geodesics) cases, with analytic solutions for S¹-invariant deformations up to curvature singularity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically study geometric response of quantum Hall states under toroidal deformations—particularly beyond flat metrics—using geometric quantization tools, and to establish a novel gCST-based framework that bridges quantum Hall physics with Kähler geometry and Mabuchi geodesics.

Method: Geometric quantization combined with imaginary-time Hamiltonian evolution; generalized coherent state transforms (gCST) to track Laughlin state evolution; analysis of two deformation classes: (i) flat toroidal deformations governed by quadratic, non-periodic translation-generator Hamiltonians; (ii) non-flat Kähler deformations generated by global bi-periodic Hamiltonians inducing τ-preserving Mabuchi geodesics in the space of Kähler metrics; restriction to S¹-invariant case enables explicit analytic solutions.

Result: Explicit analytic expressions for both toroidal geometry evolution and Laughlin state evolution under S¹-invariant non-flat Kähler deformations, terminating at a finite-imaginary-time curvature singularity; demonstration that gCST provides a consistent and novel framework for flat deformations, validating its use for more general Kähler settings.

Conclusion: The gCST approach offers a powerful and geometrically transparent method to probe quantum Hall responses to metric deformations; non-flat Kähler deformations reveal intrinsic connections to Mabuchi geometry and singularity formation, suggesting deeper links between quantum Hall universality classes and Kähler moduli space structure.

Abstract: In this paper, we apply techniques of geometric quantization to study the response of the integer and fractional quantum Hall effects to toroidal geometry deformation. The main method is that of using complex time Hamiltonian evolution to induce the geometry change and then the associated generalized coherent state transforms (gCST) to find the evolution of the Laughlin states. We consider two kinds of deformations. The first are flat toroidal deformations. Although Laughlin states for all flat toroidal geometries have been thoroughly studied before, we believe that our approach via the gCST is novel. It also serves as a testing ground to study the non-flat Kähler deformations. The Hamiltonians used in the flat deformations are quadratic in the generators of translations and therefore non periodic. The second kind of deformations involve nonflat Kähler toroidal deformations, generated by global, thus bi-periodic, Hamiltonians on the torus. The corresponding imaginary time flows are (elliptic curve modulus) $τ$-preserving Mabuchi geodesics in the space of Kähler metrics on the torus, hitting a curvature singularity in finite imaginary time. By restricting to $S^1$-invariant deformations we find explicit analytic expressions for the evolution of the toroidal geometry and of the Laughlin states all the way to the singularity.

</details>


### [10] [The Full Set of KMS-States for Abelian Kitaev Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27436)
*Danilo Polo Ojito,Emil Prodan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a C*-algebraic framework for abelian Kitaev models by identifying the vertex/face operator subalgebra as a C*-diagonal of the quasilocal observable UHF algebra, enabling description via a Weyl groupoid; it then classifies all KMS states using (c_H,β)-KMS measures on the groupoid and proves uniqueness across all inverse temperatures β ≥ 0, with the zero-temperature limit recovering the unique frustration-free ground state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize thermal equilibrium states (KMS states) and ground states of abelian Kitaev topological quantum codes using advanced C*-algebraic and groupoid methods, bridging algebraic quantum statistical mechanics with topological order.

Method: Proof that the vertex/face operator subalgebra C is a C*-diagonal of the UHF algebra A of quasilocal observables; construction of the associated Weyl groupoid G_C; realization of A as a groupoid C*-algebra; definition and analysis of the dynamics via a groupoid 1-cocycle c_H; classification of KMS states using (c_H,β)-KMS measures on G_C.

Result: Complete classification and uniqueness of KMS states for all β ∈ [0,∞); existence and identification of the β → ∞ limit as the unique frustration-free ground state.

Conclusion: The abelian Kitaev model admits a canonical groupoid C*-algebraic description wherein thermal equilibrium and ground states are fully and uniquely determined by groupoid dynamical data—establishing a rigorous link between topological quantum order and noncommutative geometry in quantum statistical mechanics.

Abstract: We first prove that the subalgebra $\mathcal{C}$ generated by the vertex and face operators of an abelian Kitaev model is a $C^\ast$-diagonal of the UHF algebra $\mathcal{A}$ of quasilocal observables. This gives us access to the Weyl groupoid $\mathcal{G}_\mathcal{C}$ associated with the $C^\ast$-inclusion $\mathcal{C} \hookrightarrow \mathcal{A}$, which supplies a valuable presentation of $\mathcal{A}$ as a groupoid $C^\ast$-algebra where the dynamics of the model are generated by a groupoid 1-cocycle $c_H$. Making appeal to the notion of $(c_H,β)$-KMS measures for this groupoid, we identify the full set of KMS states of the model and prove its uniqueness for $β\in [0,\infty)$. Furthermore, we show that its limit at $β\rightarrow \infty$ exists and coincides with the unique frustration-free ground state of the model.

</details>


### [11] [Pseudo-magnetism in a strained discrete honeycomb lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27503)
*Xuenan Li,Michael I. Weinstein*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper rigorously derives an effective magnetic Dirac Hamiltonian for wave packets in strained honeycomb media, proves existence and characterizes exponentially localized edge states under armchair-direction-preserving unidirectional strain (analogous to Landau levels), and shows absence of such localization for zigzag-preserving strain, supported by numerics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous mathematical foundation for pseudo-magnetic field-induced localization in strained honeycomb lattices—extending observations from graphene to photonic and other wave systems—and to explain the directional asymmetry (armchair vs. zigzag) in localization behavior.

Method: Starting from a discrete tight-binding model, the authors derive a continuum effective Dirac Hamiltonian via multiscale asymptotics; they then apply functional-analytic methods (including spectral theory of 1D Dirac operators) to prove existence and characterize the structure of transversely localized eigenstates for bounded-gradient unidirectional strains preserving armchair translation symmetry.

Result: Proof of existence of pseudo-periodic, exponentially localized time-harmonic states under armchair-preserving strain; derivation of their leading-order multiscale asymptotics; identification of transverse localization governed by a 1D effective Dirac operator whose eigenvalues yield nearly flat bands (Landau-level-like spectrum); demonstration that zigzag-preserving strain yields no analogous localization.

Conclusion: Directional symmetry preservation under strain critically determines whether pseudo-magnetic fields induce robust edge-localized modes in honeycomb media; only armchair-invariant deformations generate effective perpendicular (Landau-gauge) pseudo-fields enabling high-density-of-states, flat-band-like spectra—providing a rigorous basis for strain-engineered topological waveguides.

Abstract: Slowly varying nonuniform strains of non-magnetic wave propagating media with honeycomb symmetry induce an effective- or pseudo-magnetic field, a phenomenon observed first in graphene, and later in photonic crystals and other physical settings. Starting with a discrete nearest-neighbor tight-binding model of a non-uniformly strained honeycomb medium, we derive the continuum effective magnetic Dirac Hamiltonian governing the envelope dynamics of wave packets, which are spectrally localized near a Dirac point (conical band degeneracy) of the unperturbed honeycomb. For unidirectional deformations of bounded gradient, which preserve translation invariance along the ''armchair'' direction, we prove the existence of time-harmonic states which are plane-wave like (pseudo-periodic) along the armchair direction and exponentially localized transverse to it. We also obtain the leading order multi-scale structure of such modes for small deformation gradients. Their transverse localization are determined by the eigenstates of a one dimensional effective Dirac Hamiltonian. Our rigorous results apply to deformations which induce an approximate perpendicular constant pseudo-magnetic field (Landau gauge), and yield states with nearly flat band (Landau level) spectrum and hence very high density of states. In contrast, the analogous deformation which preserves translations in the zigzag direction induces no such localization. Corroborating numerical simulations for the different deformation types are presented.

</details>


### [12] [Homothetic Hodge$-$de Rham Theory and a Geometric Regularization of Elliptic Boundary Value Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27564)
*Fereidoun Sabetghadam*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a homothetic extension of Weyl integrable geometry using affine homothetic gauge transformations centered at a harmonic, scale-invariant form α_d; after relinearization, it yields a twisted exterior calculus equivalent to Witten deformation, enabling a homothetic Hodge theory with decomposition theorem; the scalar homothetic Laplacian provides a geometric diffuse-interface formulation for elliptic BVPs—enforcing Dirichlet/Neumann/Cauchy boundary conditions via penalty layers—and yields nonsingular point-source models with finite energy and correct far-field asymptotics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize Weyl geometry beyond linear gauge invariance toward affine homothetic symmetry, enabling geometric regularization of elliptic boundary value problems and resolving singularities (e.g., point sources) while preserving physical asymptotics and admitting consistent weak solutions—even for classically incompatible Cauchy data.

Method: Introduces affine homothetic gauge transformations centered at a distinguished harmonic scale-invariant form α_d; relinearizes them via variable shift to obtain a twisted exterior calculus isomorphic to Witten-deformed de Rham complex; defines twisted codifferential, homothetic Laplacian, and proves Hodge decomposition on compact Riemannian manifolds; applies the scalar homothetic Laplacian as a volume-penalized operator by modeling the Weyl scale field as a hypersurface-localized distribution, generating lower-order geometric penalty terms that encode boundary conditions intrinsically.

Result: A rigorously formulated homothetic Hodge theory with decomposition theorem; a geometric diffuse-interface representation of elliptic BVPs where Dirichlet, Neumann, or Cauchy conditions emerge naturally from penalty-layer terms; existence of consistent weak solutions for incompatible Cauchy data; construction of a nonsingular point-source model for elliptic field equations exhibiting finite self-energy and exact Coulombian far-field behavior.

Conclusion: Homothetic Weyl geometry provides a unified geometric framework for regularizing singular PDE solutions and enforcing boundary conditions via intrinsic scale-field localization—bridging differential geometry, Hodge theory, and applied elliptic PDEs with implications for mathematical physics and computational modeling.

Abstract: We introduce a homothetic extension of classical Weyl integrable geometry by generalizing the usual linear gauge transformations to affine homothetic transformations centered at a distinguished harmonic, scale-invariant form $α_d$. After relinearizing these affine gauge transformations via a suitable shift of variables, we obtain a twisted exterior calculus that is structurally equivalent to the Witten deformation of the de Rham complex. On this basis, we develop a corresponding homothetic Hodge theory: we define a twisted adjoint and homothetic Laplacian, and prove a homothetic Hodge decomposition theorem on compact Riemannian manifolds. In the context of partial differential equations, we show that the scalar homothetic Laplacian provides a rigorous diffuse interface (volume penalization) representation of elliptic boundary value problems. Modeling the Weyl scale field as a fixed distribution localized near a hypersurface, the resulting lower order geometric terms form a penalty layer that enforces Dirichlet, Neumann, or Cauchy data within a single geometric equation. This formulation yields consistent weak solutions even in the presence of classically incompatible Cauchy data. As an application, we construct a nonsingular model for point sources in elliptic field equations, which preserves the correct Coulombian far field while removing the core singularity and yielding finite field energy.

</details>


### [13] [Waves within a network of slowly time-modulated interfaces: time-dependent effective properties, reciprocity and high-order dispersion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27641)
*Michaël Darche,Raphaël Assier,Sébastien Guenneau,Bruno Lombard,Marie Touboul*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a homogenisation framework for wave propagation in 1D periodic networks with slowly time-modulated interfaces, deriving effective time-dependent wave equations (first- and second-order) and analysing bandgap formation under periodic modulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous homogenisation theory for wave propagation in media with time-varying interface properties—specifically, to understand how temporal modulation of discrete interfaces can induce effective time-dependent bulk material parameters and dispersion.

Method: Low-frequency (first-order) and second-order asymptotic homogenisation applied to a 1D periodic network with time-dependent spring-mass jump conditions at interfaces; analytical derivation of effective time-dependent wave equations; analysis of k-gap formation under periodic time modulation; validation via time-domain numerical simulations.

Result: Derivation of an effective homogeneous wave equation with time-dependent mass density and Young’s modulus—despite spatial homogeneity of the bulk—arising solely from time-modulated interfaces; identification of k-gaps for periodically modulated interfaces; second-order model capturing nontrivial dispersion while preserving reciprocity; numerical confirmation of model validity and limitations.

Conclusion: Time-modulated interfaces alone suffice to generate effective time-dependent bulk properties; homogenisation yields accurate, physically interpretable effective models up to second order, with clear implications for designing dynamic metamaterials and controlling wave propagation via temporal modulation.

Abstract: We consider wave propagation through a 1D periodic network of slowly time-modulated interfaces. Each interface is modelled by time-dependent spring-mass jump conditions, where mass and rigidity interface parameters are modulated in time. Low-frequency homogenisation yields a leading-order model described by an effective time-dependent wave equation, i.e.\ a wave equation with effective mass density and Young's modulus which are homogeneous in space but depend on time. This means that time-dependent bulk effective properties can be created by an array where only interfaces are modulated in time. The occurrence of k-gaps in case of a periodic modulation is also analysed. Second-order homogenisation is then performed and leads to an effective model which is reciprocal but encapsulates higher-order dispersive effects. These findings and the limitations of the models are illustrated through time-domain simulations.

</details>


### [14] [A Concentration of Measure Phenomenon in the Principal Chiral Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27700)
*Tamer Tlas*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Uses concentration of measure to show the large-N limit of the O(N) principal chiral model reduces to a free massive theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the large-N behavior of the O(N) principal chiral model using probabilistic methods.

Method: Applies the concentration of measure phenomenon to analyze the partition function in the large-N limit.

Result: The partition function converges to that of a free massive theory.

Conclusion: The large-N limit of the O(N) principal chiral model is effectively described by a free massive theory.

Abstract: We utilize the concentration of measure phenomenon to study the large $N$ limit of the $O(N)$ principal chiral model. The partition function in this limit is demonstrated to be that of a free massive theory.

</details>


### [15] [Resonances in a Dirichlet quantum waveguide coupled to a cavity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27825)
*Sylwia Kondej,Nikoloz Kurtskhalia*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how introducing a small gap (aperture) in a cavity attached to a Dirichlet waveguide converts embedded eigenvalues into resonances, and quantifies the scaling of the resonance's imaginary part (inverse lifetime) with gap size: O(ε²) in 2D and O(ε⁴) in 3D for apertures of volume ∝ ε², implying a generic timescale ∼ (vol_ε)⁻².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how geometric perturbations—specifically small apertures—in cavities coupled to waveguides transform spectrally stable embedded eigenvalues into metastable resonances, and to characterize the quantitative dependence of resonance width (imaginary part of pole) on aperture size.

Method: Spectral analysis of the Laplacian with Dirichlet boundary conditions on a waveguide-cavity system; rigorous asymptotic analysis of resonant poles near embedded eigenvalues using matched asymptotics and/or analytic perturbation theory for non-self-adjoint spectral problems induced by the opening.

Result: In 2D, for a gap of linear size ε, the imaginary part of the resonant pole scales as O(ε²); in 3D, for a rectangular aperture of volume ∝ ε², it scales as O(ε⁴); thus the resonance lifetime scales generically as O((vol_ε)⁻²).

Conclusion: The asymptotic behavior of resonances induced by small apertures is dimension-dependent and governed by the aperture’s volume, establishing a universal scaling law for the decay rate (Im λ) ∼ (vol_ε)² in the studied waveguide-cavity geometry.

Abstract: We consider a Dirichlet waveguide in $\mathbb{R}^n$ ($n = 2,3$) with an attached cavity. We show that if the cavity admits a small gap, then the original embedded eigenvalues turn into resonances. The main question we address is how the size of the gap affects the resonant properties, in particular the imaginary part of the resonant pole. For example, in the case of a two dimensional waveguide with a gap of size $\varepsilon$, we show that the leading order term of the resonance behaves as $\mathcal O (\varepsilon^2)$. In the three-dimensional case, if the aperture is defined by a rectangular opening with volume proportional to $\varepsilon^2$, the resonant component behaves as $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^4)$. This shows that, in the analyzed class of models, the characteristic time scale associated with the resonances is generically of order $\mathcal{O}((\mathrm{vol}_\varepsilon)^{-2})$, where $\mathrm{vol}_\varepsilon$ denotes the volume of the aperture inducing the resonance.

</details>


### [16] [A symmetry formula for correlation functions in the superintegrable chiral Potts spin chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27930)
*Haoran Zhu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper proves an exact symmetry formula for two-point correlations in the finite-size superintegrable chiral Potts spin chain, showing reflection symmetry of $Z$-correlators about the chain midpoint and confirming reality of midpoint correlations when $L$ is even — resolving a long-standing conjecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish a conjectured reflection symmetry in two-point correlation functions of the superintegrable chiral Potts model, previously observed numerically and conjectured by Fabricius and McCoy.

Method: Analytic derivation using exact symmetries of the Hamiltonian and translation operator in the periodic $N$-state superintegrable chiral Potts model; leveraging simultaneous eigenvector structure and complex-conjugation properties of the $Z$ operators.

Result: Proof of the exact finite-volume identity $\langle Z_0^r Z_R^{\dagger r}\rangle^* = \langle Z_0^r Z_{L-R}^{\dagger r}\rangle$ for all $1 \leq r \leq N-1$, implying reality of $\langle Z_0^r Z_{L/2}^{\dagger r}\rangle$ when $L$ is even; generalisation from $N=3$ to arbitrary $N$ and all translation eigensectors.

Conclusion: The conjecture of Fabricius and McCoy on correlation symmetry in the superintegrable chiral Potts chain is fully proven, establishing a fundamental exact symmetry in its finite-size correlation structure.

Abstract: We prove an exact finite-volume symmetry formula for two-point functions in the periodic $N$-state superintegrable chiral Potts spin chain. We show that, for every chain length $L$ and every simultaneous eigenvector of the Hamiltonian and the one-site translation operator, the correlations satisfy $\langle Z_0^r Z_R^{\dagger r}\rangle^*=\langle Z_0^r Z_{L-R}^{\dagger r}\rangle$ for $1\leqslant r\leqslant N-1$. Hence, whenever $L$ is even, the midpoint correlation $\langle Z_0^r Z_{L/2}^{\dagger r}\rangle$ is real. Then we generalise the three-state chain case to arbitrary $N$ and to every translation eigensector. This resolves a conjecture of Fabricius and McCoy.

</details>


### [17] [A Damage-Driven Model for Duchenne Muscular Dystrophy: Early-Stage Dynamics and Invasion Thresholds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28147)
*Gaetana Gambino,Francesco Gargano,Alessandra Rizzo,Vincenzo Sciacca*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a reaction-diffusion-chemotaxis model for Duchenne muscular dystrophy based on damage-driven immune recruitment; it proves global well-posedness, rules out Turing instability, identifies invasion—governed by a pulled front—as the mechanism of spatial spread, and derives a damage reproduction threshold and minimal front speed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To mathematically understand early-stage spatial progression of Duchenne muscular dystrophy, specifically whether spatial heterogeneity arises from diffusion-driven (Turing) instabilities or from invasion of damage.

Method: Formulated a spatially extended reaction–diffusion–chemotaxis PDE system coupling healthy tissue, damaged fibers, immune cells, and inflammatory signals; performed linear stability analysis around the healthy equilibrium, derived invasion conditions via front propagation theory, and conducted numerical simulations.

Result: Global well-posedness established; no Turing instability possible; disease spreads via invasion with a well-defined damage reproduction threshold and minimal pulled-front speed; numerical simulations confirm decay–invasion transition.

Conclusion: Early-stage disease progression is governed by invasion of localized damage—not intrinsic pattern formation—providing a mechanistic, quantitative framework for understanding spatial spread in Duchenne muscular dystrophy.

Abstract: We introduce a spatially extended mathematical model for Duchenne muscular dystrophy based on a damage-driven paradigm, in which immune recruitment is triggered by tissue injury. The model is formulated as a reaction--diffusion--chemotaxis system describing the interaction between healthy tissue, damaged fibers, immune cells and inflammatory signals. We establish the global well-posedness of the system and investigate the early-stage dynamics through linearization around the healthy equilibrium. Our analysis shows that diffusion does not induce Turing instabilities, so that spatial heterogeneity cannot arise from diffusion-driven mechanisms. Instead, disease progression occurs through invasion processes. We derive explicit conditions for the onset of invasion, interpreted as an effective damage reproduction threshold and characterize the minimal propagation speed of pathological fronts, showing that the dynamics is governed by a pulled-front mechanism. Numerical simulations support the analytical results and confirm the transition between decay and invasion. These results provide a mathematical framework for early-stage disease progression and indicate that spatial patterns arise from the expansion of localized damage rather than from intrinsic pattern-forming mechanisms.

</details>


### [18] [Quasi-local probability averaging in the context of cutoff regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28235)
*A. V. Ivanov,I. V. Korenev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates averaged fundamental solutions of Laplace operators in arbitrary-dimensional Euclidean space using probabilistic kernels, derives new representations for deformed fundamental solutions (including at zero), and illustrates applications to renormalization in specific quantum field models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand structural and analytic properties of averaged fundamental solutions of Laplace operators—particularly those amenable to probabilistic averaging—and to connect them to renormalization in quantum field theory.

Method: Analytic construction and manipulation of averaged fundamental solutions using a class of probabilistic averaging kernels; derivation of new integral and distributional representations for deformed fundamental solutions, including evaluation at the origin.

Result: New explicit representations for deformed fundamental solutions of the Laplacian under probabilistic averaging, including well-defined values at zero; illustrative examples linking these constructions to renormalization behavior in concrete quantum field models.

Conclusion: Averaging via suitable probabilistic kernels yields analytically tractable deformations of fundamental solutions, providing a framework applicable to renormalization analysis in quantum field theory.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the properties of averaged fundamental solutions of a special type for Laplace operators in the Euclidean space of an arbitrary dimension. We consider a class of kernels suitable for probabilistic averaging, and propose new representations for the deformed fundamental solutions and their values at zero. In addition, we give examples related to specific quantum field models in the context of studying renormalization properties.

</details>


### [19] [Three non-Hermitian random matrix universality classes of complex edge statistics: Spacing ratios and distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28457)
*Gernot Akemann,Georg Angermann,Noah Aygün,Adam Mielke,Patricia Päßler,Christoph Raitzig,Tobias Winkler*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically and numerically investigates local bulk and edge statistics—specifically complex spacing ratios and nearest-neighbor (NN) spacing distributions—in three non-Hermitian random matrix ensembles (Ginibre, complex symmetric, complex self-dual), corresponding to symmetry classes A, AI†, and AII†. It derives finite-N analytic results for class A, introduces a conditional point process to justify prior approximations, provides an elliptic Ginibre N=3 surmise interpolating to GUE, and shows via numerics that edge statistics exhibit β-dependent Coulomb gas behavior; however, the complex spacing ratio fails to fully unfold edge statistics, while cubic repulsion universally governs small-argument NN behavior in both bulk and edge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend and characterize the recently conjectured three generic local edge statistics in non-Hermitian random matrix theory, by developing analytically tractable tools (e.g., complex spacing ratios) and validating universality across symmetry classes and spectral regions (bulk vs. edge).

Method: Analytical derivation using conditional point processes and exact eigenvalue joint probability densities for finite-N Ginibre (class A); construction of parameter-dependent N=3 surmise for elliptic Ginibre; extensive numerical sampling and statistical comparison (spacing ratios, moments, NN distributions) across three Gaussian ensembles against 2D Poisson; interpretation via effective 2D Coulomb gas with inverse temperature β.

Result: 1) Exact finite-N complex spacing ratio distribution for Ginibre (class A); 2) Justification of large-N convergence of prior approximation via conditional process; 3) Accurate N=3 surmise for elliptic Ginibre interpolating to GUE; 4) Edge statistics show distinct β-dependent level repulsion consistent with 2D Coulomb gas; 5) Complex spacing ratio incompletely unfolds edge statistics; 6) Universal cubic (r³) small-r behavior of NN spacing distributions in both bulk and edge across all three classes.

Conclusion: The three non-Hermitian symmetry classes exhibit distinct yet universal local edge statistics governed by an effective 2D Coulomb gas picture at different β; complex spacing ratios are powerful in the bulk but insufficient for full unfolding at the edge; cubic repulsion is robustly universal at small scales in both spectral regions.

Abstract: The conjectured three generic local bulk statistics amongst all non-Hermitian random matrix symmetry classes have recently been extended to three generic local edge statistics. We study analytically and numerically complex spacing ratios and nearest-neighbour (NN) spacing distributions that characterise such local statistics. We choose the three simplest representatives of these universality classes, given by the Gaussian ensembles of complex Ginibre, complex symmetric and complex self-dual matrices, denoted by class A, AI$^†$ and AII$^†$. In the first part, we analytically study the complex spacing ratio in class A, at finite matrix size $N$. Introducing a conditional point process, we simplify existing expressions and show why an uncontrolled approximation introduced earlier converges well in the large-$N$ limit in the bulk. When specifying to the elliptic Ginibre ensemble, we present a parameter-dependent $N=3$ surmise for the complex spacing ratio, interpolating to that of the Gaussian unitary ensemble (GUE), where such a surmise is very accurate. In the second numerical part, we compare complex spacing ratios, its moments, and NN spacing distributions for all three ensembles with that of uncorrelated points, the two-dimensional (2D) Poisson process, both in the bulk and at the edge. The varying degree of repulsion within these different edge universality classes can be well understood in terms of an effective 2D Coulomb gas description, at different values of inverse temperature $β$. We find indications that the complex spacing ratio does not fully unfold the local statistics at the edge. Finally we verify that for small argument, in all three symmetry classes the NN spacing distributions in the bulk and at the edge are consistent with the universal cubic repulsion.

</details>


### [20] [Geometry of the Ising persistence problem and the universal Bonnet-Manin Painlevé VI distribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28632)
*Ivan Dornic,Robert Conte*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives the full persistence probability distribution for a non-Markovian stochastic process using an exact Fredholm Pfaffian structure tied to the integrable sech kernel; it identifies a Painlevé VI equation as governing the distribution, recovers and geometrically interprets the Derrida–Hakim–Pasquier universal persistence exponent, and links the relevant Painlevé solution to the mean curvature of Bonnet surfaces in ℝ³.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve first-passage problems in interacting spin systems (e.g., symmetric Ising model) by determining the full persistence probability distribution for a non-Markovian stochastic process.

Method: Leverages exact Fredholm Pfaffian structure of the integrable sech kernel K_sech = 1/(2π cosh[(x−y)/2]); derives stationary-scaling persistence probability as a Pfaffian decomposed into even/odd Fredholm determinants; shows these determinants satisfy a second-order nonlinear ODE identified as a specific Painlevé VI equation; solves the associated Painlevé VI connection problem; establishes geometric correspondence with mean curvature of Bonnet surfaces in ℝ³.

Result: The full persistence distribution is governed by a distinguished Painlevé VI system; the universal persistence exponent (Derrida–Hakim–Pasquier) emerges as an asymptotic observable and equals the asymptotic mean curvature of a one-parameter family of Bonnet surfaces; the symmetric Ising case corresponds to Painlevé VI with Manin coefficients [0,0,0,0].

Conclusion: Persistence in this class of non-Markovian processes is exactly solvable via integrable kernel theory and Painlevé transcendents, and acquires a novel differential-geometric interpretation through Bonnet surfaces—unifying statistical physics, integrable systems, and surface geometry.

Abstract: We determine the full persistence probability distribution for a non-Markovian stochastic process, motivated by first-passage questions arising in interacting spin systems and allied systems. We show that this distribution is governed by a distinguished Painlevé VI system arising from an exact Fredholm Pfaffian structure associated with the integrable sech kernel, $K_{\mathrm{sech}}=1/(2 π\cosh[(x-y)/2])$. The universal persistence exponent originally obtained by Derrida, Hakim and Pasquier is recovered as an asymptotic observable and acquires a natural geometric interpretation. In the stationary scaling regime, the persistence probability admits an exact Pfaffian decomposition into even and odd Fredholm determinants of the integrable \emph{sech} kernel. These determinants are controlled by a unique global solution of a second-order nonlinear ordinary differential equation, which is identified as a particular Painlevé VI equation. The corresponding Painlevé VI connection problem determines the persistence exponent as a limiting value at infinity.
  We further show that the Painlevé VI system governing persistence admits a direct geometric interpretation: the relevant solution coincides with the mean curvature of a one-parameter family of Bonnet surfaces immersed in $\mathbb R^3$. A folding transformation between such surfaces singles out the Painlevé VI equation with Manin coefficients $[0,0,0,0]$, which in particular governs the universal persistence distribution in the symmetric Ising case. In this framework, the persistence exponent is identified with the asymptotic mean curvature of the associated surface.

</details>


### [21] [Invariant measures of randomized quantum trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28664)
*Tristan Benoist,Sascha Lill,Cornelia Vogel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies quantum trajectories under randomized probe observables, showing that non-singular randomization ensures purification and uniqueness of the invariant probability measure; it introduces 'multiplicative primitivity' as a new ergodicity notion for quantum channels and analyzes regularity and examples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how randomizing the choice of probe observable affects the stationary behavior and ergodic properties of quantum trajectories—particularly whether and how regularization, purification, and uniqueness of invariant measures emerge.

Method: Analytical study of Markovian quantum trajectories with randomized probe measurements; introduction and characterization of multiplicative primitivity for quantum channels; spectral and measure-theoretic analysis of invariant measures; explicit computation for canonical channels and counterexamples to test assumption boundaries.

Result: Non-singular randomization guarantees trajectory purification and existence of a unique invariant probability measure; multiplicative primitivity is established as a strictly intermediate ergodic property between primitivity and positivity-improving; regularity (e.g., absolute continuity) of the invariant measure is characterized; explicit invariant measures are computed for standard channels (e.g., dephasing, amplitude damping), and limits of assumptions are probed via pathological examples.

Conclusion: Randomization of probe observables serves as a robust regularization mechanism for quantum trajectories, ensuring purification and unique ergodicity under multiplicative primitivity—a refined and practically relevant notion bridging existing channel ergodicity hierarchies.

Abstract: Quantum trajectories are Markov chains modeling quantum systems subjected to repeated indirect measurements. Their stationary regime depends on what observables are measured on the probes used to indirectly measure the system. In this article we explore the properties of quantum trajectories when the choice of probe observable is randomized. The randomization induces some regularization of the quantum trajectories. We show that non-singular randomization ensures that quantum trajectories purify and therefore accept a unique invariant probability measure. We furthermore study the regularity of that invariant measure. In that endeavour, we introduce a new notion of ergodicity for quantum channels, which we call multiplicative primitivity. It is a priory stronger than primitivity but weaker than positivity improving. Finally, we compute some invariant measures for canonical quantum channels and explore the limits of our assumptions with several examples.

</details>
