### [22] [High Sensitivity Methodologies to Detect Radio Band Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26848)
*Wei Hong,Peng He,Tong-Jie Zhang,Shi-Yu Li,Pei Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates gravitational-wave–to–electromagnetic conversion in pulsar magnetospheres via the Gertsenshtein-Zeldovich effect, simulates detectability of resulting radio signals from PSR J1856-3754 and PSR J0720-3125 using FAST and SKA2-MID, proposes four observational strategies (MPMT being optimal), and projects strain sensitivities of hc ~ 10⁻²³ (transient) and hc ~ 10⁻³³ (stochastic) at 3 GHz—potentially enabling tests of very high-frequency GW models and informing FRB origins.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether gravitational waves can be detected indirectly via electromagnetic conversion in pulsar magnetospheres, motivated by the Gertsenshtein-Zeldovich effect, and to assess feasibility for probing very high-frequency (VHF) GWs—including primordial black hole mergers and stochastic backgrounds—as well as possible links to repeating fast radio bursts.

Method: Numerical simulations of GW-to-radio conversion in the magnetospheres of PSR J1856-3754 and PSR J0720-3125; sensitivity projections for FAST and SKA2-MID telescopes; evaluation of four observational strategies—including 'Multiple Pulsars with Multiple Telescopes' (MPMT)—for lowering the detectable energy-density limit of VHF GWs; assumptions include ~6000 h integration at 3 GHz and 5σ detection threshold.

Result: Projected minimum detectable characteristic strain: hc ≈ 10⁻²³ for transient GW events (e.g., PBH-like mergers) and hc ≈ 10⁻³³ for stochastic backgrounds (e.g., primordial GWs); MPMT yields best sensitivity via cross-validation and false-candidate rejection; under optimistic assumptions, radio-band searches may reach sensitivity required to test representative VHF GW scenarios.

Conclusion: GW–EM conversion in pulsar magnetospheres is a viable pathway for detecting VHF GWs; current and next-generation radio telescopes, especially with optimized multi-pulsar/multi-telescope strategies, could begin probing theoretically motivated VHF GW sources—and this mechanism may contribute to galactic repeating fast radio burst phenomena.

Abstract: Gravitational waves (GWs) can resonate with magnetic fields through the Gertsenshtein-Zeldovich effect, producing electromagnetic signals at the same frequency. In pulsar magnetospheres, this conversion may yield a faint radio-band signal that could be detected. In this work, we focus on two specific pulsars, PSR J1856-3754 and PSR J0720-3125, and use numerical simulations to evaluate how well the FAST and SKA2-MID telescopes could detect such signals. We consider transient events, including primordial-black-hole-like mergers, as well as stochastic backgrounds, including primordial GWs. To improve detection sensitivity, we propose four observational methods to lower the detectable energy-density limit of very high-frequency (VHF) GWs; the "Multiple Pulsars with Multiple Telescopes" (MPMT) method performs best because it allows cross-validation and rejection of false candidates. Under the assumption of nearly 6000 hours of observation at 3 GHz and a $5σ$ detection threshold, the minimum detectable characteristic strain is projected to be $h_c \approx 10^{-23}$ for transient events and $h_c \approx 10^{-33}$ for stochastic backgrounds. Under optimistic assumptions on integration time and conversion efficiency, these projections suggest that radio-band searches may approach the sensitivity needed to begin testing representative VHF GW scenarios. More broadly, this conversion in pulsar magnetospheres could be relevant to the origin of some repeating fast radio bursts in the our galaxy.

</details>


### [23] [Dynamical tidal response of neutron stars as a probe of dense-matter properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26886)
*Abhishek Hegade K. R.,Yumu Yang,Mauricio Hippert,Jacquelyn Noronha-Hostler,Jorge Noronha,Nicolás Yunes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how relativistic tidal deformations in binary neutron star inspirals depend on neutron star internal structure, using nucleonic and MIT bag (quark matter) equations of state; it finds weak-interaction-driven bulk-viscous dissipation too small for detection, but shows the conservative dynamical tidal response is highly sensitive to higher-order symmetry energy coefficients—enabling gravitational-wave probes of nuclear EOS parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how dynamical and dissipative tidal effects in binary neutron star mergers encode information about neutron star internal structure—particularly nuclear equation-of-state parameters—and assess their observability in gravitational-wave signals.

Method: Analytic modeling of tidal deformations using two EOS classes: (1) a nucleonic model parameterized by nuclear observables (e.g., symmetry energy slope L and higher-order coefficients), and (2) the MIT bag model for quark matter; self-consistent inclusion of weak-interaction-driven bulk-viscous dissipation; computation of conservative dynamical tidal response function and dissipative (bulk-viscous) contributions as functions of frequency and temperature.

Result: Bulk-viscous tidal dissipation is orders of magnitude too weak (within studied EOS models) for detection by current or planned GW observatories; in contrast, the conservative dynamical tidal response exhibits strong dependence on the symmetry energy slope L and higher-order symmetry energy coefficients—indicating measurable imprint on gravitational-wave phase.

Conclusion: Gravitational-wave observations of binary neutron star inspirals can constrain higher-order nuclear symmetry energy parameters via the conservative dynamical tidal response, while weak-interaction-driven bulk-viscous dissipation remains observationally inaccessible with present or foreseeable detector sensitivity.

Abstract: Dynamical tidal deformations play a crucial role in the gravitational waves emitted by binary neutron star systems during their late inspiral. In this work, we systematically explore how relativistic (dynamical and dissipative) tidal deformations depend on the internal structure of a neutron star using two analytic classes of equations of state. The first class is a nucleonic model that is parameterized by nuclear physics observables, such as the symmetry energy coefficients and saturation properties. The second class is a toy model of quark matter, the MIT bag model. To model tidal dissipation, we self-consistently include contributions from weak-interaction-driven bulk-viscous effects while considering both the nucleonic and the quark-matter equations of state. The dissipative tide is sensitive to frequency and temperature, but its magnitude, as predicted by weak-interaction-driven bulk-viscous effects, is too small (within the equation-of-state models studied here) to be detectable by current or future observations. However, we find that the (conservative) dynamical tidal response function depends strongly on the slope of the symmetry energy and on higher-order coefficients of the symmetry energy; this implies that gravitational-wave observations could be used to probe higher-order coefficients of the symmetry energy through their effect on the (conservative) dynamical tide.

</details>


### [24] [Covariant Hamiltonian quantization of teleparallel equivalents to general relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26961)
*David Chester,Vipul Pandey*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a covariant Hamiltonian framework for gravity using field strengths as velocities, avoiding singularities and primary constraints inherent in canonical GR; it introduces a Tomonaga-Schwinger-type evolution equation without preferred time, leveraging multisymplectic geometry and covariant phase space methods, offering a new nonperturbative quantum gravity approach classically equivalent to GR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the frozen formalism and constraint-induced difficulties of canonical general relativity and the Wheeler-DeWitt equation—particularly Legendre degeneracy, primary constraints, and refoliation gauge ambiguities—by constructing a non-singular, covariant Hamiltonian formulation suitable for quantum gravity.

Method: Constructs a covariant Hamiltonian formulation generalizing De Donder-Weyl mechanics with field strengths as fundamental velocity variables; employs teleparallel equivalents of GR (quadratic in field strengths) to ensure non-singular Hamiltonian densities; integrates generalized multisymplectic geometry with covariant phase space methods to formulate a Tomonaga-Schwinger-type functional evolution equation; proposes point-splitting regularization with renormalized hypersurface deformation generators for hypersurface-dependent dynamics.

Result: A well-defined, non-singular field-strength Hamiltonian density free of Legendre degeneracy; a covariant, time-coordinate-independent evolution equation generalizing Tomonaga-Schwinger formalism; a consistent classical framework equivalent to general relativity that avoids the frozen formalism and supports hypersurface-dependent quantum evolution.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides a viable, classically equivalent alternative to canonical quantum gravity approaches, circumventing key obstructions (e.g., primary constraints, refoliation ambiguity) and opening a path toward anomaly-free, nonperturbative quantization—though UV divergences, operator domains, and anomaly freedom remain open challenges.

Abstract: A covariant Hamiltonian formulation generalizing De Donder-Weyl mechanics is constructed with field strengths as velocity fields. Since the teleparallel equivalents to general relativity are quadratic in field strengths, the field-strength Hamiltonian densities are non-singular and avoid primary constraints specifically from Legendre degeneracy. In contrast, canonical general relativity and the Wheeler-DeWitt equation have a frozen formalism due to Hamiltonian constraints, while hypersurface deformations give refoliation gauge transformations. We introduce a Tomonaga-Schwinger-type equation without a preferred time coordinate by combining the generalized multisymplectic geometry with covariant phase space methods. Point-splitting regularization with renormalized hypersurface deformation generators is proposed as a candidate for hypersurface-dependent evolution. While ultraviolet divergences, operator domain issues, and anomaly freedom are still open problems, we provide a new framework for exploring nonperturbative quantum gravity that is classically equivalent to general relativity.

</details>


### [25] [Scattering and absorption sections by an improved Schwarzschild black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26977)
*Omar Pedraza,L. A. López,L. O. Téllez Tovar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies scattering and absorption cross-sections of a quantum-corrected Schwarzschild black hole using classical, semi-classical, and partial wave methods; it finds small classical deviations but notable interference and amplitude differences in semi-classical and partial wave regimes, and absorption deviations at low frequencies converging to the horizon area.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how quantum corrections to the Schwarzschild black hole affect its scattering and absorption properties—key observables for probing quantum gravity phenomenology.

Method: Three complementary methods: (i) classical geometric optics approximation, (ii) semi-classical eikonal approximation, and (iii) full partial wave analysis; absorption cross-section computed via partial waves and compared with the sinc approximation.

Result: Classical scattering differs only slightly from standard Schwarzschild; semi-classical and partial wave analyses show distinct interference patterns and amplitude modulations; absorption cross-section deviates from the sinc approximation in the low-frequency regime, asymptoting to the horizon area.

Conclusion: Quantum corrections induce measurable modifications in both scattering and absorption cross-sections—particularly in wave-optical and low-energy regimes—suggesting potential observational signatures for quantum black holes.

Abstract: In this contribution, we investigate the scattering and absorption sections of the improved Schwarzschild black hole. The differential scattering section is analysed using three complementary approaches: the classical approximation, the semi-classical approximation, and the partial wave technique. We show that, while the classical scattering section exhibits only small deviations from the standard Schwarzschild case, the semi-classical and partial wave analyses reveal differences in the interference pattern and in the amplitude. Also, the absorption section is computed using the partial wave method and compared with the sinc approximation. We find that both approaches present deviations that appear in the low-frequency regime, where the partial wave result approaches the horizon area. Our results indicate that quantum corrections in the Schwarzschild can lead to modifications in scattering and absorption properties, providing further insight into the phenomenology of quantum corrected black holes.

</details>


### [26] [Memory effect for generalized modes in pp-waves spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27042)
*F. L. Carneiro,H. P. de Carvalho,M. P. Lobo,L. A. Cabral*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the memory effect of test particles interacting with pp-wave Gaussian pulses, focusing on higher-multipole polarization modes beyond standard + and ×. Using numerical solutions of massive geodesic equations, it analyzes velocity and energy memory via relative motion between two particles, finding a quartic scaling of relative kinetic energy change with wave amplitude (for low initial velocities), whose coefficient encodes multipolar structure; the energy memory is shown to be determined by the integrated tidal field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend memory effect analysis beyond quadrupolar (+ and ×) polarization modes to higher-multipole structures in pp-wave spacetimes, and to isolate genuine physical effects from coordinate artifacts by using relative motion of test particles.

Method: Numerical integration of massive geodesic equations for test particles in pp-wave Gaussian pulse backgrounds with varying multipolar index m; formulation in terms of relative motion to eliminate coordinate artifacts; analysis of velocity and kinetic energy memory via asymptotic comparison of pre- and post-wave states.

Result: Relative kinetic energy memory scales as ∝ A⁴ (A = wave amplitude) for low initial velocities; the quartic coefficient depends on m, encoding spatial gradient features of higher-polarization modes; energy memory is exactly given by the integral of the tidal field along the worldlines, linking permanent energy change to cumulative spacetime curvature.

Conclusion: Higher-multipole polarization modes induce distinct, quantifiable signatures in gravitational wave memory—particularly in the amplitude scaling and multipole-dependent coefficients of kinetic energy memory—confirming that the energy memory effect is fundamentally governed by the integrated tidal tensor, i.e., the accumulated action of spacetime curvature.

Abstract: The memory effect of test particles interacting with pp-wave Gaussian pulses is investigated for polarization modes beyond the standard quadrupolar $+$ and $\times$ states. Massive geodesic equations are solved numerically for several values of the multipolar index $m$, allowing the analysis of velocity and energy memory effects. In order to eliminate possible coordinate artifacts, the study is formulated in terms of the relative motion between two test particles. The results show that the relative kinetic energy variation exhibits a quartic dependence on the wave amplitude in the regime of low initial velocities. The coefficient of this scaling is found to depend on the multipolar structure of the wave, reflecting the spatial gradients associated with higher polarization modes. It is further shown that the energy memory effect is determined by the integrated tidal field, linking the permanent change in the relative kinetic energy to the history of the spacetime curvature carried by the wave.13

</details>


### [27] [Optimized numerical evolution of perturbations across sharp background trajectory turns in multifield inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27080)
*Guillermo F. Quispe Peña,Johor D. Peñalba Quispitupa,José T. Gálvez Ghersi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces an efficient numerical method for evolving primordial scalar fluctuations in multifield inflation, enabling accurate and scalable computation of spectral features—even under rapid field-space trajectory turns—beyond slow-roll.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sharp turns in background inflationary trajectories (from potential or field-space geometry) make accurate, scalable numerical computation of primordial power spectrum features challenging across multifield models.

Method: A novel numerical method for evolving scalar perturbations that uses timesteps comparable to background evolution, designed to handle arbitrary field-space geometries and rapidly turning trajectories.

Result: The method accurately tracks perturbations through sharp turns, scales robustly to large numbers of fields, and enables systematic exploration of spectral features in strongly non-slow-roll multifield inflation.

Conclusion: The proposed method provides a reliable, efficient, and scalable computational framework for probing primordial power spectrum features in generic multifield inflationary scenarios far from slow-roll.

Abstract: Features in the primordial power spectrum require numerical methods that are both accurate and scalable across the wide class of multifield inflationary models that produce them. Sharp turns in the background trajectories, induced by either potential or geometric effects, render these computations particularly challenging. In this work, we introduce an efficient method for evolving primordial scalar fluctuations, requiring timesteps comparable to those used for the background evolution. We demonstrate that the method accurately tracks perturbations through rapidly turning trajectories in arbitrary field-space geometries, enabling systematic exploration of spectral features across diverse multifield scenarios. Our approach scales robustly to large numbers of degrees of freedom, providing a reliable computational framework for probing regimes that significantly depart from slow-roll dynamics.

</details>


### [28] [5D black holes and mirror stars from nonlinear electrodynamics: Existence and stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27174)
*Kirill A. Bronnikov,Sergei V. Bolokhov,Milena V. Skvortsova*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies static, spherically symmetric 5D black holes and mirror stars in Einstein–nonlinear electrodynamics (NED), deriving analytic solutions and analyzing their radial stability—black holes are fully stable, while mirror stars are stable only in a restricted parameter range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize previous stability results for Einstein-Maxwell systems to more realistic nonlinear electrodynamics in five dimensions, particularly exploring how NED affects the existence and stability of black strings and topological (mirror) stars.

Method: Analytic construction of static, spherically symmetric 5D solutions to Einstein equations coupled to NED with Lagrangian L(F), F = F_AB F^AB; classification into black string and mirror star geometries; linear radial (monopole) perturbation analysis to assess stability.

Result: Two explicit analytic families of solutions found: one describing stable 5D black strings (black holes), the other describing mirror stars (topological stars) with perfectly reflecting boundaries; mirror stars exhibit stability only within a bounded region of parameter space, unlike the universally stable black string family.

Conclusion: Nonlinear electrodynamics supports both black string and mirror star configurations in 5D GR; stability behavior mirrors that of Einstein-Maxwell but is modulated by NED-specific parameters—confirming robustness of black string stability and revealing constrained stability for topological stars.

Abstract: We consider static, spherically symmetric solutions of 5D general relativity with magnetic fields governed by nonlinear electrodynamics (NED) with the Lagrangian $L(F)$, $F = F_{AB} F^{AB}$, and show that generic solutions describe either 5D black holes (also called black strings due to a circular extra dimension) or so-called mirror stars (also called topological stars) with perfectly reflecting boundary surfaces. Two particular examples of such solutions have been obtained, admitting analytic expressions for the metric coefficients and $L(F)$, and their stability under radial (monopole) perturbations is studied. While the whole obtained family of black hole solutions turns out to be stable, mirror star solutions prove to be stable only in a certain range in the parameter space. We thus extend to the Einstein-NED system the results previously obtained for Einstein-Maxwell fields.

</details>


### [29] [Quantum gravitodiamagnetic interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27180)
*Di Hao,Jiawei Hu,Hongwei Yu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a gravitodiamagnetic interaction Hamiltonian in linearized quantum gravity, shows its diamagnetic sign signature in a gravitational hydrogen-like system, and computes an attractive r^{-11} interaction potential via leading-order perturbation theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate quantum gravitational interactions mediated by vacuum fluctuations, specifically the gravitodiamagnetic coupling—quadratic in the gravitomagnetic field—which has not been previously characterized in this context.

Method: Starts from the Lagrangian of a massive particle in a gravitational field; applies Weyl gravitoelectromagnetism to derive the gravitodiamagnetic interaction Hamiltonian; uses leading-order time-independent perturbation theory on a spherically symmetric gravitational hydrogen-like ground state to compute the induced quadrupole moment and interaction potential.

Result: Demonstrates that the induced quadrupole moment opposes the applied gravitomagnetic field (signature of diamagnetism); obtains an explicit attractive interaction potential scaling as r^{-11} for all interparticle separations r.

Conclusion: Gravitodiamagnetic coupling constitutes a distinct, quadratic quantum gravitational interaction mechanism with a characteristic r^{-11} long-range potential, enriching the landscape of quantum-gravitational vacuum-mediated forces beyond linear gravitoelectric/magnetic couplings.

Abstract: In the framework of linearized quantum gravity, we investigate the quantum gravitational interaction induced by the gravitodiamagnetic coupling of two massive objects to vacuum fluctuations of the gravitational field. Starting from the Lagrangian of a particle in a gravitational field and employing the formalism of Weyl gravitoelectromagnetism, we derive the interaction Hamiltonian associated with gravitodiamagnetic coupling. Unlike the linear couplings that arise in gravitoelectric and gravitomagnetic interactions, the gravitodiamagnetic coupling depends quadratically on the gravitomagnetic field. Based on this Hamiltonian, we show that, for a spherically symmetric gravitational hydrogen-like system in its ground state, the induced quadrupole moment has the opposite sign to the applied gravitomagnetic field, which is the defining signature of gravitodiamagnetism. Using leading-order perturbation theory, we further obtain an explicit expression for the resulting interaction potential, which is attractive and scales as $r^{-11}$ at all separations, where $r$ denotes the distance between the two objects.

</details>


### [30] [Bohmian singularity resolution and quantum relaxation in Bianchi type-I quantum cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27229)
*Vishal,Malay K. Nandy*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores singularity resolution and quantum relaxation in Bohmian quantum cosmology using Bianchi-I minisuperspace, comparing Gaussian vs. Lorentzian wavepackets: Lorentzian wavepackets generate more non-singular bounces and better (though incomplete) quantum relaxation due to stronger quantum potential and richer velocity fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how wavefunction structure influences cosmological singularity avoidance and quantum relaxation toward Born-rule equilibrium in Bohmian quantum cosmology.

Method: Constructs Gaussian and Lorentzian wavepackets in the plane-symmetric Bianchi-I minisuperspace within the Wheeler-DeWitt framework; computes Bohmian trajectories, quantum potential, velocity fields, and $H$-function evolution for non-equilibrium distributions.

Result: Lorentzian wavepackets produce significantly more non-singular bounce trajectories and monotonic $H$-function decay (improved relaxation), whereas Gaussian wavepackets yield mostly singular trajectories and laminar flow with saturated $H$-function.

Conclusion: The functional form of the wavepacket—particularly its momentum-space tail (power-law vs. exponential)—critically determines both singularity resolution and the efficacy of quantum relaxation via the structure of the Bohmian velocity field and quantum potential.

Abstract: We investigate cosmological singularity resolution and relaxation dynamics within the Bohmian mechanics via the plane-symmetric Bianchi type-I minisuperspace model in the Wheeler-DeWitt framework of quantum cosmology by constructing wave functions as Gaussian and Lorentzian wavepackets. Our analyses of the corresponding Bohmian trajectories reveal that Gaussian superposition predominantly yields classical singular solutions, with only a low fraction of small-amplitude cyclic trajectories. On the other hand, the Lorentzian wavepacket, characterized by the power-law momentum tail, generates stronger quantum potential barrier and a substantially rich velocity field, producing a significant fraction of non-singular bounce trajectories over extended volume ranges. We further examine quantum relaxation by evolving non-equilibrium distributions under the corresponding guidance dynamics. The Gaussian superposition exhibits laminar flow leading to boundary accumulation and incomplete relaxation, with non-monotonic decay of the $H$-function followed by saturation. In contrast, the Lorentzian wavepacket induces more complex trajectories, yielding monotonic decay of the $H$-function and better, though still incomplete, approach to Born-rule equilibrium. These results demonstrate that the inherent structure of the wave packet governs both singularity resolution and quantum relaxation through the nature of the Bohmian velocity field.

</details>


### [31] [The point-particle-limit effective-source approach for computing gravitational self-force in the Lorenz gauge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27284)
*Chao Zhang,Yungui Gong,Xuchen Lu,Wenting Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Introduces a point-particle-limit effective source method that eliminates analytical complexity and smoothness constraints of the traditional effective-source approach, enabling accurate, efficient time-domain computation of gravitational self-force via discontinuous Galerkin enforcement of metric jump conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome high computational cost and implementation complexity caused by complex analytical expressions and inherent smoothness limits in the traditional effective-source method for self-force calculations.

Method: Analytically takes the effective source size to zero (point-particle limit), reformulating the problem as a well-defined jump condition of the retarded metric field at the particle location governed by the local singular field; couples this formulation with a discontinuous Galerkin numerical scheme to enforce jump conditions accurately.

Result: Superior numerical accuracy and efficiency compared to the traditional effective-source method in computing time-domain gravitational metric perturbations and self-force for a point particle on a circular orbit around a Schwarzschild black hole in the Lorenz gauge.

Conclusion: The point-particle-limit effective source method is validated as correct and efficient, providing a robust numerical foundation for extending computations to generic geodesic orbits and long-time self-consistent orbital evolution.

Abstract: The traditional effective-source method is hampered by complex analytical expressions and the inherent smoothness limit, which incur high computational costs and complicate implementation. To overcome these limitations, we introduce the point-particle-limit effective source method, which analytically takes the size of the effective source to zero, thereby transforming the problem into a well-defined jump condition of retarded metric field at the particle position governed by the local singular field. This formulation naturally pairs with a discontinuous Galerkin scheme, whose inherent capacity for accommodating solution discontinuities enables highly accurate enforcement of the jump conditions. We apply both the traditional and point-particle-limit effective source method to calculate the time-domain gravitational metric perturbation and gravitational self-force in the Lorenz gauge on a point particle in a circular orbit around a Schwarzschild black hole. The comparison of numerical results shows the excellent advantage of the point-particle-limit effective source method, which validates the correctness and efficiency of the point-particle-limit effective source method and thereby establishes a numerical foundation for computing generic geodesic orbits or long-time self-consistent orbital evolution.

</details>


### [32] [Quantizing the exterior region of a Schwarzschild-AdS black hole leads to a resolution of the information paradox on a quantum level](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27339)
*Claus Gerhardt*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper resolves the black hole information paradox by unitarily relating quantum descriptions of Schwarzschild-AdS black hole exterior and interior regions via shared temporal eigenvalues and consistent spatial eigendistribution multiplicities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the black hole information paradox in the quantum description of Schwarzschild-AdS black holes, particularly addressing ambiguity in spatial eigendistribution multiplicities ($m_i$) in the exterior region.

Method: Quantizes both exterior and interior regions of a Schwarzschild-AdS black hole; solves the resulting hyperbolic equation using temporal eigenfunctions $w_i$ (simple eigenvalues) and spatial eigendistributions $v_{ij}$ (with multiplicities $m_i$); imposes consistency of $m_i$ between regions to enforce unitary equivalence of Hilbert spaces and Hamiltonians.

Result: A unitary equivalence is established between the quantum descriptions of the exterior and interior regions, ensuring preservation of quantum information and eliminating the information paradox at the quantum level.

Conclusion: The information paradox does not arise in this quantum gravity model because the exterior and interior quantizations are unitarily equivalent via shared temporal spectrum and consistently assigned spatial degeneracies.

Abstract: We quantize the exterior region of a Schwarzschild-AdS black hole using our model of quantum gravity. The resulting hyperbolic equation is solved by products of temporal eigenfunctions $w_i$, the eigenvalues of which all have multiplicity one, and spatial eigendistributions $v_{ij}$ having the same eigenvalues but with multiplicities $1\le m_i$, where the $m_i$ could in principle be arbitrarily large. Regarding only the exterior region, there was no guidance how to determine the values of the $m_i$. However, considering also the quantization of the interior region, where the same question did not arise since the $m_i$ could be chosen by maximizing the value, it seemed logical to choose the same values, too, in the exterior case. Since the eigenvalues in the interior are the same because the temporal Hamiltonian is the same in both cases, this choice defined a unitary equivalence between the respective Hilbert spaces and the respective Hamiltonians. Hence, there is no information paradox on a quantum level.

</details>


### [33] [Continuous Sensitivity Analysis for $δN$ Formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27366)
*S. Mohammad Ahmadi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the δN formalism by incorporating spatial gradient interactions via Continuous Sensitivity Analysis, enabling efficient analytical and numerical computation of cosmological observables—including the power spectrum and equilateral non-Gaussianity—in models with ultra-slow-roll transitions (e.g., Starobinsky).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the technical difficulty of computing phase-space derivatives in the gradient-corrected δN formalism, which is essential for predicting observables beyond the separate-universe approximation.

Method: Applies Continuous Sensitivity Analysis to the gradient-corrected δN framework: solves coupled first-order ODEs for the field Jacobian and Hessian to compute e-fold sensitivity to initial conditions; implements this in the Starobinsky model with ultra-slow-roll transition.

Result: Derives k-dependent power spectrum with full gradient corrections analytically; obtains an analytical estimate of f_NL^eq that captures gradient-sourced contributions accurately.

Conclusion: Continuous Sensitivity Analysis provides a systematic, computationally efficient route to evaluate gradient-corrected δN predictions—enabling robust, high-precision inflationary forecasts beyond the separate-universe limit.

Abstract: The $δN$ formalism provides a powerful non-perturbative framework for following the evolution of primordial curvature perturbations on super-horizon scales. However, its standard implementation relies on the separate universe assumption, which neglects significant spatial gradient interactions. Recent work has addressed this limitation by incorporating gradient interactions directly into the background dynamics through an effective source term in the Klein--Gordon equation, thereby extending the applicability of the $δN$ framework beyond the separate universe approximation. Despite this conceptual progress, practical calculations within the $δN$ formalism remain technically challenging, as cosmological observables require evaluating the sensitivity of the total number of $e$-folds to initial conditions, a task that becomes even more involved once gradient contributions are included. In this work, we develop a systematic method to simplify these calculations by applying Continuous Sensitivity Analysis to the gradient-corrected $δN$ framework. In this approach, the required phase-space derivatives are obtained by solving a set of coupled first-order differential equations for the field Jacobian and Hessian, which significantly streamlines both analytical and numerical evaluations of $δN$ formalism. As an explicit demonstration, we apply the method to the Starobinsky model, which features a sharp transition into an ultra-slow-roll phase. Within this setup, we derive analytical expressions for the $k$-dependent power spectrum including full gradient corrections, and obtain an analytical estimate of the equilateral non-Gaussianity parameter $f_{\rm NL}^{\rm eq}$ that accurately captures the gradient-sourced contributions.

</details>


### [34] [Thermal channels of scalar and tensor waves in Jordan-frame scalar--tensor gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27386)
*David S. Pereira,Francisco S. N Lobo,José Pedro Mimoso*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes scalar and tensor perturbations in Jordan-frame scalar-tensor gravity using an Einstein-like effective-fluid decomposition, identifying exact Eckart-type constitutive relations for effective fluid variables (density, pressure, heat flux, anisotropic stress) at linear order; it shows these variables govern each linearized Einstein equation channel, links gravitational-wave damping modifications to the transverse-traceless anisotropic stress, and clarifies the gauge behavior and matching conditions of the invariant κT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a thermodynamically inspired, effective-fluid description of scalar-tensor gravity perturbations that unifies scalar and tensor dynamics and potentially reveals deeper thermodynamic structure—especially for gravitational-wave propagation—beyond standard geometric formulations.

Method: Linear perturbation theory about a spatially flat FLRW background in Jordan-frame scalar-tensor gravity; use of Einstein-like effective-fluid decomposition of the scalar sector; derivation of perturbed effective density, pressure, heat flux, and anisotropic stress in the scalar-gradient frame; identification of exact Eckart-type constitutive relations at first order; mapping of each effective-fluid variable to a specific linearized Einstein or tensor propagation equation; analysis of the invariant κT evolution and its gauge properties and flux-matching constraints.

Result: Exact linear-order Eckart-type constitutive identifications for all effective-fluid variables; one-to-one correspondence between effective-fluid channels and linearized field equations (Hamiltonian → density, momentum → heat flux, trace → pressure, traceless → anisotropic stress, tensor → transverse-traceless anisotropic stress); Jordan-frame GW damping modification fully encoded in the scalar-sector transverse-traceless anisotropic stress; κT perturbation is gauge-dependent and not fixed by FLRW flux matching—only its background value is constrained.

Conclusion: The effective-fluid picture provides a complete, channel-wise decomposition of linearized scalar-tensor dynamics, supporting a thermodynamic interpretation of gravitational waves in such theories—but only as an effective (not intrinsic) description at this stage; the freedom in δ(κT) leaves room for future work on intrinsic thermodynamic characterizations.

Abstract: We study first-order scalar and tensor perturbations of Jordan-frame scalar--tensor gravity about a spatially flat FLRW background using the Einstein-like effective-fluid decomposition of the scalar sector. In the scalar-gradient frame, we derive the perturbed effective density, pressure, heat flux, and anisotropic stress, and show that they admit an exact Eckart-type constitutive identification at linear order. We then show that these same quantities appear explicitly and exhaustively in the linearized field equations: the scalar Hamiltonian, momentum, trace, and traceless Einstein-like equations are governed, respectively, by the effective density, heat-flux, pressure, and anisotropic-stress channels, while the tensor propagation equation is governed by the transverse-traceless anisotropic-stress channel. In particular, the Jordan-frame modification of gravitational-wave damping is identified with the effective transverse-traceless anisotropic stress of the scalar sector. We also derive the perturbed evolution equation for the invariant product $κT$, clarify its gauge behavior, and show that flux matching on FLRW fixes only the background value $\overline{κT}$, not its perturbation. These results leave open the possibility that gravitational waves in scalar--tensor gravity admit a deeper thermodynamic characterization, perhaps even an intrinsic one, although the present analysis establishes this only at the level of an effective constitutive description.

</details>

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the gravitational lensing effects and the dynamic evolution of the shadow of Vaidya black holes by employing backward ray-tracing techniques. Notably, during and after the active accretion phase, a distinct lensing ring emerges outside the shadow. Extending this analysis to the thin accretion disk model reveals richer observational signatures. A bright ring, formed by the superposition of the photon ring and lensing ring, appears outside the shadow but persists only during the initial and final stages of accretion, vanishing entirely when accretion becomes active. Interestingly, as the accretion process progresses, an additional ring-like structure, which is caused by the dynamical redshift effect, emerges in the image. This ring-like structure not only contracts inward but also brightens continuously as accretion proceeds. Under varying observational inclinations, the Doppler effect and the dynamical redshift effect jointly modulate the brightness distribution of the image, resulting in significant asymmetry in the inner shadow, bright ring, and additional ring. Our findings uncover dynamical redshift as a novel observable phenomenon intrinsic to evolving spacetimes, offering a potential discriminant for identifying accreting black holes and providing observational access to the imprints of temporal spacetime evolution on black hole images.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the thermodynamics of Schwarzschild black and white holes within a $q$-deformed Wheeler--DeWitt framework. By introducing a $q$-deformed Heisenberg--Weyl algebra at a root of unity, we derive a finite-dimensional Hilbert space, a bounded mass spectrum, and an adiabatic invariant leading to a bounded entropy-mass relation. The deformation results in a universal logarithmic correction, as well as a minimum temperature and a maximum entropy that matches the de Sitter bound. Also, we examine the interpretation of a cold remnant, which is dynamically stable because its radiation rate approaches zero, even though its heat capacity remains negative. We also explore the holographic implications of this limited entropy. Our results thus provide a consistent semiclassical picture, where quantum deformation naturally introduces an entropy bound, avoids divergences at the final evaporation stage, and suggests a smooth transition from quantum gravity to cosmology.

Abstract: We investigate the realization of a nonsingular cosmological bounce in metric $f(R)$ gravity using a controlled exponential deformation of the Starobinsky $R^{2}$ model. Adopting a smooth Gaussian-type bouncing scale factor, we first demonstrate a no-go result showing that a positive-curvature vacuum bounce cannot be supported by the model $f(R)=R+αR^{2}(1-e^{-R/R_b})$ alone. We then show that a minimal extension obtained by introducing a constant term restores the bounce exactly, with the constant fixed algebraically by the bounce condition. A systematic parameter-space scan is performed to identify regions free of ghost and tachyonic instabilities. Working in the Einstein frame, we study the evolution of scalar and tensor perturbations across the bounce and show that both remain finite and well behaved. Our results establish a minimal, perturbatively stable realization of a vacuum bounce in $f(R)$ gravity that goes beyond background-level constructions.

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify black hole shadow observations and gravitational ringdown (QNM) spectroscopy as complementary probes of strong-field gravity, particularly in modified gravity (STVG/MOG) coupled to dark matter (PFDM), where independent constraints are limited.

Result: Both shadow radius R_sh and QNM real frequency ω_R increase with PFDM intensity λ but decrease with MOG parameter α; the eikonal relation ω_R = l / R_sh holds numerically across all three methods for large l; peak height of effective potential mirrors this parametric trend.

Abstract: We investigate the connection between black hole shadow and quasinormal mode (QNM) spectra in the context of scalar--tensor--vector gravity (STVG) coupled to perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM), characterized by the MOG parameter $α$ and the dark matter intensity $λ$. Employing complementary methods -- namely the sixth-order WKB approximation, Padé resummation, and time-domain numerical integration -- we compute QNM frequencies for scalar ($s=0$), electromagnetic ($s=1$), and axial gravitational ($s=2$) perturbations. Both the real part of the QNM frequencies and the peak height of the effective potential display a consistent parametric dependence: they increase with $λ$ yet decrease with growing $α$. In the eikonal limit ($l \gg 1$), we derive an exact analytical link between the shadow radius $R_{\mathrm{sh}}$ and the QNM frequency $ω_R$. Noting that $R_{\mathrm{sh}}$ is determined by the critical impact parameter $b_c = r_{\mathrm{ph}}/\sqrt{f(r_{\mathrm{ph}})}$, while $ω_R = Ωl$ with photon angular velocity $Ω= \sqrt{f(r_{\mathrm{ph}})}/r_{\mathrm{ph}}$, we obtain the precise relation $ω_R = l / b_c$, identifying $R_{\mathrm{sh}} \equiv b_c$ for an asymptotically flat observer. This prediction is robustly validated by numerical results across all three computational approaches at large multipole numbers. Our findings reveal that the black hole shadow and gravitational ringdown are not independent phenomena, but dual observational signatures of the same underlying structure -- the unstable photon orbit -- thereby offering a unified multi-messenger framework to simultaneously constrain modified gravity and dark matter in the strong-field regime.

</details>


### [39] [The Hodograph Transform Between Thermodynamics and Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28039)
*Leonid Polterovich*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper links the geometric evolution of light-ray 'skies' for an accelerating observer in Minkowski spacetime to thermodynamic concepts via contact geometry and a hyperbolic hodograph transform, yielding an acceleration-dependent effective temperature consistent in scaling—but not magnitude—with the Unruh effect.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover a geometric-thermodynamic correspondence in general relativity by interpreting light-ray skies (Legendrian submanifolds) in contact-geometric frameworks and relating their evolution under acceleration to thermodynamic potentials.

Result: Generating functions of accelerating observers' skies, after rescaling, behave as reduced free energies of equilibrium thermodynamic systems modulated by the relativistic Doppler effect; an associated effective temperature emerges, linearly proportional to proper acceleration.

Abstract: In the contact-geometric approach to general relativity, the sky of an event - namely, the set of all incoming light rays - forms a Legendrian submanifold of the spherical cotangent bundle of a Cauchy hypersurface. When the hypersurface is chosen to be the Minkowski hyperboloid, a hyperbolic version of the hodograph transform identifies this bundle with a thermodynamic phase space. We consider a uniformly accelerating observer starting on the hyperboloid and study the evolution of its skies. We show that the associated generating functions, after a suitable rescaling, admit a natural interpretation as reduced free energies of equilibrium thermodynamic systems governed by the relativistic Doppler effect. From this data, we extract an effective temperature that is proportional to the acceleration, in agreement with the scaling of the Unruh effect, although the numerical constant differs from the Unruh value.

</details>

Result: High-accuracy analytic formulas validated against Teukolsky results; quantification of eccentricity’s impact on PN convergence and accuracy; demonstration that hybrid approximations retain precision with reduced computational cost; evidence that exponential resummation improves convergence at higher PN orders.

Conclusion: The derived analytic secular evolution formulas—validated, hybridized, and resummed—form robust building blocks for fast, accurate, adiabatic EMRI waveform models essential for LISA data analysis.

Abstract: We analytically derive the secular changes of the orbital parameters, i.e., energy, angular momentum, and Carter constant, for general bound orbits in Kerr spacetime, at leading order in the mass ratio, through the 6th post-Newtonian (6PN) order and the 16th order in orbital eccentricity. We validate the formulas against high-precision numerical Teukolsky results and quantify how eccentricity affects both the achievable accuracy and the PN convergence. We then construct and test a simple ``hybrid'' approximation that combines different PN and eccentricity truncations to retain accuracy at reduced computational cost. We also assess the performance of exponential resummation at higher PN orders. These results provide building blocks for fast, (analytic) adiabatic inspiral and waveform models for extreme mass ratio inspirals relevant to space-based detectors such as the Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA).

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>

Result: Establishes an intrinsic Schrödinger propagator via Heisenberg-group convolution with Lagrangian delta distributions; proves commutativity of polarization-intertwining diagrams up to Maslov phase; identifies the conformal tensor H(u) as governing both null-cone geometry and Schrödinger Hamiltonian; connects analysis to Weil representation (Lion–Vergne) and Mumford’s theta function theory.

Conclusion: The three structural layers—progressing-wave representation, Heisenberg Fourier analysis, and Schrödinger evolution—are deeply intertwined through the geometry of a positive Lagrangian curve, yielding a coherent quantization-compatible framework for wave dynamics on plane wave backgrounds, with implications for mathematical physics and geometric representation theory.

Abstract: This article studies wave equations and their solutions on plane wave spacetimes of arbitrary dimension, developing the interplay among three structural layers: the Ward progressing-wave representation of solutions to the scalar wave equation, the Fourier analysis of the Heisenberg group naturally associated to the plane wave, and the Schrödinger propagator governing the evolution of initial data. The central geometric object is a positive curve in the Lagrangian Grassmannian determined by the plane wave metric, previously studied in the authors' series. The conformal tensor $H(u)$ that parametrises this curve plays a dual role: it encodes the null-cone geometry of the spacetime and simultaneously appears as the time-dependent parameter in the Schrödinger representation of the Heisenberg group acting by isometries on the plane wave. Parallel to the classical Fourier inversion theorem, convolution by Lagrangian delta distributions on the Heisenberg group furnishes an intrinsic description of the Schrödinger propagator, and the intertwining of different polarisations by this propagator is captured by a diagram that commutes up to a Maslov phase. The theta functions and Bargmann transforms that arise from imaginary polarisations complete the analytic picture, connecting the present work to the theory of the Weil representation as developed by Lion--Vergne and to Mumford's systematic treatment of theta functions.

</details>

Abstract: This paper investigates the characteristics of quasinormal modes (QNMs) of static, spherically symmetric black holes under the combined influence of spontaneous Lorentz symmetry breaking (LSB) induced by the Kalb-Ramond (KR) field and perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM). Using M87$^\ast$ shadow data from the Event Horizon Telescope (EHT), we constrain the LSB factor $τ$ and PFDM parameter $ζ$ at 1$σ$ confidence. By combining the sixth-order WKB approximation method with timedomain numerical integration, we systematically compute the complex frequency spectrum of QNMs for black holes in this spacetime background. The numerical results reveal an intriguing conclusion: as the LSB factor $τ$ or the PFDM parameter $ζ$ increases, both the real part and the absolute value of the imaginary part of the QNMs frequencies exhibit a monotonic increase, demonstrating a unique "stiffening" effect. This characteristic stands in stark contrast to the decreasing trend of QNMs frequencies observed in models that consider only traditional dark matter, revealing the critical influence of the coupling between the KR field and PFDM on the dynamic evolution of black holes. This study not only enriches and deepens the understanding of black hole perturbation theory within the framework of modified gravity but also, by identifying the distinctive spectral features of QNMs, offers the potential to distinguish whether the KR field and dark matter are coupled in future observations. Thus, it provides a theoretical foundation for testing mechanisms of spacetime symmetry breaking beyond the standard model and for exploring the nature of dark matter.

### [43] [Gravitational wave polarization modes and stability analysis in Weyl geometry gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27985)

Result: Tensor sector: two massless, luminal, transverse-traceless modes. Vector sector: one dynamical degree of freedom but zero polarization modes. Scalar sector: one mixed breathing–longitudinal mode with superluminal phase velocity and exponential amplitude decay due to the Weyl gauge field; suffers Ostrogradsky ghost instability. No Laplacian or ghost instabilities in tensor/vector sectors.

Conclusion: Weyl geometry gravity predicts distinct GW signatures—especially a detectable, decaying scalar mode—but is theoretically challenged by scalar-sector ghost instability, constraining its viability as a fundamental theory unless cured by UV completion or modifications.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational wave polarization modes and stability in Weyl geometry gravity within a Minkowski background. Our results indicate that the tensor sector consists of two standard modes propagating at the speed of light. Although the vector sector possesses a dynamical degree of freedom, it generates no polarization modes. The scalar sector, in contrast, features a mixture mode of breathing and longitudinal modes associated with a single scalar degree of freedom. This degree of freedom exhibits superluminal propagation and intrinsic amplitude decay, both driven by the background Weyl gauge field. We further discuss the observational detectability of this scalar mode. Our stability analysis reveals that, while the tensor and vector sectors are free from ghost and Laplacian instabilities, the scalar sector suffers from an Ostrogradsky ghost instability. These findings clarify the unique gravitational wave propagation characteristics in Weyl geometry gravity and provide theoretical foundations for testing the theory through future multi-messenger observations.

</details>

Abstract: We present an analytic flat-spacetime accelerating boundary analog of Hawking-type emission that possesses infinite asymptotic acceleration (and radial acceleration in the black hole analog) but finite total radiated energy (and zero surface gravity in the black hole analog). The particle spectrum, energy spectrum, and equivalent spacetime metric are derived, revealing an interesting mix of normal and extremal black hole properties.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
