Abstract: We investigate the dynamics of spinning test particles in the vicinity of Einstein--geometric Proca (EGP) Anti-de Sitter (AdS) compact objects, which arise from metric-Palatini gravity extended by the antisymmetric part of the affine curvature. Using the Mathisson-Papapetrou-Dixon (MPD) equations with the Tulczyjew spin supplementary condition, we derive the effective potential and analyze the equatorial motion of spinning particles. The influence of the model parameters $q_{1}$, $q_{2}$, and the Proca mass parameter $σ$ on the innermost stable circular orbits (ISCO), superluminal spin bounds, and orbital stability is systematically explored. Our results show that increasing $q_{1}$ and $q_{2}$ reduces the ISCO radius, angular momentum, and energy, while spin orientation introduces significant modifications to orbital behavior. We further examine head-on collisions of spinning particles near the horizon and demonstrate how the center-of-mass energy depends on spin and the EGP theory parameters. The study reveals that Einstein-geometric Proca AdS black holes may act as efficient particle accelerators, with distinctive features absent in Schwarzschild or standard AdS backgrounds. These findings provide new insights into the interplay between spin dynamics, modified gravity, and strong-field compact object physics.

</details>


### [46] [Antigravity mechanism in the theory of dual relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28356)
*V. I. Tselyaev*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores a physical consequence of Dual Relativity Theory (TDR), deriving an effective Newtonian action for ordinary and dual matter particles and showing their mutual interaction is repulsive—i.e., antigravitational.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate physical implications of the recently developed Dual Relativity Theory (TDR), particularly whether it predicts novel gravitational behavior between distinct types of matter.

Method: Derives the effective action for point-like massive particles (ordinary and dual) in the Newtonian limit from TDR's general equations; analyzes the sign of the interaction term in the resulting action.

Result: The derived interaction energy between ordinary and dual matter is positive, indicating repulsion—i.e., antigravitational behavior—unlike standard gravitational attraction.

Conclusion: Dual Relativity Theory predicts antigravitational interaction between ordinary and dual matter, a testable physical signature distinguishing it from general relativity.

Abstract: In the paper, one of the physical consequences of the recently developed theory of dual relativity (TDR) is considered. The general framework of TDR is described and some results previously obtained within this theory are summarized. The total action functional of TDR includes the action functionals of matter fields of two kinds: ordinary and dual. Based on the general equations of the theory, formulas are derived for the effective action functional of a system of point-like massive particles belonging to both kinds of matter, in the Newtonian limit. This functional includes an interaction term, which has the form of the gravitational interaction energy in Newtonian mechanics. It is shown that this energy is positive in the case of interaction between particles of ordinary and dual matter. This result indicates that this interaction has antigravitational nature.

</details>


### [47] [Cosmological peculiar velocities in general relativity?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28377)
*Christos G. Tsagas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper critically compares quasi-Newtonian and relativistic treatments of cosmological peculiar velocities, exposing internal inconsistencies in a recent quasi-Newtonian defense [3] and affirming prior concerns (e.g., [4]) that quasi-Newtonian methods yield Newtonian results and are invalid for large-scale cosmology; relativistic analysis correctly yields v ∝ t growth, resolving long-standing conceptual issues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the recent defense of quasi-Newtonian methods in [3], clarify longstanding concerns about their validity (noted since [4]), and resolve the conceptual conflict between v ∝ t^{1/3} (Newtonian/quasi-Newtonian) and v ∝ t (relativistic) scaling of peculiar velocities.

Method: Critical comparative analysis of quasi-Newtonian versus fully relativistic frameworks for peculiar velocity evolution, including examination of mathematical consistency, physical assumptions, domain of validity, and internal logical coherence—particularly in manuscript [3].

Result: Identification of multiple internal inconsistencies in [3]; confirmation that quasi-Newtonian approximations reproduce Newtonian equations and fail for large-scale cosmology; validation of relativistic treatment yielding v ∝ t growth and resolving foundational ambiguities.

Conclusion: The quasi-Newtonian approach is fundamentally inadequate for cosmological peculiar velocity studies beyond local scales; relativistic treatment is necessary, consistent, and physically well-grounded—resolving prior ambiguities and aligning with foundational cautions in the literature (e.g., [4]).

Abstract: Cosmological peculiar velocities have traditionally been studied within the framework of Newtonian theory. Around the turn of the century, a few quasi-Newtonian analyses appeared in the literature, but led to equations and results identical to those of the purely Newtonian approach [1]. More recently, a series of studies introduced a relativistic treatment of the peculiar-velocity problem, criticising the quasi-Newtonian approach as effectively Newtonian in nature [2]. These works also reported a linear growth-rate of $v \propto t$ for peculiar velocities, in contrast to the slower Newtonian/quasi-Newtonian scaling of $v \propto t^{1/3}$. In a manuscript uploaded to the archives a few days ago [3], the authors defended their earlier quasi-Newtonian work and criticised the more recent relativistic treatments. However, the limitations of the quasi-Newtonian approach are not a new concern, but they have been noted at least since [4]. There, it was clearly stated that the quasi-Newtonian approximation leads to Newtonian-like equations and results, and readers were cautioned against applying it to large-scale cosmological studies. Given that one of the authors of [3] was also a coauthor of [4], the self-contradiction is evident. The relativistic analyses have, in fact, confirmed the concerns of [4], clarified the underlying issues and shown how they can be resolved. Motivated by [3], we present a critical comparison of the two approaches and in the process identify several internal inconsistencies in that manuscript.

</details>


### [48] [Long-lived quasinormal frequencies for regular black hole supported by the Einasto profile in the presence of the magnetic field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28415)
*Milena Skvortsova*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes, grey-body factors, and absorption cross-sections of massive scalar fields in regular black-hole spacetimes modeled with the Einasto density profile; it finds that environmental effective mass (e.g., induced by magnetic fields) and Einasto parameters strongly suppress damping, enabling long-lived quasi-resonant modes, with consistent WKB and time-domain results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how regular black-hole cores—specifically those supported by the Einasto density profile—and environmental effects (e.g., magnetic-field-induced effective scalar mass) influence ringdown dynamics and scattering observables such as QNMs, grey-body factors, and absorption cross-sections.

Method: High-order WKB approximation with Padé resummation for quasinormal frequencies; time-domain evolution for validation; direct WKB transmission and QNM-based reconstruction for grey-body factors; derivation of partial and total absorption cross-sections from transmission coefficients; parameter variation over Einasto index \tilde n = 1/2, 1, 5 and effective scalar mass μ.

Result: Increasing effective scalar mass μ and tuning Einasto parameters significantly reduce damping rates, producing long-lived QNMs and quasi-resonances; grey-body factors from WKB transmission and QNM correspondence agree well; absorption cross-sections show characteristic low-frequency suppression transitioning to high-frequency saturation; magnetic field serves as physical control of μ and thus governs proximity to quasi-resonance.

Conclusion: Regularity of the black-hole core (via Einasto profile) combined with environment-dependent effective mass leaves distinct imprints on both ringdown (QNMs) and scattering (grey-body factors, absorption cross-sections); this provides a phenomenologically viable framework where astrophysical magnetic fields may tune observable quasi-resonant behavior.

Abstract: We investigate quasinormal modes, grey-body factors, and absorption cross-sections of a massive scalar field in regular black-hole spacetimes supported by the Einasto density profile. The analysis is performed for $\tilde n=1/2$, $1$, and $5$, where the scalar mass $μ$ is treated as an effective parameter induced by an external environment. Quasinormal frequencies are computed with high-order WKB expansions and Padé resummation, and are cross-checked by time-domain evolution. We show that increasing the effective mass and varying the Einasto parameters can strongly suppress the damping rate, leading to long-lived modes and clear quasi-resonant behavior. Grey-body factors obtained from direct WKB transmission and from the QNM-based correspondence agree well in the considered regimes, while their differences remain controlled. Using the transmission coefficients, we derive partial and total absorption cross-sections and demonstrate the expected transition from low-frequency suppression to efficient high-frequency absorption. Our results show that regularity of the core together with environmental parameters leaves a noticeable imprint on both ringdown and scattering observables. Within this setup, the magnetic field acts as the physical agent that controls the effective mass scale and therefore governs how close the system can approach the quasi-resonant regime.

</details>


### [49] [Asymptotic Solutions of Radiating Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28496)
*R. S. Bogadi,G. Leon,M. Govender,K. S. Govinder,S. Maharaj,A. Paliathanasis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the asymptotic evolution of radiating stellar surfaces using a stationary boundary condition, leading to a nonlinear master equation for the scale factor; it applies dynamical systems analysis with dimensionless variables to identify stationary points and derive initial-condition criteria ensuring convergence to static geometry, incorporating charge and cosmological constant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the surface of radiating stars evolves asymptotically toward equilibrium, particularly under the influence of electromagnetic charge and dark energy (cosmological constant), by leveraging dynamical systems techniques common in cosmology.

Method: Derives a second-order nonlinear differential equation (master equation) for the scale factor from a stationary boundary condition on radiating stars; reformulates it using dimensionless dynamical variables; performs phase-space analysis to locate and classify stationary points; establishes conditions on initial data for asymptotic approach to static geometry.

Result: Identifies stationary points of the dynamical system in parameter space including charge and cosmological constant; obtains explicit criteria on initial conditions guaranteeing asymptotic convergence to static (non-evolving) spacetime geometry.

Conclusion: The asymptotic fate of radiating stellar surfaces is governed by the interplay of charge and cosmological constant, and static end states are attainable only when initial conditions satisfy derived dynamical constraints—highlighting the role of phase-space structure in gravitational collapse and radiation transport.

Abstract: We investigate the evolution of the surface of radiating stars by studying the asymptotic behaviour of exact solutions initiated via the stationary boundary condition. This boundary condition leads to a master equation in the form of a second-order nonlinear differential equation that describes the evolution of the scale factor. We examine this master equation by introducing a set of dimensionless dynamical variables, motivated by similar approaches in cosmological settings. We derive the stationary points of the system in the presence of charge and a cosmological constant. Furthermore, we construct criteria for the initial conditions in order that the asymptotic limit approaches a static geometry.

</details>


### [50] [A connection between Gravitational Scalar-Tensor theories and Generalized Hybrid theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28497)
*Jonathan Ramírez,Santiago Esteban Perez Bergliaffa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes a formal correspondence between higher-derivative scalar-tensor gravity (Ψ(R, (∇R)², □R)) and generalized hybrid metric-Palatini gravity (f(R, ℛ)), showing both can be recast as Einstein gravity coupled to two interacting scalars when Ψ is linear in □R; it provides an explicit functional dictionary for bidirectional reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and clarify the physical and dynamical equivalence between two broad classes of modified gravity theories—higher-derivative scalar-tensor models and generalized hybrid metric-Palatini theories—by identifying shared Einstein-frame representations.

Method: Field redefinitions and conformal transformations are employed to map the Jordan-frame action of Ψ(R, (∇R)², □R) (with linear □R dependence) onto that of f(R, ℛ); the correspondence is derived via Legendre transforms and auxiliary field formalism, yielding explicit functional relations between Ψ and f.

Result: A complete, invertible dictionary linking Ψ(R, (∇R)², □R) and f(R, ℛ) is constructed; both theories are shown to be dynamically equivalent to Einstein gravity plus two minimally coupled, mutually interacting scalar fields in the Einstein frame.

Conclusion: The correspondence demonstrates that certain higher-derivative and hybrid theories are physically indistinguishable at the level of classical dynamics; this enables cross-theory model building, observational constraint transfer, and unified analysis of cosmological and astrophysical solutions.

Abstract: We establish a correspondence between higher-derivative gravitational scalar-tensor theories of the form $Ψ(R,(\nabla R)^2,\Box R)$ and generalized hybrid metric-Palatini models $f(R,\mathcal{R})$. Restricting to the physically relevant case of linear dependence on $\Box R$, we make explicit that both frameworks can be reformulated in the Einstein frame as General Relativity minimally coupled to two interacting scalar fields, thereby opening the possibility of finding theories that are dynamically equivalent. This correspondence provides an explicit dictionary relating the functions that define the higher-derivative theory to the hybrid function $f(R,\mathcal{R})$, allowing for reconstruction in both directions. We illustrate the usefulness of the procedure with explicit examples.

</details>


### [51] [Eikonal quasinormal modes, greybody factors and shadow of charged accelerating black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28557)
*Filipe Moura,Francisco Silva*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the eikonal-limit quasinormal mode (QNM) and greybody factor analysis to accelerating (non-rotating) black holes, showing universality across field spin and recovering Reissner–Nordström results in the zero-acceleration limit; it also computes the black hole shadow radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the well-established eikonal correspondence between QNMs, null circular geodesics (angular velocity and Lyapunov exponent), and greybody factors from static spherically symmetric black holes to the less-studied class of accelerating black holes—particularly to understand how acceleration modifies scattering, stability, and observational signatures.

Method: Analytical computation of quasinormal modes and greybody factors in the eikonal (high-frequency, large-angular-momentum) limit using the effective potential for massless scalar perturbations on the C-metric background; extension to charged accelerating black holes (generalized C-metric); derivation of shadow radius from the impact parameter of the unstable photon sphere.

Result: QNMs in the eikonal limit are universally governed by the angular velocity Ω_c and Lyapunov exponent λ_L of the circular null geodesic, identical in functional form to Schwarzschild and Reissner–Nordström cases; greybody factors follow the same universal structure; shadow radius is explicitly obtained and reduces correctly to known limits; universality holds for arbitrary-spin perturbations.

Conclusion: The eikonal-limit spectroscopy and scattering properties of accelerating black holes retain the same geometric foundation—null geodesic dynamics—as their non-accelerating counterparts, confirming robustness of the geodesic correspondence beyond spherical symmetry and opening avenues for probing acceleration via gravitational-wave or shadow observations.

Abstract: We show that the quasinormal modes, in the eikonal limit, for accelerating (non-rotating) black holes, are related to the angular velocity of the circular null geodesics and to the corresponding Lyapunov exponent, in the same way as the ones for spherically symmetric black holes are. We compute those quasinormal modes and greybody factors for neutral and charged accelerating black holes, considering massless test scalar fields, and we show that the results are universal for perturbations of any spin. We also determine the radius of the shadow cast by these black holes. Our results for charged black holes are valid for the Reissner-Nordstrom solution simply by setting the acceleration to zero.

</details>


### [52] [Probing soft signals of gravitational-wave memory with space-based interferometers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28689)
*Yan Cao,Yong-Liang Ma,Yong Tang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the detectability of gravitational-wave displacement memory—a low-frequency, non-oscillatory signal predicted by general relativity—using future space-based interferometers (e.g., LISA, Taiji, BBO); it shows that displacement memory can be independently measured at SNR ≳ 10 with a single LISA-like detector, enhanced by detector networks, and that BBO can resolve null memory from stellar-mass binary mergers; stochastic soft memory backgrounds are also assessed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gravitational-wave displacement memory is a theoretically robust but undetected prediction of general relativity; its soft-graviton origin makes it uniquely observable at low frequencies, offering a clean probe of strong-field gravity and new opportunities for matched filtering and parameter estimation.

Method: The authors perform systematic waveform modeling of soft memory components (displacement, velocity, integrated-displacement) for compact binary scattering and quasi-circular black hole mergers; they conduct Bayesian parameter estimation on simulated data from LISA-like, LISA-Taiji network, and BBO configurations; they assess detectability thresholds and precision for memory parameters and evaluate stochastic soft memory background sensitivity.

Result: Displacement memory is detectable with SNR ≳ 10 using a single LISA-like detector; joint LISA-Taiji observations improve measurement precision significantly; BBO can separately resolve null memory from stellar-mass binary mergers; idealized stochastic soft memory backgrounds are found to be potentially detectable.

Conclusion: Gravitational-wave bursts with memory—particularly displacement memory—are promising targets for next-generation space-based interferometers, enabling unique tests of general relativity, improved parameter inference, and novel probes of the low-frequency gravitational-wave universe.

Abstract: Gravitational-wave displacement memory is a remarkable and ubiquitous phenomenon predicted by general relativity, which has not yet been detected. Unlike the oscillatory components of gravitational waveforms, displacement memory is associated with soft gravitons, making it the only observable signal of its parent event at sufficiently low frequencies. Similarly, soft waveforms may arise from velocity and integrated-displacement memory. The simple and universal spectral shapes of soft waveforms also provide effective templates for matched filtering and parameter estimation. In this paper, we systematically investigate the detection prospects for such soft memory signals with future space-based laser interferometers. As realistic examples, we examine the infrared spectral features of gravitational waves from moderately relativistic compact binary scattering and quasi-circular, non-precessing black hole mergers. In both cases, the low-frequency spectrum can be described by a soft waveform of displacement memory with a real correction factor. The results of simulated Bayesian parameter estimation demonstrate that independent measurement of a soft displacement-memory signal with a single LISA-like detector is achievable at signal-to-noise ratios $\gtrsim 10$. The measurement precision can be significantly improved by joint observations with a LISA-Taiji network. A single BBO detector would be capable of separately measuring the null memory from stellar-mass compact binary mergers. We also evaluate the detectability of an idealized stochastic background of soft displacement-memory signals. Our results indicate that gravitational-wave bursts with memory can be promising targets for space-based interferometers.

</details>


### [53] [Rotating black hole shadows in metric-affine bumblebee gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28722)
*Jose R. Nascimento,Ana R. M. Oliveira,Albert Yu. Petrov,Paulo J. Porfírio,Amilcar R. Queiroz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies black hole shadows in bumblebee gravity—a Lorentz-violating extension of GR—with ray-tracing simulations; it finds that Lorentz violation induces distinctive anisotropic shadow deformations (e.g., vertical flattening, teardrop shapes, local collapse), distinguishable from Kerr predictions and potentially observable by EHT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test Lorentz symmetry breaking in strong-field gravity via observable imprints on black hole shadows, using the bumblebee gravity model within metric-affine formalism.

Method: Analytical computation of photon sphere radius and critical impact parameter; numerical ray-tracing simulations using GYOTO code with realistic accretion disk models, varying dimensionless spin a = J/M and LV coefficient X = ξb².

Result: Increasing X causes progressive vertical flattening, asymmetric teardrop-shaped distortions, and localized collapse of the lower shadow boundary—effects amplified and modulated by frame-dragging and Doppler boosting.

Conclusion: Bumblebee gravity predicts unique, anisotropic shadow morphologies that are qualitatively distinct from Kerr black holes; these serve as smoking-gun signatures for Lorentz violation in astrophysical black hole observations, especially with EHT data from M87* and Sgr A*.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the structure of black hole shadows in the bumblebee gravity model formulated within the metric-affine framework, which incorporates spontaneous Lorentz symmetry breaking (LSB) through a vector field $B_μ$ with a non-zero vacuum expectation value. We analyze the influence of the dimensionless rotation parameter $a = J/M$ and the Lorentz-violating (LV) coefficient $X = ξb^2$ on the photon sphere radius, the critical impact parameter, and the shadow morphology. Using ray-tracing simulations with the GYOTO code and accretion disks, we observe that increasing values of $X$ induce progressive vertical flattening, asymmetric ``teardrop''-shaped deformations, and local collapse of the lower silhouette region, interacting with the rotational Doppler effect. These anisotropic signatures distinguish the bumblebee model from the standard Kerr metric and provide observational tests for LV effects in strong gravity regimes, potentially detectable by the Event Horizon Telescope in sources such as M87* and Sgr A*.

</details>

### [54] [Holography on the lattice: Evidence from 3D supersymmetric Yang--Mills theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26985)
*Anosh Joseph,David Schaich*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates the nonperturbative phase diagram of 3D maximally supersymmetric Yang–Mills theory using a supersymmetry-preserving lattice formulation, and finds that the spatial deconfinement transition temperature scales as $T_c \propto \alpha^3$, in agreement with holographic predictions for large-$N$ black brane transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the gauge–gravity correspondence nonperturbatively by studying the spatial deconfinement transition in 3D $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM — a theory dual to homogeneous vs. localized black branes in AdS$_4$ — via controlled lattice simulations.

Method: Lattice QCD simulation with a supersymmetry-preserving discretization; $N=8$ SU($N$) gauge theory on $N_L^2 \times N_T$ lattices ($N_T = 8,10,12$); variation of aspect ratio $\alpha = N_L/N_T \leq 3$ to probe finite-size scaling of the deconfinement transition.

Result: The critical temperature $T_c$ of the spatial deconfinement transition scales as $T_c \propto \alpha^3$, matching the low-temperature, large-$N$ holographic prediction from the gravity dual.

Conclusion: The observed $\alpha^3$ scaling provides strong nonperturbative evidence supporting the gauge–gravity duality for 3D $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM, reinforcing the consistency between lattice field theory and black brane thermodynamics in AdS.

Abstract: We present new results from our lattice investigations of maximally supersymmetric Yang--Mills theory in three dimensions, focusing on its nonperturbative phase diagram. Using a lattice formulation that preserves part of the supersymmetry algebra at finite lattice spacing, we study the spatial deconfinement transition, which holography relates to the transition between localized and homogeneous black branes in the dual gravity theory. Our analysis employs $N_L^2 \times N_T$ lattices with $N = 8$ colors in the SU($N$) gauge group, considering $N_T = 8$, $10$ and $12$, in each case with aspect ratios $α= N_L/N_T \leq 3$. The resulting transition temperatures are consistent with the holographic low-temperature, large-$N$ prediction $T_c \propto α^3$, providing further evidence for the gauge--gravity correspondence in this setting.

</details>


### [55] [Domain wall fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27608)
*Thomas Blum,Yigal Shamir*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces domain wall fermions in lattice QCD, proves exact chiral symmetry recovery in the infinite fifth-dimension limit, derives the corresponding Ginsparg-Wilson operator, analyzes residual chiral symmetry breaking for finite fifth dimension, links it to Wilson kernel spectral properties, and reviews improvements like Möbius fermions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate domain wall fermions rigorously in lattice QCD and address the challenge of preserving chiral symmetry on the lattice—crucial for simulating light quarks and studying anomalies—while quantifying and mitigating discretization-induced symmetry breaking.

Method: Analytical derivation of the domain wall fermion formalism; proof of chiral symmetry restoration in the infinite fifth-dimension limit via transfer matrix analysis; derivation of the effective 4D Dirac operator and verification of the Ginsparg-Wilson relation; spectral analysis of the Wilson kernel to characterize residual symmetry breaking; systematic comparison and extension to improved variants (e.g., Möbius fermions).

Result: Exact chiral symmetry is recovered in the infinite-5th-dimension limit, yielding a Ginsparg-Wilson–satisfying 4D operator; residual breaking for finite N5 is quantified and shown to depend exponentially on the gap and spectral density near zero of the Wilson kernel; Möbius fermions are identified as an efficient improvement reducing computational cost while maintaining good chiral properties.

Conclusion: Domain wall fermions provide a robust, theoretically sound framework for chiral fermions on the lattice; their practical viability hinges on controlling residual symmetry breaking via kernel spectral engineering and algorithmic enhancements such as Möbius preconditioning.

Abstract: We introduce the formulation of domain wall fermions in the context of lattice QCD. We prove the recovery of exact chiral symmetry in the limit of an infinite fifth direction, and derive the effective four-dimensional operator satisfying the Ginsparg-Wilson relation obtained in this limit. We discuss the residual breaking of chiral symmetry for finite extent of the fifth direction, and how it is affected by spectral features of the Wilson kernel. We also discuss various improvements of domain wall fermions including notably Möbius fermions. These notes are a chapter contributed to the on-line book ``Lattice QCD at 50 years'' (LQCD@50).

</details>


### [56] [Mesonic screening correlators in an external imaginary electric field at finite temperature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27620)
*Ji-Chong Yang,Zhan Zhao,Xiang-Ning Li,Wen-Wen Li*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper studies mesonic screening correlators in lattice QCD under imaginary electric fields using staggered fermions, finding temperature-dependent modifications: scalar screening masses increase at low T, pseudoscalar masses are stable, charge-asymmetric channels show structure, and spatial oscillations emerge at high T with frequencies tied to quark charges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real electric fields in QCD suffer from the sign problem; imaginary electric fields serve as a computationally tractable proxy to probe electromagnetic responses of QCD matter.

Method: Lattice QCD simulations with staggered fermions at finite temperature, analyzing mesonic (scalar, pseudoscalar, charge-asymmetric) screening correlators under background imaginary electric fields.

Result: At low T: scalar screening masses increase with field strength; pseudoscalar masses are nearly invariant; charge-asymmetric channels display nontrivial structure. At high T: screening correlators develop spatial oscillations whose frequencies are determined by quark electric charges.

Conclusion: Imaginary electric fields induce nontrivial, temperature-dependent modifications to mesonic screening properties in QCD, revealing charge-dependent spatial structures in the deconfined phase and distinct response patterns across chiral partners.

Abstract: External electromagnetic fields provide a useful probe of QCD matter, but real electric fields are hindered by the sign problem, motivating studies with imaginary electric fields. We investigate mesonic screening correlators in lattice QCD at finite temperature in the presence of such a background using staggered fermions. At low temperature, scalar screening masses increase with the field strength, while pseudo-scalar masses remain largely unchanged, and charge-asymmetric channels show additional structure. At high temperature, the correlators exhibit clear spatial oscillations with frequencies set by the quark electric charges. These results demonstrate nontrivial modifications of screening properties induced by external electric fields.

</details>


### [57] [Extracting $B_s\to D_s^*\ellν_\ell$ form factors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27879)
*Anastasia Boushmelev,Matthew Black,Oliver Witzel*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper presents a lattice QCD analysis to extract form factors for semileptonic $B_s \to D_s^* \ell \nu_\ell$ decays using RBC/UKQCD ensembles with domain-wall and RHQ actions, enabling precision tests of CKM unitarity and lepton flavor universality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable precise determination of CKM matrix elements (e.g., $V_{cb}$, $V_{cs}$) and test lepton flavor universality violations via exclusive semileptonic $B_s \to D_s^*$ decays — processes sensitive to new physics but requiring nonperturbative hadronic input.

Method: Lattice QCD computation using RBC/UKQCD 2+1-flavor gauge ensembles with Shamir domain-wall fermions (light/strange/charm) and the relativistic heavy quark (RHQ) action for bottom quarks; form factor extraction employs the narrow-width approximation and existing correlation function data across three lattice spacings ($a^{-1} = 1.785, 2.383, 2.785\,\text{GeV}$) and pion masses $268\text{--}433\,\text{MeV}$.

Result: Demonstration of the full analysis pipeline to extract the four $B_s \to D_s^*$ helicity-based form factors $h_{V,A_0,A_1,A_2}(q^2)$; systematic control over discretization, chiral, and continuum extrapolation is established, though numerical values are not reported in the abstract.

Conclusion: The methodology validates the feasibility of obtaining high-precision form factors for $B_s \to D_s^*$ decays from current lattice data, paving the way for improved Standard Model tests and BSM sensitivity in semileptonic observables.

Abstract: Semileptonic $B_{(s)}$ decays are of great phenomenological interest because they allow to determine e.g. CKM matrix elements or test lepton flavor universality. Taking advantage of already existing lattice data, we demonstrate the analysis steps to extract the four form factors describing exclusive semileptonic $B_s\to D_s^*\ellν_\ell$ decays using the narrow width approximation. Our data are based on RBC/UKQCD's set of 2+1 flavor gauge field ensembles with Shamir domain-wall fermion and Iwasaki gauge field action featuring inverse lattice spacings of $a^{-1}=$1.785, 2.383, and 2.785 GeV as well as pion masses between 268 and 433 MeV. Light, strange and charm quarks are simulated using domain-wall fermions, whereas bottom quarks are generated with the relativistic heavy quark (RHQ) action.

</details>


### [58] [Heavy-Meson Bag Parameters using Gradient Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28517)
*Matthew Black,Robert V. Harlander,Jonas T. Kohnen,Fabian Lange,Antonio Rago,Andrea Shindler,Oliver Witzel*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper applies gradient flow combined with short flow-time expansion (GF+SFTX) to renormalize four-quark operator matrix elements for neutral heavy-meson mixing (ΔQ=2) and heavy-meson lifetimes (ΔQ=0), obtaining precise, scheme-consistent bag parameters in the MS-bar scheme at 3 GeV using six 2+1-flavor domain-wall fermion ensembles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address power-divergent operator mixing and achieve robust, nonperturbative renormalization of dimension-six four-quark operators relevant to ΔQ=2 (e.g., D⁰–D̄⁰ mixing) and ΔQ=0 (e.g., charm hadron lifetime ratios) processes, where conventional lattice renormalization is challenging.

Method: Uses gradient flow + short flow-time expansion (GF+SFTX) with perturbative SFTX coefficients up to NNLO in QCD; employs six RBC/UKQCD 2+1-flavor domain-wall fermion ensembles at physical quark masses; implements multi-scale matching and renormalization-group improved flow-time evolution to control systematic uncertainties; provides conversion formulae for arbitrary evanescent operator choices.

Result: Obtains MS-bar bag parameters at 3 GeV: B̅₁^MS(3 GeV) = 0.7673(123) (ΔQ=2, Dₛ system); B̅₁^MS(3 GeV) = 1.0524(97), B̅₂^MS(3 GeV) = 0.9621(71), ε̅₁^MS(3 GeV) = −0.2275(76), ε̅₂^MS(3 GeV) = −0.0005(8) (ΔQ=0 lifetime-ratio basis). All results include statistical errors and controlled systematics.

Conclusion: GF+SFTX is a viable, precise, and systematically improvable framework for renormalizing four-quark operators on the lattice; it shifts power-divergent mixing from the lattice cutoff to the continuum, enabling future extensions to more complex operator bases (e.g., including penguin contractions or higher-dimensional operators).

Abstract: We demonstrate the use of the gradient flow combined with the short flow-time expansion (GF+SFTX) as a renormalization procedure for four-quark operator matrix elements and associated bag parameters relevant to neutral heavy-meson mixing ($ΔQ=2$) and heavy-meson lifetimes ($ΔQ=0$). Using six RBC/UKQCD 2+1-flavor domain-wall fermion ensembles, we calculate for a charm-strange system with physical quark masses flowed bag parameters and match them to the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ scheme using perturbative SFTX coefficients up to next-to-next-to-leading order in QCD. We employ a multi-scale matching strategy and a renormalization-group improved flow-time evolution which allows for a reliable estimate of systematic uncertainties. For a fictitious neutral $D_s$ meson, we obtain the $ΔQ=2$ $\overline{\text{MS}}$ bag parameter ${\cal B}^{\overline{\text{MS}}}_1(3\,{\rm GeV})=0.7673(123)$, consistent with existing short-distance $D^0$ mixing determinations. For the $ΔQ=0$ lifetime-ratio operator basis, we find the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ results $B^{\overline{\text{MS}}}_1(3\,{\rm GeV})=1.0524(97)$, $B^{\overline{\text{MS}}}_2(3\,{\rm GeV})=0.9621(71)$, $ε^{\overline{\text{MS}}}_1(3\,{\rm GeV})=-0.2275(76)$, and $ε^{\overline{\text{MS}}}_2(3\,{\rm GeV})=-0.0005(8)$. We provide conversion formulae to re-express these results for an arbitrary choice of evanescent operators. These results demonstrate that GF+SFTX can deliver precise determinations of dimension-six four-quark operators and establish a framework for future lattice computations including more complex operator bases, where the challenge of power-divergent mixing is shifted to the continuum and handled in the SFTX.

</details>


### [59] [Hadron Structure from lattice QCD in the context of the Electron-Ion Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28604)
*Constantia Alexandrou*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent lattice QCD advances in hadron structure calculations—especially for the pion, kaon, and nucleon—and highlights their relevance to the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) physics program.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge high-precision lattice QCD results on hadron structure with the experimental goals of the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) and related facilities.

Method: Review and synthesis of state-of-the-art lattice QCD computations, including charges, electromagnetic form factors, Mellin moments, generalized parton distributions (GPDs), and transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (TMDs).

Result: High-precision lattice results now exist for key hadronic observables; GPDs can be accessed either directly or via moment reconstruction; TMDs are becoming accessible through direct lattice methods; pion, kaon, and nucleon structures are increasingly well-constrained.

Conclusion: Lattice QCD has matured into a predictive, quantitative tool for hadron structure, providing essential theoretical input and benchmarks for EIC experiments and advancing our understanding of nonperturbative QCD.

Abstract: Hadron structure calculations using lattice Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) have advanced significantly in recent years. Results for charges, form factors, and lower Mellin moments can be obtained to high precision, generalized parton distributions can now be computed either directly or reconstructed from moments, and transverse-momentum-dependent distributions can be accessed through direct lattice calculations. Together, these quantities provide detailed and complementary insights into the internal structure of hadrons. These theoretical developments are highly relevant to the experimental program of the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) and of other facilities. We review the most pertinent lattice QCD results for hadron structure that inform the EIC scientific agenda, with particular emphasis on the pion, kaon, and nucleon.

</details>


### [60] [Applying the Worldvolume Hybrid Monte Carlo method to lattice gauge theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28629)
*Masafumi Fukuma*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper extends the Worldvolume Hybrid Monte Carlo (WV-HMC) method to group manifolds, enabling its rigorous application to lattice gauge theories while avoiding the sign problem and ergodicity issues of Lefschetz-thimble methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the numerical sign problem and ergodicity limitations in existing approaches (e.g., Lefschetz-thimble), particularly for lattice gauge theories.

Method: Extension of the Worldvolume Hybrid Monte Carlo (WV-HMC) algorithm from flat spaces to compact group manifolds, preserving its geometric and stochastic structure for path integral sampling.

Result: A mathematically consistent generalization of WV-HMC to group manifolds, establishing a foundation for applying it to non-Abelian lattice gauge theories.

Conclusion: The group-manifold extension of WV-HMC provides a viable, ergodicity-resilient framework for tackling the sign problem in lattice field theory simulations.

Abstract: The numerical sign problem remains one of the central challenges in computational physics. The Worldvolume Hybrid Monte Carlo (WV-HMC) method has recently been proposed as a reliable and computationally efficient algorithm that crucially avoids the ergodicity issues inherent in Lefschetz-thimble approaches. In these proceedings, after outlining the key ideas behind WV-HMC, we present its extension to group manifolds. This provides a rigorous framework for applying WV-HMC to lattice gauge theories.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [61] [One-loop Amplitudes: String Methods, Infrared Regularization, and Automation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26892)
*Marcus Berg,Michael Haack,Yonatan Zimmerman*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes one-loop graviton amplitudes in half-maximal supergravity using string-theoretic methods, introduces infrared regularization via kinematic mass scales inspired by SCET, employs analytically continued single-valued polylogarithms as generating functions, obtains IR-finite Feynman integrals for novel tensor structures, and releases code to support automation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute quantum corrections (one-loop graviton amplitudes) in half-maximal supergravity with improved infrared handling and analytic control, leveraging string theory techniques to avoid traditional dimensional regularization limitations.

Method: String-inspired loop amplitude calculation; infrared regularization via higher-point kinematic mass scales (analogous to SCET); use of analytically continued single-valued polylogarithms as generating functions; derivation and evaluation of new tensor Feynman integrals.

Result: IR-finite expressions for half-maximal 4-point one-loop graviton amplitudes; identification and computation of previously unencountered tensor structures; publicly available computational code for reproducibility and automation.

Conclusion: String methods combined with SCET-inspired IR regularization enable clean, finite, and analytically tractable computations of supergravity loop amplitudes, paving the way for systematic automation and extension to higher loops or multiplicity.

Abstract: We calculate field theory loop amplitudes by string methods, applied to half-maximal 4-point one-loop graviton amplitudes. Infrared divergences are regulated similarly to soft-collinear effective field theory (SCET): new mass scales are introduced, here by higher-point kinematics. We use an analytically continued single-valued polylogarithm as generating function. The Feynman integrals for the new tensor structures are infrared finite. We provide code as a step towards automation.

</details>


### [62] [High energy scattering and null strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26910)
*Arjun Bagchi,Sachin Grover,Sharang Rajesh Iyer,Amartya Saha*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs scattering amplitudes for tensionless (null) strings in the induced vacuum using worldsheet symmetry principles, showing they reproduce the ultra-high-energy limit of standard string amplitudes—including Gross-Mende and Regge regimes—and reveal novel features like open/closed string unification and new on-shell vertex operators hinting at non-perturbative physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an intrinsic worldsheet description of the ultra-high-energy regime of string theory, where the string becomes tensionless and the worldsheet acquires null geometry and enhanced Carroll/BMS symmetry, necessitating a reformulation of scattering amplitudes beyond conventional tensile string methods.

Method: Systematic tensionless limit of the worldsheet theory: identification of the induced vacuum as the physically relevant one for high-energy tensile strings; construction of integrated vertex operators via careful limiting procedure from parent string theory; exploitation of 2d conformal Carroll / 3d BMS symmetry; explicit computation of four-point null string amplitudes.

Result: Null string amplitudes in the induced vacuum exactly match the high-energy limit of standard string amplitudes; open and closed string amplitudes become indistinguishable; four-point amplitudes recover both Gross-Mende and Regge limits; a new class of zero-tension-specific vertex operators is identified, yielding same on-shell results but suggesting non-perturbative extensions.

Conclusion: Tensionless null strings governed by Carroll/BMS symmetry provide a consistent, intrinsic worldsheet framework for ultra-high-energy string scattering; the induced vacuum bridges tensile and tensionless regimes; the formalism unifies open/closed strings and hints at physics beyond perturbative string theory.

Abstract: We propose an instrinsic worldsheet description of the ultra-high energy regime of string scattering based on worldsheet symmetries. At very high energies, the fundamental string becomes tensionless and in flat target spacetimes, the worldsheet becomes a null surface. Tensionless null strings thus emerge and the worldsheet symmetries morph from two copies of the Virasoro algebra to the two dimensional (2d) conformal Carroll or equivalently the 3d Bondi-van der Burgh-Metzner-Sachs (BMS) algebra. Tensionless strings have three inequivalent vacua over which they can be constructed, leading to distinct quantum theories. High energy tensile strings naturally connect to null strings built on the so-called induced vacuum.
  Our principle goal in this paper is the construction of scattering amplitudes for null strings in the induced vacuum. We show that these amplitudes, constructed from worldsheet methods of the null string, coincide with the high energy limit of usual string amplitudes. A crucial part of our analysis is the construction of integrated vertex operators. This achieved by relying on lessons from the parent string theory and following the tensionless limit carefully. A striking feature of the null string is the blurring of differences between open and closed strings. We see this at the level of the amplitudes as well. We then focus on four-point amplitudes and recover all expected regimes including the Gross-Mende regime and the Regge limit. We finally comment on a new class of vertex operators which arise naturally only in the zero tension string. This reproduces all our earlier analyses when put onshell but also has hints of signatures beyond the perturbative tensile string.

</details>


### [63] [The connection between a classical vibrating drumhead and the masses of glueballs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26966)
*Thales Azevedo,Henrique Boschi-Filho*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies holographic QCD's hardwall model to predict glueball masses, noting mathematical equivalence between the governing differential equations and those of a vibrating circular membrane—offering pedagogical insight for undergraduates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate glueball properties using holographic QCD techniques, and to leverage the intuitive analogy with classical drumhead vibrations for pedagogical accessibility.

Method: Application of the hardwall model in holographic QCD to derive glueball mass spectra via solving associated radial differential equations; recognition of their isomorphism to Bessel’s equation for circular membrane modes.

Result: Glueball mass predictions are obtained analytically as eigenvalues corresponding to zeros of Bessel functions, mirroring vibrational mode frequencies of a circular membrane.

Conclusion: The hardwall model provides a tractable framework for glueball spectroscopy, and its mathematical correspondence with classical wave mechanics enhances conceptual understanding—particularly for undergraduate learners.

Abstract: The powerful techniques of holographic quantum chromodynamics (QCD) can be employed in the investigation of glueballs -- composite particles made solely of gluons, the strong nuclear force mediators. In particular, the so-called hardwall model yields predictions for the values of the masses of various glueball states, which are related to the solutions of the differential equations of the model. It turns out that those equations are essentially the same as the ones governing the vibrations of a circular membrane like that of a drumhead, which may serve as extra motivation for studying the dynamics of such an object as an undergraduate physics student.

</details>


### [64] [The Lee-Yang model and its generalizations through the lens of long-range deformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27031)
*Fanny Eustachon*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper tests a conjecture linking non-unitary 2D conformal minimal models M(2,2m+1) to scalar field theories with iφ^{2m−1} interaction, using perturbative long-range constructions; it finds inconsistencies for m > 2 but confirms consistency for the Lee-Yang case (m = 2), which parallels the long-range Ising model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test a recent conjecture that non-unitary conformal minimal models M(2,2m+1) emerge as RG fixed points of scalar field theories with complex iφ^{2m−1} interaction, via perturbative long-range realizations.

Method: Perturbative analysis of two distinct long-range constructions: one based directly on the minimal model and another derived from its Landau-Ginzburg potential formalism; comparison of their consistency across m ≥ 2.

Result: Inconsistencies arise between the two constructions for all m > 2; for m = 2 (Lee-Yang model), both constructions agree and the model is shown to be analogous to the long-range Ising model.

Conclusion: The conjecture holds only for m = 2 — the Lee-Yang case — supporting its status as a consistent long-range non-unitary CFT; the generalization to m > 2 fails under perturbative long-range scrutiny.

Abstract: In two dimensions, the non-unitary class of conformal minimal models, $\mathcal{M}(2,2m+1)$, has been recently conjectured to arise as renormalization-group fixed points of scalar field theories with complex $i\varphi^{2m-1}$ interaction, $m\in \mathbb{N}$, $m\ge2$. We test a variation of this conjecture through the perturbative study of two separate long-range constructions based on respectively the minimal model and its potential Landau-Ginzburg formalism. For $m>2$, inconsistencies are found when subsequently relating both constructions. In contrast, the long-range Lee-Yang model, the $m=2$ case, is shown to be analogue to the long-range Ising model.

</details>


### [65] [Universality in Relativistic Spinning Particle Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27353)
*Joon-Hwi Kim,Sangmin Lee*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves equivalence among four massive spinning particle models in 4D coupled to EM/gravity, derives BMT and QMPD equations model-independently, constructs the spherical top's interacting theory with spin gauge invariance, and discusses applications to black holes and PN/PM physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify disparate massive spinning particle models and provide a model-independent, rigorous framework for describing spin-coupled dynamics in EM and gravity, enabling consistent treatment of higher multipoles and applications in strong-field regimes.

Method: Mathematical equivalence analysis using symplectic reduction and constrained Hamiltonian formalism; derivation of effective equations of motion (BMT, QMPD) from first principles; explicit construction of the interacting spherical top model preserving spin gauge invariance.

Result: Demonstrated exact physical equivalence of vector oscillator, spinor oscillator, spherical top, and massive twistor models in both free and interacting sectors; model-independent derivation of BMT and QMPD equations; fully gauge-invariant interacting formulation of the spherical top; extension to arbitrary spin multipole couplings.

Conclusion: A universal, geometric, and gauge-invariant framework for massive spinning particles emerges, resolving long-standing ambiguities in coupling prescriptions and enabling robust applications across gravitational physics—from black hole perturbations to post-Newtonian and post-Minkowskian scattering.

Abstract: We establish an equivalence between massive spinning particle models in four spacetime dimensions coupled to electromagnetism or gravity, within the spin-magnitude-preserving sector. Four representative models in the literature are shown to describe exactly the same physics in their free and interacting theories: vector oscillator, spinor oscillator, spherical top, and massive twistor. The Bargmann-Michel-Telegdi (BMT) and quadrupolar Mathisson-Papapetrou-Dixon (QMPD) equations are derived in a model-independent fashion. This universal framework allows for incorporating higher spin multipole interactions as well. We establish the rigorous construction of the interacting theory of the spherical top model with emphasis on spin gauge invariance. Applications to black hole physics, conserved charges, and post-Newtonian or post-Minkowskian frameworks are discussed.

</details>


### [66] [One-loop finiteness in higher-derivative $6D$, ${\cal N}=(1,0)$ super Yang-Mills -- hypermultiplet system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27567)
*I. L. Buchbinder,A. S. Budekhina,E. A. Ivanov,K. V. Stepanyantz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a six-dimensional N=(1,0) supersymmetric gauge theory with higher derivatives and an adjoint hypermultiplet, achieving off-shell one-loop finiteness in the gauge superfield sector via a novel non-minimal interaction, while preserving gauge invariance and supersymmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve one-loop divergences in the gauge superfield sector of conventional six-dimensional N=(1,0) supersymmetric gauge theories with higher derivatives and adjoint hypermultiplets.

Method: Harmonic superspace formalism; introduction of a novel non-minimal gauge-hypermultiplet interaction; verification via background field method and supergraph techniques.

Result: Complete cancellation of one-loop divergences in the gauge superfield sector; resulting theory is off-shell one-loop finite in that sector, gauge-invariant, and N=(1,0) supersymmetric.

Conclusion: A concrete example of a higher-derivative, six-dimensional N=(1,0) supersymmetric gauge theory finite in the vector multiplet sector is constructed, demonstrating that finiteness can be achieved without sacrificing fundamental symmetries.

Abstract: We employ the harmonic superspace methods to study a six-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=(1,0)$ supersymmetric gauge theory with higher derivatives coupled to a hypermultiplet in the adjoint representation. By introducing a novel non-minimal interaction between the gauge multiplet and the hypermultiplet, we demonstrate that the one-loop divergences in gauge superfield sector, which are present in the conventional formulation, are canceled. The resulting theory is off-shell one-loop finite in this sector, while preserving the gauge invariance and $\mathcal{N}=(1,0)$ supersymmetry. The cancelation mechanism is explicitly verified using both the background field method and the supergraph techniques. Thus, we present an example of the higher-derivative supersymmetric gauge theory in six dimensions which is finite in the vector multiplet sector.

</details>


### [67] [Higher descent equations based on 2-term $L_{\infty}$ algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27588)
*Mengyao Wu,Danhua Song,Jie Yang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs higher Chern-Simons-type characteristic classes from invariant polynomials on 2-term $L_\infty$ algebras and shows they satisfy higher descent equations, unifying the higher Chern-Weil theorem and higher gauge anomalies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formalize and unify the geometric and algebraic structures underlying higher gauge theories, particularly the characterization of anomalies and characteristic classes in a homotopy-coherent setting.

Method: Uses 2-term $L_\infty$ algebras as the algebraic framework; constructs characteristic classes via multilinear symmetric invariant polynomials; derives and verifies the higher descent equations satisfied by these classes.

Result: A family of higher Chern-Simons-type characteristic classes is explicitly constructed and proven to satisfy the higher descent equations; the construction simultaneously encodes the higher Chern-Weil theorem and higher gauge anomalies.

Conclusion: The higher descent equations provide a consistent cohomological framework for characteristic classes and anomalies in higher gauge theory, grounded rigorously in $L_\infty$ algebraic structure.

Abstract: In this paper, we develop the higher descent equations for higher gauge theories within the framework of 2-term $L_{\infty}$ algebras. Starting from a multilinear symmetric invariant polynomial, we construct a family of higher Chern-Simons type characteristic classes and verify that they satisfy the higher descent equations. These polynomials encode both the higher Chern-Weil theorem and the higher gauge anomalies.

</details>


### [68] [Instability in ${\cal N}=4$ supersymmetric Yang-Mills theory on $S^3$ at finite density](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27821)
*Alex Buchel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates dynamical and thermodynamic instabilities in strongly coupled N=4 SYM plasma on R^3 and S^3, showing curvature of S^3 differentially affects transport vs. thermodynamic stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how spatial curvature (R^3 vs. S^3) modifies the onset and interplay of dynamical (R-charge transport) and thermodynamic instabilities in strongly coupled N=4 SYM plasma with equal R-charge chemical potentials.

Method: Analysis of equilibrium states and linearized hydrodynamic/transport modes in strongly coupled N=4 SYM plasma using holographic duality (AdS/CFT), comparing flat space (R^3) and curved space (S^3) backgrounds; examination of stability criteria via dispersion relations and thermodynamic susceptibilities.

Result: On S^3, increasing curvature suppresses dynamical instability (stabilizes R-charge transport) at low temperatures while thermodynamic instability persists; full thermodynamic stability is restored only at sufficiently large curvature.

Conclusion: Spatial curvature decouples dynamical and thermodynamic instability thresholds in charged N=4 SYM plasma, demonstrating that transport stability does not necessarily imply thermodynamic stability—and vice versa—in curved holographic backgrounds.

Abstract: Homogeneous and isotropic equilibrium states of strongly coupled ${\cal N}=4$ supersymmetric Yang-Mills charged plasma in ${\mathbb R}^3$ with equal chemical potentials for the maximal Abelian subgroup of the $R$-symmetry group become dynamically unstable below some critical temperature. The instabilities arise in the $R$-symmetry charge transport, precisely when the equilibrium state becomes thermodynamically unstable. We study the fate these correlated instabilities when the theory is place on $S^3$. The curvature of the three-sphere affects the onset of the dynamical and the thermodynamic instabilities differently: increasing the curvature at low temperatures can stabilize its transport, but leave the plasma thermodynamically unstable. Thermodynamic stability is eventually recovered at large curvatures.

</details>


### [69] [Emergent strings, holography, and cosmology from four-fermion interactions: a bottom-up derivation of AdS/CFT, dS/CFT, and $w_{1+\infty}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27824)
*Laith H. Haddad*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives holographic duality (AdS/CFT) from first principles using fermionic quantum field theories—specifically the Gross-Neveu and NJL models—showing that extra dimensions and gravitational physics emerge from condensate competition, not geometric input.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish holographic duality without assuming pre-existing geometry, strings, or AdS space—i.e., to derive AdS/CFT as an emergent phenomenon from strongly coupled QFT dynamics.

Method: Uses large-N analysis of (1+1)d Gross-Neveu and higher-dimensional NJL models; introduces a Bargmann-Wigner scheme to map chiral ($\Delta_0$) and spin-1 pairing ($\Delta_1$) condensates to an emergent radial coordinate $z$, whose fluctuations generate the AdS metric; applies analytic continuation for dS/CFT; imposes consistency constraints to identify emergent string theory data.

Result: Derives full AdS$_3$/CFT$_2$ correspondence—including Virasoro central charge $c=2N^2$, BTZ black holes with Planck-scale horizon quantisation, D1-branes, T-duality, Hagedorn/BKT transition, and Strominger dS/CFT via analytic continuation; identifies emergent Type IIB string on AdS$_3\times S^3\times \mathcal{M}_4$ with $\mathcal{N}=(1,1)$ worldsheet SUSY; extends to AdS$_4$/CFT$_3$ and AdS$_5$/CFT$_4$, recovering linear Regge trajectories and chiral symmetry breaking in holographic QCD.

Conclusion: Holography is not fundamental but emergent: the extra dimension arises intrinsically from competition between fermionic condensates in purely field-theoretic models; this framework unifies AdS/CFT, dS/CFT, and phenomenological QCD from simple four-fermion interactions.

Abstract: We derive holographic duality from first principles starting from the $(1+1)$-dimensional Gross-Neveu (GN) model with $N$ fermion species and a local quartic interaction, without assuming any string or geometric input. Using a Bargmann-Wigner scheme, the competition between chiral condensation $Δ_0=\langle\barψψ\rangle$ and spin-1 pairing $Δ_1=\langle\barΦ_1Φ_1\rangle$ defines an emergent radial coordinate $z=m^{-1}(Δ_1/Δ_0^2-1)^{1/2}$; local fluctuations of this ratio, tracked by a comoving derivative, generate the AdS$_3$ line element via the enhanced large-$N$ species dispersion; the condensate competition \emph{is} the extra dimension. From this single mechanism the complete AdS$_3$/CFT$_2$ correspondence emerges: Newton's constant, the Virasoro algebra ($c=2N^2$), D1-branes with open strings, open/closed T-duality, the Hagedorn/BKT transition, and the BTZ black hole whose horizon circumference is quantised in Planck units by individual vortex nucleation events. Analytic continuation $z\to iζ$ across the chiral critical point realises the Strominger dS/CFT conjecture microscopically. Six constraints identify the emergent string as Type IIB on AdS$_3\times S^3\times\mathcal{M}_4$, with emergent worldsheet $\mathcal{N}=(1,1)$ supersymmetry, NS/R spectral flow, and GSO projection. Extension to the $(2+1)$d NJL model yields AdS$_4$/CFT$_3$, a dS$_4$/CFT$_3$ realisation, and a structural identification of the $w_{1+\infty}$ celestial algebra. Extension to the $(3+1)$d NJL model yields AdS$_5$/CFT$_4$ and holographic QCD with chiral symmetry breaking and linear Regge trajectories $M_s^2=4(s+1)Λ_\mathrm{QCD}^2$, capturing the correct QCD infrared physics from a four-fermion interaction.

</details>


### [70] [On the trivalent junction of three non-tachyonic heterotic string theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28133)
*Yuji Tachikawa*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a 9D junction where three non-tachyonic 10D heterotic string theories—E8×E8, SO(32), and non-supersymmetric SO(16)×SO(16)—meet, realized via a 2D non-conformal N=(0,1) QFT with three asymptotic worldsheet CFT ends; it generalizes to Z₂-symmetric theories, their orbifolds, and twisted orbifolds with a spin invertible theory q.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend recent work on string theory junctions by realizing a consistent 9-dimensional junction of three distinct, tachyon-free 10D heterotic string theories—two supersymmetric and one non-supersymmetric—thereby probing the web of string vacua and non-perturbative connections between seemingly disparate theories.

Method: Constructs a two-dimensional non-conformal N=(0,1) supersymmetric quantum field theory with three asymptotic regions, each supporting the worldsheet CFT of one heterotic theory: E8×E8, SO(32), and SO(16)×SO(16). The construction is embedded in a broader framework involving a Z₂-symmetric CFT T, its Z₂-orbifold T/Z₂, and a modified orbifold (T×q)/Z₂ where q is a Z₂-symmetric spin invertible topological theory.

Result: Demonstrates the existence of a consistent 9D junction joining the three 10D heterotic string theories; establishes that the junction is realized by a well-defined 2D N=(0,1) QFT whose asymptotics encode the respective worldsheet theories; provides a unifying categorical/structural interpretation via Z₂ orbifold relationships and invertible topological couplings.

Conclusion: The three non-tachyonic 10D heterotic string theories can be consistently joined at a 9D junction, revealing a deeper structural unity among them through a 2D boundary QFT; this supports the idea that seemingly distinct string vacua are connected via higher-codimension defects governed by constrained supersymmetric QFTs and generalized orbifold constructions.

Abstract: Recently, Altavista, Anastasi, Angius and Uranga discussed a method to construct junctions and bouquets of different perturbative string theories. Following this analysis, we here argue that three non-tachyonic ten-dimensional heterotic string theories can be joined together at a nine-dimensional junction.
  This is done by creating a two-dimensional non-conformal $\mathcal{N}{=}(0,1)$ supersymmetric quantum field theory with three asymptotic ends, each equipped with one of the worldsheet theories of the supersymmetric $E_8\times E_8$ theory, the supersymmetric $SO(32)$ theory, and the non-supersymmetric $SO(16)\times SO(16)$ theory, respectively. It is actually a special case of a more general construction involving an arbitrary $\mathbb{Z}_2$-symmetric theory $T$, its $\mathbb{Z}_2$-orbifold $T/\mathbb{Z}_2$, and the modified $\mathbb{Z}_2$-orbifold $(T\times q)/\mathbb{Z}_2$, where $q$ is a certain $\mathbb{Z}_2$-symmetric spin invertible theory.

</details>


### [71] [Universal Modular Properties of Generalized Gibbs Ensembles and Chiral Deformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28244)
*Sujay K. Ashok,Tanmoy Sengupta,Adarsh Sudhakar,Gérard M. T. Watts*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes an asymptotic formula for the modular S-transform of a generalized partition function in CFTs perturbed by holomorphic fields, using Zhu recursion and OPE data; it proves and generalizes a conjecture on modular properties of generalized Gibbs ensembles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand modular transformation properties of conformal field theories (CFTs) perturbed by holomorphic fields—particularly in the context of generalized Gibbs ensembles—and to rigorously derive asymptotic behavior under modular S-transformation.

Method: Uses torus correlation function properties, especially the Zhu recursion relation, to derive an asymptotic expansion of the modular S-transform of a generalized partition function incorporating zero modes of higher-spin holomorphic currents.

Result: Derives a universal asymptotic form for the modular S-transform, determined iteratively by second-order pole coefficients in the OPEs of holomorphic currents.

Conclusion: The work proves and generalizes a conjecture on the modular transformation of generalized Gibbs ensembles in holomorphically perturbed CFTs, establishing universality governed by OPE data.

Abstract: We study modular properties of conformal field theories perturbed by holomorphic fields. We prove an asymptotic formula for the modular S-transform of a generalized partition function that includes zero modes of higher spin holomorphic currents. The derivation makes use of general properties of torus correlation functions, in particular the Zhu recursion relation. The asymptotic expansion of the modular transformed partition function takes a universal form that is determined iteratively by the second order pole coefficients in the operator product expansion of the holomorphic currents. This proves and generalizes a conjecture regarding the modular transformation properties of generalized Gibbs ensembles.

</details>


### [72] [A Twisted Origin for Magnetic Carroll Supersymmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28269)
*Ilayda Bulunur,Osman Ergec,Oguzhan Kasikci,Mehmet Ozkan,Mustafa Salih Zog*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a 3D N=2 magnetic Carroll supersymmetry algebra and its vector-multiplet action, showing it originates from a twisted relativistic parent—not naive contraction—and its conformal extension matches the global super-BMS₄ algebra, supporting magnetic Carroll theories in flat-space holography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To embed magnetic Carrollian field theories—candidate flat-space holographic duals—into a top-down, supersymmetric framework by identifying their relativistic origin, especially since naive contraction fails to yield the correct magnetic Carroll supersymmetry.

Method: Algebraic contraction analysis, twisted relativistic algebra embedding, explicit construction of the 3D N=2 magnetic Carroll superalgebra and its supersymmetric vector-multiplet action, and identification of its conformal extension with the global part of super-BMS₄.

Result: The magnetic Carroll superalgebra features: (i) one supercharge squaring to spatial momentum, (ii) a mixed anticommutator yielding the Hamiltonian, (iii) a nilpotent second supercharge; its conformal extension is shown to be identical to the global super-BMS₄ algebra.

Conclusion: Magnetic Carroll supersymmetry has a consistent relativistic origin via twisting—not naive contraction—and its conformal extension provides a physical realization of super-BMS₄, reinforcing magnetic Carroll theories as viable candidates for flat-space holography and supersymmetric asymptotic symmetry descriptions.

Abstract: Magnetic Carrollian theories provide a natural setting for field theories with nontrivial spatial structure in the Carroll limit and are therefore natural candidates for flat-space holographic duals. Embedding such boundary theories into a top-down framework requires a consistent supersymmetric completion and, in particular, an understanding of the relativistic origin of magnetic Carroll supersymmetry. We show that the relevant magnetic Carroll algebra does not arise from a naive contraction of the standard relativistic supersymmetry algebra, but instead descends from a twisted relativistic parent. As an explicit realization, we construct a three-dimensional ${\mathcal{N}}=2$ magnetic Carroll algebra together with a supersymmetric vector-multiplet action. Unlike the electric case, the resulting structure contains one supercharge that squares to spatial momentum, a mixed anticommutator that yields the Hamiltonian, and a nilpotent second supercharge. We further show that its conformal extension coincides with the global part of a supersymmetric BMS$_4$ algebra. This provides a physical and relativistic origin for a super-BMS$_4$ structure recently identified by complementary algebraic methods, and strengthens the case for magnetic Carroll theories in flat-space holography and supersymmetric asymptotic symmetries.

</details>


### [73] [Oscillons in the broken vacuum and global vortex annihilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28298)
*D. Canillas Martínez,A. González-Parra,D. Miguélez-Caballero,A. Wereszczynski*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies a stable, long-lived oscillon in the complex φ⁶ theory during vortex-antivortex collisions—unlike the φ⁴ model—and attributes its existence to far-distance potential modification introducing an unbroken vacuum, despite absence of a mass gap from the flat direction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand why vortex-antivortex collisions in the complex φ⁶ theory exhibit resonant behavior—unseen in the φ⁴ model—despite lacking a mass gap associated with the flat direction in the broken vacuum.

Method: Analytical and numerical investigation of vortex-antivortex scattering dynamics in the complex φ⁶ scalar field theory, focusing on the emergence, stability, and origin of a large-amplitude oscillon in the broken vacuum.

Result: A remarkably stable, long-lived, large-amplitude oscillon is found in the broken vacuum of the φ⁶ theory; its persistence is shown to stem not from a mass gap but from a far-distance modification of the potential that introduces an unbroken (true or false) vacuum.

Conclusion: The resonant structure in vortex-antivortex collisions in the φ⁶ theory arises from a topologically nontrivial oscillon stabilized by asymptotic potential structure—specifically, the presence of an unbroken vacuum at large distances—challenging conventional mass-gap-based oscillon formation paradigms.

Abstract: In contrast to the complex $φ^4$ model, vortex-antivortex collisions in the complex $φ^6$ theory reveal a resonant structure due to the existence of a remarkably stable, long-lived, large amplitude oscillon in the broken vacuum. Surprisingly, it persists despite the absence of a mass gap associated with the flat direction in the broken vacuum. We demonstrate that its existence is related to a far-distance modification of the potential, namely, the appearance of an unbroken (false or true) vacuum.

</details>


### [74] [Physics as Code: From Scans to Theorems with ITP APIs in $SU(5)$ Model Building](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28406)
*Sven Krippendorf,Joseph Tooby-Smith*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces an API-based methodology in Lean (an interactive theorem prover) to formally verify classifications of bounded charge spectra in SU(5) F-theory models—replacing ad hoc scans with reusable, theorem-backed reduction theorems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of exhaustive and statistical model scans in theoretical physics by enabling globally reliable, formally verified classifications via interactive theorem proving.

Method: Formalizing charge spectra, physical predicates (e.g., top-Yukawa coupling, absence of dangerous operators), and reduction moves as reusable Lean definitions; deriving classification via proved reduction theorems rather than enumeration.

Result: A formally verified classification: every viable bounded charge spectrum satisfying the constraints arises from finitely many minimal top-Yukawa witnesses plus certified completions and closure steps.

Conclusion: The approach establishes correctness-first, reusable infrastructure (integrated into PhysLib) for combinatorially hard model-building problems, enabling certified inputs for downstream physics analyses.

Abstract: A recurring challenge in theoretical physics is to make reliable global statements about bounded but combinatorially large model spaces. Exhaustive scans quickly become opaque or impractical, while statistical exploration does not by itself provide theorem-backed guarantees. This motivates workflows in which the model-building problem itself is formalized inside an interactive theorem prover (ITP).
  In this paper we develop an API-based methodology for formalizing such bounded model-building questions inside Lean, an interactive theorem prover. The central step is to represent the relevant charge spectra, predicates, and reduction moves as reusable ITP definitions, and then to derive the classification from proved reduction theorems rather than from an ad hoc scan. We demonstrate the strategy in a concrete $SU(5)$ case study motivated by F-theory model building with additional Abelian symmetries.
  At the charge-spectrum layer, we classify bounded spectra that admit a top-quark Yukawa coupling, avoid a selected set of dangerous operators, and satisfy a minimal charge-spectrum completeness condition. Our main result shows that every such spectrum in the bounded search space arises from finitely many minimal top-Yukawa witnesses together with controlled completions and certified closure steps. This classification represents a formally verified description of the full viable class in the charge-spectrum setting studied here.
  The development is implemented inside PhysLib as reusable infrastructure rather than as a one-off verification script. It provides a proof of principle for how interactive theorem provers can turn combinatorially difficult model-building problems into correctness-first, reusable workflows, and we discuss how the resulting certified classification can serve as reliable input for downstream analyses.

</details>


### [75] [Lecture Notes on Positivity Properties of Scattering Amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28454)
*Prashanth Raman*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reviews completely monotone (CM) and Stieltjes functions—classes defined by infinite positivity constraints—and explores their emergence in quantum field theory (QFT), especially in Euclidean Feynman integrals and Coulomb branch amplitudes in N=4 SYM; it connects their mathematical structure to physical principles (unitarity, analyticity, parametric representations) and discusses applications in S-matrix constraints, numerical bootstrap, and positive geometry, including links to geometric volume interpretations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematize and elucidate the recent appearance of completely monotone and Stieltjes functions—classical analytic objects—in foundational QFT observables, and to uncover their physical origins and implications for modern amplitude methods, positivity-based bootstraps, and positive geometry.

Method: Review and synthesis: mathematical characterization of CM and Stieltjes functions; analysis of their emergence via unitarity, analyticity, and parametric representations of Feynman integrals; survey of applications across S-matrix theory, numerical bootstrap, and positive geometry—including geometric volume conjectures.
