Result: Establishes that CM and Stieltjes properties are not accidental but arise generically from fundamental QFT principles; provides conceptual bridges between analytic function theory, scattering amplitudes, and positive geometry; offers evidence linking these functions to geometric volume interpretations in positive geometries.

Conclusion: Completely monotone and Stieltjes functions serve as unifying analytic signatures encoding deep physical constraints (e.g., unitarity, locality) in QFT; their systematic study enriches both rigorous amplitude methods and the geometric understanding of scattering processes and cosmological correlators.

Abstract: We review completely monotone (CM) and Stieltjes functions, which are classes of functions obeying an infinite hierarchy of positivity constraints. While these are classical concepts in analysis, such properties have recently been shown to arise in many fundamental building blocks and observables of quantum field theory (QFT), including scalar Feynman integrals in the Euclidean region and Coulomb branch amplitudes in $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM.
  After reviewing their mathematical structure, we discuss the physical and geometric origins of these properties, ranging from unitarity and analyticity in scattering amplitudes to the structure of parametric representations of Feynman integrals. We then survey a number of applications, including constraints on the analytic S-matrix, implications for numerical bootstrap methods, and connections to positive geometry, where we present evidence for a close relation between these functions and geometric volume interpretations.
  These notes are based on an extended series of lectures delivered at the \emph{Positive Geometry in Scattering Amplitudes and Cosmological Correlators} workshop, held at the International Centre for Theoretical Sciences (ICTS), Bengaluru, in February 2025.

</details>


### [76] [Supersymmetry and Attractors in N = 4 Supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28495)
*Abhinava Bhattacharjee,Bindusar Sahoo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper numerically demonstrates the attractor mechanism for extremal, spherically symmetric black holes in pure N = 4 Poincaré supergravity and shows that generic dyonic solutions with $p^2q^2 > (p \cdot q)^2$ preserve exactly 1/4 of the supersymmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how scalar moduli are fixed at the horizon of extremal black holes (the attractor mechanism) and to classify the residual supersymmetry preserved by such solutions in pure N = 4 supergravity.

Method: Numerical analysis of extremal, spherically symmetric black hole solutions; analytical study of Killing spinor equations for constant moduli configurations; use of charge invariants ($p^2$, $q^2$, $p \\cdot q$) to characterize supersymmetry preservation.

Result: The attractor mechanism is confirmed numerically for extremal black holes in pure N = 4 Poincaré supergravity; constant-moduli solutions with generic dyonic charges satisfying $p^2q^2 > (p \\cdot q)^2$ preserve precisely 1/4 of the 16 supercharges.

Conclusion: The attractor behavior holds in this minimal N = 4 setting, and the supersymmetry fraction is universally 1/4 for generic dyonic charges—establishing a clean link between charge geometry and supersymmetry breaking pattern.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the attractor mechanism for extremal, spherically symmetric black holes in pure N = 4 Poincaré supergravity, which we demonstrate numerically. We further study the supersymmetries preserved by these solutions by focussing specifically on the constant moduli solutions and show that, for a generic dyonic charge configuration satisfying $p^2q^2>(p.q)^2$, they always preserve 1/4th of the total supersymmetries.

</details>


### [77] [Which Functions Admit a Positive Geometry? From Branch Cuts to String Amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28543)
*Hyungrok Kim,Jonah Stalknecht*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends positive geometry to infinite unions of line segments and continuum limits, enabling canonical forms with branch cuts; introduces 'pseudogenus' to classify meromorphic functions, showing canonical forms are dlog of pseudogenus-zero functions; constrains physical spectra (e.g., string/KK towers) compatible with such geometries; and provides a geometric interpretation of the KLT double copy via dlog forms of open/closed string amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize positive geometry beyond rational canonical forms—particularly to accommodate branch cuts and non-rational observables arising in string theory and Kaluza-Klein scenarios—while maintaining geometric interpretability of scattering amplitudes.

Method: Analyzes one-dimensional positive geometries as infinite unions of line segments and their continuum limits; introduces pseudogenus as a topological invariant for meromorphic functions on ℂℙ¹; derives necessary conditions for a function to arise as a canonical form; applies these to string and KK spectral constraints; constructs dlog representations for open/closed string amplitudes and identifies their associated positive geometries.

Result: Canonical forms of one-dimensional positive geometries are precisely dlog f where f has pseudogenus zero; branch-cut-containing forms emerge naturally in continuum limits; stringent constraints arise on viable infinite-state spectra (e.g., only sparse contributions allowed for stringy/KK towers); four-point KLT double copy is geometrically realized via intersection of open-string and closed-string dlog geometries.

Conclusion: The framework unifies rational and non-rational amplitudes under positive geometry, reveals deep connections between analytic structure (branch cuts), topology (pseudogenus), and physics (spectral sparsity), and provides a geometric foundation for the double copy—establishing positive geometry as a robust language for quantum scattering beyond perturbative QFT.

Abstract: Positive geometry provides a geometric framework where physical observables are encoded as canonical forms associated to regions of kinematic space. In this paper we consider a generalisation to an infinite union of line segments, which allows us to capture canonical forms beyond rational functions. In the continuum limit of positive geometries, we show that we can generalise even further and describe positive geometries whose canonical forms contain branch cuts. We will constrain which functions can be obtained as the canonical form of one-dimensional positive geometries. We introduce the notion of the pseudogenus to classify meromorphic functions, and show that canonical forms can be written as the $\mathrm d\log$ of a function with pseudogenus zero. Furthermore, we argue that the spectrum encoded by a union of line segments is consistent with the presence of a stringy tower of states or a Kaluza-Klein tower with three or more compact directions only if nearly all such states do not contribute to the scattering amplitude. In addition, we show how the d log of both open and closed string amplitudes admits a positive geometry. This allows us to give a fully geometric interpretation for the KLT double copy at four points.

</details>


### [78] [Symmetry Resolved Entanglement Entropy: Equipartition under Driven and Non-unitary Evolution in a Compact Boson CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28567)
*Filiberto Ares,Jayashish Das,Arnab Kundu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates symmetry-resolved entanglement entropy dynamics in bulk-driven Floquet 2D CFTs, specifically the free compact boson. It identifies an sl^(k)(2,R) subalgebra of Virasoro that introduces a tunable parameter k controlling equipartition breakdown among charge sectors, attributed to low–high frequency mode coupling; the mechanism is argued to extend beyond the free boson, and non-unitary boundary dynamics under post-selected weak measurements are also discussed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how symmetry-resolved entanglement entropy evolves in periodically driven (Floquet) conformal field theories, particularly whether charge sectors remain equally populated (equipartition) and what mechanisms govern its breakdown.

Method: Analytical study using representation theory of the Virasoro algebra, focusing on the existence and implications of an sl^(k)(2,R) subalgebra in the context of the two-dimensional free compact boson CFT; employs oscillator realization of Virasoro and extends reasoning to generic CFTs; considers real-time dynamics of boundary states under non-unitary evolution induced by post-selected weak measurements.

Result: The sl^(k)(2,R) subalgebra introduces a continuous parameter k that tunes the degree of equipartition violation in symmetry-resolved Rényi entropies; this violation arises from explicit low–high frequency mode coupling; the mechanism is conjectured to be generic beyond free bosons; non-unitary evolution modifies fine-grained symmetry-resolved entropy dynamics of boundary states.

Conclusion: Equipartition in symmetry-resolved entanglement is not robust under bulk Floquet driving in CFTs; its breakdown is governed by algebraic structure (sl^(k)(2,R)) and spectral coupling, offering a controllable probe of quantum dynamics and symmetry resolution in driven systems; non-unitary protocols further enrich the phenomenology of symmetry-resolved entanglement.

Abstract: We study the evolution of symmetry-resolved entanglement entropy in bulk-driven Floquet conformal field theories (CFTs). Focusing on the two-dimensional free compact boson CFT, we analyze how symmetry-resolved Rényi entropies approach or depart from equipartition among charge sectors. We show that the existence of an $\mathfrak{sl}^{(k)}(2,\mathbb{R})$ subalgebra of the Virasoro algebra introduces a free parameter, the label $k$, which allows us to control the breakdown of equipartition. We argue that this effect originates from an explicit coupling between low- and high-frequency modes. Based on a general oscillator representation of the Virasoro algebra, we expect this mechanism to persist beyond the free boson CFT. Finally, we discuss how the real-time dynamics of fine-grained symmetry-resolved entropies of a boundary state are modified under non-unitary evolution, which can be associated with post-selected weak measurements.

</details>


### [79] [3D gravity and double copy theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28639)
*Maor Ben-Shahar,Francesco Bonechi,Maxim Zabzine*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates 3D gravity using divergenceless vector frames, establishing on-shell equivalence with standard 3D gravity and clarifying the geometric meaning of the double copy via Chern-Simons theory; it connects the formulation to a Chern-Simons-like action, suggests a higher-dimensional origin, and extends it to $AdS_3$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometrically transparent reformulation of 3D gravity that elucidates the double-copy structure—previously observed in Chern-Simons theory—and to explore its connections to gauge-theoretic and holographic frameworks.

Method: Constructs a new formulation of 3D gravity based on divergenceless vector frames; establishes on-shell equivalence with Einstein gravity; identifies a Chern-Simons-like action; analyzes geometric and algebraic structure; proposes dimensional uplift and extensions to $AdS_3$.

Result: A fully equivalent, frame-based reformulation of 3D gravity is obtained; the double copy acquires a clear geometric interpretation in terms of constrained vector fields; the theory is recast as a Chern-Simons-like theory; plausible higher-dimensional embeddings and $AdS_3$ generalizations are identified.

Conclusion: The divergenceless vector frame formulation provides a novel, geometrically intuitive realization of 3D gravity that unifies double-copy insights with Chern-Simons structure, offering new avenues for understanding holography, higher-dimensional embeddings, and asymptotically AdS spacetimes.

Abstract: We introduce a novel reformulation of three-dimensional gravity in terms of divergenceless vector frames, inspired by the double copy for Chern-Simons theory. This formulation is on-shell equivalent to conventional 3D gravity and provides a transparent geometric interpretation of the double-copy construction. We relate the resulting theory to a Chern-Simons-like action, propose a higher-dimensional origin, and explore extensions that give rise to $AdS_3$

</details>


### [80] [Scattering in strong field QED in a non-null background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28665)
*Patrick Copinger,James P. Edwards,Karthik Rajeev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs master formulae for N-photon scattering amplitudes in non-null electromagnetic backgrounds (A_μ(n·x) with n² ≠ 0) using the first-quantised worldline formalism, enabling systematic expansion in n² and partial resummation; validated against known low-order results and an exactly solvable crossed-field case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond constant or null (plane-wave) background approximations and incorporate more realistic laser-plasma dispersive effects by treating non-null electromagnetic backgrounds—where the wave vector satisfies n² ≠ 0—as a physically relevant generalisation.

Method: Employs the first-quantised worldline formalism to derive non-perturbative worldline representations of propagators (coordinate/momentum space), LSZ-truncated amplitudes, and the effective action in a non-null background A_μ(n·x); constructs master formulae for N-photon amplitudes as power series in the non-null parameter n², and performs partial resummation of this expansion.

Result: Derives explicit master formulae for tree-level and one-loop N-photon amplitudes in scalar and spinor QED in non-null backgrounds; verifies them via consistency with known wavefunction and non-linear Compton scattering results at leading order in n², and tests against an exactly solvable non-null constant crossed field case (no photon insertion).

Conclusion: The worldline-based master formulae provide a systematic, non-perturbative-in-the-background framework for computing multi-photon processes in physically motivated non-null electromagnetic fields, extending prior null/constant-background results and enabling controlled expansions and resummations in n².

Abstract: We examine scattering amplitudes for an arbitrary number of photons in a class of non-null background electromagnetic fields, studying tree-level and one-loop amplitudes in scalar and spinor quantum-electrodynamics in backgrounds defined by a gauge field $A_μ(\mathfrak{n}\cdot x)$ for $\mathfrak{n}^2\neq 0$. Motivated to account for more physically realistic laser-plasma dispersive properties, our approach overcomes prior work studying such amplitudes in a constant background field and relaxes the familiar null criterion assumed for plane waves.
  Master Formulae for the $N$-photon amplitudes dressed by the non-null background are constructed using the first-quantised worldline formalism, which can systematically account for all orders in the non-null parameter, $\mathfrak{n}^2$, treated here as an expansion parameter. These are derived from worldline representations of the coordinate and momentum space propagators (and their LSZ-truncated amplitudes) and the effective action, each incorporating the non-null background non-perturbatively. We then outline a partial resummation of their expansions in $\mathfrak{n}^{2}$. A special exactly solvable case of non-null constant crossed fields without photon insertion in the effective action is explored to test the Master Formulae that result. The validity of the presented master formulae is further checked against known expressions for the wavefunction and non-linear Compton scattering in a non-null background to lowest order in the non-null parameter.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [81] [Universal Geometric Scaling in Cosmic Ray Spallation: Evidence of a Dynamical Causal Horizon from AMS-02](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26824)
*Yi Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper resolves the anomaly of energy-independent plateaus in secondary cosmic-ray ratios (e.g., Li/B, Be/B, Li/Be) at high rigidity (>30 GV) by proposing a geometric thermal framework rooted in ultra-relativistic spallation dynamics, predicting an Unruh temperature (~5.6–5.8 MeV) consistent with nuclear liquid-gas phase transition and validated via Be/B calibration and blind lithium-ratio tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the unexpected energy independence of secondary-to-secondary cosmic-ray flux ratios (Li/B, Be/B, Li/Be) observed by AMS-02 above 30 GV—contradicting standard kinematic fragmentation models that predict energy-dependent evolution.

Method: Develops a macroscopic geometric framework where ultra-relativistic spallation induces strong deceleration of nuclear remnants via snapped QCD flux tubes; estimates an effective Unruh temperature using semi-microscopic modeling (Woods-Saxon potential + pion exchange); calibrates the asymptotic scale from the Be/B ratio; performs blind predictive tests on Li/B and Li/Be.

Result: Extracted asymptotic thermal scale: 6.08 MeV from Be/B—within ~1% of theoretical Unruh estimate (5.6–5.8 MeV) and matches nuclear liquid-gas critical temperature; Li/B and Li/Be ratios confirm universal zero-slope convergence, validating the geometric thermal bath model.

Conclusion: At high rigidity, cosmic-ray secondary production is governed not by detailed kinematics but by a universal, constant-temperature geometric thermal bath arising from relativistic acceleration effects in spallation—redefining interpretation of high-precision cosmic-ray data and linking astroparticle physics to nuclear thermodynamics.

Abstract: The interpretation of high-precision cosmic ray spectra is fundamentally bottlenecked by uncertainties in fragmentation cross-sections. Traditional kinematic models, driven by phase-space expansions, typically predict complex, energy-dependent evolutions. However, AMS-02 measurements reveal that at high rigidities ($R > 30$~GV), secondary-to-secondary flux ratios (Li/B, Be/B, and Li/Be) strictly converge to energy-independent plateaus. To understand this anomaly, we explore a macroscopic geometric framework. The ultra-relativistic spallation of a target nucleus snaps residual strong-interaction flux tubes, inducing an extreme deceleration on the remnant. Using a semi-microscopic estimation based on the Woods-Saxon potential and pion exchange, we suggest this dynamically generates a causal horizon with an effective Unruh temperature $T_U \approx 5.6-5.8$~MeV. Utilizing the Be/B ratio as an absolute calibration channel, we extract an asymptotic scale of $6.08$~MeV, remarkably consistent with our theoretical estimation and the established nuclear liquid-gas phase transition limit. Subsequent blind tests on Lithium ratios demonstrate a universal zero-slope convergence, providing compelling evidence that a constant geometric thermal bath effectively supersedes complex microscopic kinematics at high energies.

</details>


### [82] [Global Structure, Non-Invertible PQ Symmetry, and the DFSZ Domain Wall Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26867)
*Gongjun Choi,Sungwoo Hong,Seth Koren*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper re-examines the DFSZ axion model, emphasizing how global (non-simply-connected) structure of symmetry groups—e.g., (G_EW × U(1)_PQ)/ℤ₂ and (SU(3)_C × G_F)/ℤ₃—affects the domain wall problem; it shows domain wall stability arises from non-invertible chiral symmetries, proposes UV resolutions via small-instanton breaking of such symmetries ('Non-invertible Naturalness'), and discusses associated gravitational wave signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the domain wall problem in the DFSZ axion model by incorporating previously neglected global (topological) structure of symmetry groups, which impacts phenomenology and theoretical consistency.

Method: Analysis of quotient group structures encoding global symmetry identifications; identification of non-invertible chiral symmetries protecting domain walls; application of 'Non-invertible Naturalness' to motivate UV completions with small-instanton effects; estimation of gravitational wave signals from domain wall annihilation.

Result: Global structure (G_EW × U(1)_PQ)/ℤ₂ determines domain wall number N_DW in DFSZ; non-invertible symmetries in Z′ flavor models stabilize walls via (SU(3)_C × G_F)/ℤ₃; UV resolutions involve instanton-induced breaking of non-invertible symmetries; observable stochastic gravitational wave background predicted from wall decay.

Conclusion: Global structure of symmetry groups is essential for accurate phenomenological analysis—particularly for axion cosmology—and necessitates incorporation of non-invertible symmetries and their breaking mechanisms in UV model building.

Abstract: In recent years it has become increasingly clear that the previously overlooked ``global structure'' of symmetry groups can encode significant theoretical structure and, more importantly, have substantial phenomenological implications. With this in mind we re-examine the DFSZ axion, which suffers from a domain wall problem due to the Standard Model generation structure. We show that global structure $(G_{\text{EW}} \times U(1)_{\text{PQ}})/\mathbb{Z}_2$ acting between the Peccei-Quinn symmetry and the electroweak gauge group plays a crucial role in determining the precise nature of the domain wall problem, which has important implications in both cubic and quartic DFSZ. We then demonstrate that the stability of the domain walls is enforced by a non-invertible chiral symmetry in quark flavor $Z'$ models which have additional global structure $(SU(3)_C \times G_F)/\mathbb{Z}_3$ acting between the color and the gauged quark flavor groups. The strategy of Non-invertible Naturalness then leads us to UV theories that resolve the domain wall problem through small-instanton-induced breaking of non-invertible symmetries. Finally, we sketch potential gravitational wave signatures arising from the annihilation of axion domain walls. Our work illustrates the importance of considerations of global structure in realistic models of particle physics.

</details>


### [83] [Simulating Axion Electrodynamics in Magnetized Plasmas: Energy transfer in the inhomogeneous and strongly varying limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26868)
*Fabrizio Corelli,Estanis Utrilla Ginés,Enrico Cannizzaro,Andrea Caputo,Samuel J. Witte*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates axion-induced electromagnetic responses in magnetized plasmas, using frequency- and time-domain simulations to quantify photon and Alfvén mode excitation, electric field generation in plasma under-densities, and enhanced energy transfer into sub-luminal plasma modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize energy transfer and losses from ambient axion fields in highly inhomogeneous, strongly varying magnetized plasmas—relevant for extreme astrophysical environments.

Method: Combined frequency-domain and time-domain numerical simulations to solve for photon excitation efficiency, indirect Alfvén mode excitation via LO-mode resonance near cutoff-resonance, and electric field generation in localized plasma under-densities.

Result: Identification of a regime where sub-luminal plasma modes (ω < k) absorb axion energy more efficiently than super-luminal modes (ω > k); demonstration of resonant LO–Alfvén coupling and localized electric field excitation.

Conclusion: Axions—and other light EM-mixing degrees of freedom like dark photons or gravitons—can interact through non-standard channels in extreme plasmas, expanding viable detection signatures beyond conventional photon conversion.

Abstract: In this work we study the electromagnetic response induced by axions in a magnetized plasma, focusing specifically on characterizing energy transfer and energy losses from the ambient axion field in highly inhomogeneous and strongly varying backgrounds. Using a suite of both frequency-domain and time-domain simulations, we solve for: the efficiency of photon excitation in a rapidly varying background, the indirect excitation of Alfvén modes, occurring when a Langmuir-Ordinary (LO) mode is resonantly excited near a combined cutoff-resonance of the dispersion relations of the LO and Alfvén modes, and the excitation of electric fields in small localized plasma under-densities. We identify a particularly interesting regime in which energy can be transferred into sub-luminal plasma modes ($ω< k$) with an efficiency greater than that of super-luminal modes ($ω> k$). Our results highlight a variety of less conventional ways in which axions (and other light degrees of freedom that mix with electromagnetism, such as dark photons or gravitons) can interact in extreme astrophysical environments.

</details>


### [84] [The Physics and Prospects of Super-Tau Charm Factories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26879)
*Alexey A. Petrov,Yangheng Zheng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This review outlines the physics potential of Super tau-charm factories—high-luminosity e⁺e⁻ colliders (2–7 GeV) enabling precision SM tests and BSM searches via quantum-coherent production of τ, charm, charmonium, hyperons, and light hadrons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the unique kinematic and quantum-coherent advantages of high-luminosity e⁺e⁻ collisions in the 2–7 GeV range for precision Standard Model tests and discovery of physics beyond the Standard Model.

Method: Review-based analysis of physics prospects, including precision charm physics, CP violation (mesons/baryons), tau lepton properties and rare decays, and nonperturbative QCD phenomena (hadronization, spectroscopy, time-like form factors); also assesses experimental challenges and complementarity with other facilities.

Result: Super tau-charm factories are positioned at the precision frontier, offering unmatched capabilities for high-statistics, threshold-resolved, quantum-coherently produced data across multiple particle sectors.

Conclusion: These facilities constitute a transformative tool for fundamental physics, bridging precision SM tests and exploratory BSM searches, and synergizing with global collider and intensity-frontier programs.

Abstract: The proposed Super tau-charm factories are a powerful new class of high-luminosity electron-positron colliders operating in the center-of-mass energy range between 2 and 7 GeV, a region that spans thresholds for tau leptons, open-charm hadrons, charmonium and charmonium-like states, hyperons, and light hadrons. With unprecedented data samples, threshold kinematics, and quantum-coherent production, these facilities offer unique opportunities to advance precision tests of the Standard Model and to search for physics beyond it. In this review, we examine the physics prospects of the Super Tau-Charm Facility, focusing on precision charm measurements, CP violation in mesons and baryons, tau lepton properties and rare decays, and nonperturbative QCD phenomena such as hadronization, spectroscopy, and time-like form factors. We also discuss the experimental landscape, technological challenges, and complementarity with existing and planned facilities. Together, these capabilities position super tau-charm factories at the forefront of the precision frontier in particle physics.

</details>


### [85] [Axion EFT in the BMHV Scheme: Flavor Currents, Evanescent Operators and Ward Identities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26880)
*Deepanshu Bisht,Sabyasachi Chakraborty,Atanu Samanta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper systematically analyzes axion effective field theory in the BMHV scheme, addressing γ₅-related complications in d≠4 dimensions; it derives and verifies chiral Ward identities up to two loops, quantifies evanescent operator mixing, and determines finite renormalization needed to restore 4D consistency and the axial anomaly.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies arising from the non-anticommuting γ₅ in dimensional regularization (BMHV scheme) when renormalizing fermionic dimension-five operators and chiral flavor currents in axion EFT, ensuring validity of Ward identities and anomaly structure beyond one loop.

Method: Systematic renormalization within the BMHV scheme; derivation of bare and renormalized chiral Ward identities including equation-of-motion and evanescent operator contributions; explicit two-loop diagrammatic calculations (O(αₛ²)) verifying pole and finite-term consistency; analysis of evanescent-to-physical operator mixing and determination of finite counterterms.

Result: Complete two-loop verification of chiral Ward identities in BMHV scheme; explicit identification of evanescent operator contributions violating naive identities; quantification of evanescent mixing into physical operators; derivation of finite renormalization that restores four-dimensional Ward identities and correctly reproduces axial current renormalization and the Adler–Bell–Jackiw anomaly.

Conclusion: Evanescent operators are indispensable for scheme consistency in multi-loop axion EFT computations; the BMHV scheme—augmented with proper evanescent operator treatment—provides a robust, transparent framework for high-precision calculations involving chiral fermions and axion couplings.

Abstract: We present a systematic analysis of axion effective field theory within the Breitenlohner-Maison-`t Hooft-Veltman (BMHV) scheme, focusing on the renormalization of fermionic dimension-five operators and the associated chiral flavor currents. In this framework, the non-anticommuting nature of $γ_5$ in $d \neq 4$ dimensions leads to violations of naive Ward identities through the emergence of evanescent operators. We derive the bare and renormalized Ward identities for chiral currents, explicitly identifying the equation-of-motion and evanescent operator contributions. Using diagrammatic calculations, we verify the validity of these identities up to two-loop order $\mathcal{O}(α_s^2)$, including both pole and finite terms. We demonstrate how evanescent operators mix into physical operators and determine the finite renormalization required to restore four-dimensional Ward identities, recovering the expected structure of axial current renormalization and the anomaly. Our results provide a consistent and transparent framework for multi-loop computations in axion EFT and highlight the essential role of evanescent operators in maintaining scheme consistency.

</details>


### [86] [Density screening effects in the NJL model: Chiral condensate, speed of sound, and the Critical End Point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26924)
*Alejandro Rosas Díaz,Alfredo Raya,C. A. Vaquera Araujo,S. Hernández-Ortiz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the QCD phase diagram—particularly the chiral transition and Critical End Point (CEP)—using an extended NJL model with temperature- and density-dependent coupling $G(T,\mu)$, consistent regularization, and Sommerfeld expansion; it finds that medium screening shifts the CEP location and alters the chiral transition nature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To theoretically locate and characterize the Critical End Point (CEP) in the QCD phase diagram—a key unresolved goal of heavy-ion experiments (FAIR, NICA)—and reconcile theoretical predictions of crossover (at high T, low μ) and first-order (at high μ) transitions.

Method: Extended Nambu--Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model with medium-dependent coupling $G(T,\mu)$ for $\mu \gg T \sim 0$; consistent Pauli-Villars regularization; Sommerfeld expansion to incorporate finite-T and large-μ corrections in the gap equation; numerical analysis of chiral condensate, dynamical quark mass, and speed of sound.

Result: Screening effects significantly shift the predicted location of the CEP and change the chiral transition from crossover to first-order (or vice versa) in certain regions of the phase diagram; the speed of sound exhibits non-monotonic behavior near the CEP, signaling softening of the equation of state.

Conclusion: Medium screening is essential for reliable CEP prediction in effective models; the modified NJL framework improves consistency with both heavy-ion phenomenology and compact-star constraints, supporting targeted experimental searches.

Abstract: The phase diagram of Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) remains a central topic in high-energy physics. At high temperature and low baryochemical potential, the chiral transition is experimentally observed and theoretically explored to be a smooth crossover, while at high densities, a first-order phase transition is theoretically expected in lack of direct experimental evidence. The search for the Critical End Point (CEP), where both regimes meet, is one of the main objectives of heavy-ion experiments at FAIR and NICA. In this work, we explore the QCD phase diagram structure using the Nambu--Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model, incorporating medium screening effects through an effective coupling $G(T,μ)$ for $μ\gg T\sim 0$. We apply a consistent regularization scheme and Sommerfeld expansion to include low thermal and large density corrections in the gap equation. Our numerical analysis focuses on the behavior of the chiral condensate, the dynamical quark mass, and the speed of sound. We find that screening effects shift the posible position of the CEP and modify the nature of the chiral transition. These findings provide theoretical support for ongoing experimental searches and may have implications for the physics of compact stars.

</details>


### [87] [Signatures from pion condensation and lepton flavor asymmetries in the cosmological gravitational wave background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26946)
*Osvaldo Ferreira,Eduardo S. Fraga,Jürgen Schaffner-Bielich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how large tau lepton flavor asymmetries during the QCD epoch could trigger pion condensation in the early Universe, leading to a super-conformal speed of sound and a distinctive low-frequency gravitational wave (GW) signature; it estimates this GW signal, contrasts it with the zero-lepton-asymmetry case, and assesses its compatibility with the NANOGrav 15-year stochastic GW background.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore novel cosmological probes of lepton flavor asymmetries—particularly tau lepton asymmetries—via their impact on QCD-phase dynamics and resulting gravitational wave signatures, especially in light of recent Pulsar Timing Array (PTA) observations.

Method: Theoretical modeling of pion condensation induced by large lepton flavor asymmetries at the QCD epoch; calculation of the equation of state and speed of sound; derivation of the associated causal gravitational wave spectrum; comparison with the standard (symmetric) QCD transition; statistical assessment against NANOGrav 15-year stochastic background limits.

Result: Large tau lepton asymmetries can drive c_s^2 > 1/3 during pion condensation, producing an enhanced, low-frequency GW spectrum distinguishable from the symmetric case; current NANOGrav 15-yr data neither confirms nor rules out this scenario but places preliminary bounds on the allowed asymmetry magnitude and condensation duration.

Conclusion: Pion condensation from tau lepton asymmetries is a viable, testable mechanism imprinting unique features on the stochastic GW background; future PTA data will provide decisive constraints on lepton flavor asymmetries in the early Universe.

Abstract: Large lepton flavor asymmetries at the QCD epoch could generate a pion condensation phase in the early Universe. For large enough tau lepton flavor asymmetries, the speed of sound can exceed the conformal value, leaving a distinctive imprint on the low-frequency gravitational wave (GW) spectrum from causal sources. Beyond probing the formation of a pion condensation phase, the detection or non-detection of this signature would provide a novel constraint on lepton asymmetries in the early Universe. We estimate the GW signal and compare it with the standard case of vanishing lepton asymmetry. Finally, we discuss the implications for the stochastic GW background reported by Pulsar Timing Arrays, using the NANOGrav 15-year dataset.

</details>


### [88] [Agentic Diagrammatica: Towards Autonomous Symbolic Computation in High Energy Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26990)
*Tony Menzo,Alexander Roman,George T. Fleming,Sergei Gleyzer,Konstantin T. Matchev,Stephen Mrenna*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Diagrammatica extends the HEPTAPOD agentic framework with symbolic computation capabilities for theoretical physics calculations, using diagram-based specifications and trusted backends (NDA/EDA) to ensure reliability; validated on decay-width enumeration and muon-decay multiplicity sensitivity analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Symbolic computation with LLM agents suffers from unreliability due to implicit mathematical conventions not verifiable by computational backends; Diagrammatica addresses this via tool-constrained computation and knowledge grounding.

Method: Introduces Diagrammatica—a tool-constrained agent architecture where LLMs specify compact, human-auditable Feynman diagram specifications, and a trusted backend executes exact symbolic (via FeynCalc) or approximate (Naive Dimensional Analysis) calculations; supports automatic diagram enumeration and theory-aware navigation.

Result: Validated on two benchmarks: (1) exhaustive tree-level 1→2 partial decay widths across particle types with massless/threshold limits and SM consistency checks; (2) NDA-based sensitivity prediction of maximum observable e⁺e⁻ multiplicity n in μ⁺ → ν_μν̄_e + n(e⁺e⁻) + e⁻ at current/future muon experiments.

Conclusion: Tool-constrained specification—centering agent actions on convention-fixed diagram inputs—enables reliable, auditable, and scalable symbolic reasoning in theoretical particle physics, bridging LLM planning with rigorous backend computation.

Abstract: We present Diagrammatica, a symbolic computation extension to the HEPTAPOD agentic framework, which enables LLM agents to plan and execute multi-step theoretical calculations. Symbolic computation poses a distinctive reliability challenge for LLM agents, as correctness is governed by implicit mathematical conventions that are not encoded in a form that can be easily checked in the computational backend. We identify two complementary remedies, tool-constrained computation and targeted knowledge grounding, and pursue the first as the primary architecture. Concretely, we concentrate the agent's action distribution onto tool calls with convention-fixing semantics, in which the agent specifies a compact, human-auditable diagram specification and a trusted backend performs the symbolic or numerical manipulations exactly. The toolkit provides two complementary calculation paths consuming a shared diagram specification: Naive Dimensional Analysis (NDA) for order-of-magnitude rate estimates and Exact Diagrammatic Analysis (EDA) for tree-level symbolic calculations via automatic FeynCalc code generation, both supplemented by automatic Feynman diagram enumeration and a navigable theory knowledge base. The architecture is validated on two benchmarks: (1) an exhaustive catalog of all tree-level, single-vertex $1\to 2$ partial decay widths across scalar, fermion, and vector parents, with complete massless and threshold limits and Standard Model validation; and (2) an NDA sensitivity study of the muon decay multiplicity $μ^+ \to ν_μ\barν_e + n(e^+e^-) + e^-$, determining the maximum observable $n$ at current and planned muon experiments.

</details>


### [89] [Layered dark structure with a Structuring Field: A $Z_4$-symmetric Inert Doublet-Singlet realization and implications for the $S_8$ tension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27096)
*Marriam Naeem,Mohid Farhan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces Layered Dark Sectors with a Structuring Field (LDS-SF), a cosmological framework where multi-component dark sector dynamics induce scale-dependent structure growth via eigenvalue-driven perturbations; realizes it in a Z₄-symmetric Inert Doublet Singlet Model, maps interactions to an effective sound speed, implements in CLASS with late-time activation, and identifies three coupling sets that resolve the S₈ tension at 1σ with KiDS-1000/DES.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the S₈ tension between CMB-inferred and weak-lensing-measured matter clustering amplitude while preserving ΛCDM background expansion and General Relativity.

Method: Constructs LDS-SF framework using eigenmode analysis of dark sector perturbation matrix; builds minimal Z₄-IDSM realization; derives contact-interaction EFT for 60 GeV singlet DM; maps interaction to effective sound speed cₛ²; implements in CLASS with virial-projection activation function; computes relic density via micrOMEGAs; confronts with Planck, BAO, fσ₈, KiDS-1000, and DES data.

Result: Model matches ΛCDM at recombination but suppresses matter power spectrum at z < 10 and k > 0.1 h/Mpc; three viable coupling points reduce S₈ discrepancy to within 1σ of KiDS-1000 and DES weak-lensing constraints.

Conclusion: LDS-SF is a mathematically consistent, theoretically well-motivated, and observationally viable extension of ΛCDM that addresses small-scale structure anomalies without altering background cosmology or gravity.

Abstract: We introduce the Layered Dark Sectors with a Structuring Field (LDS-SF), a structured cosmological framework where the internal architecture of a multi-component dark sector naturally generates scale-dependent growth of structure. In this framework, the characteristic scale dependence is derived from the dominant eigenvalue, $λ(k)$, of the dark sector's perturbation matrix. This structurally-driven mechanism modifies structure growth while preserving the standard $Λ$CDM background expansion and General Relativity.
  We provide a minimal realization of this framework within a two-component DM $Z_4$-symmetric Inert Doublet Singlet Model ($Z_4$-IDSM). By integrating out the heavy inert doublet mediator, we derive a contact-interaction Effective Field Theory (EFT) for a 60~GeV singlet dark matter candidate. This interaction manifests macroscopically as an effective sound speed $c_s^2$, which we map to the LDS-SF eigenmode evolution. We implement this system into the CLASS Boltzmann code, employing a late-time activation function that projects virialized halo properties into the linear perturbation framework. We also compute the relic density using micrOMEGAs to further stress-test the relic abundance predictions of viable parameters.
  Our numerical analysis demonstrates that while the model remains indistinguishable from $Λ$CDM at the era of recombination, it introduces a targeted suppression of the matter power spectrum at late times ($z < 10$) and small scales ($k > 0.1~h/\text{Mpc}$). Confronting the model with Planck CMB, BAO, and growth-rate measurements, we find three instances of couplings that successfully alleviate the $S_8$ tension, bringing the predicted amplitude into $1σ$ agreement with weak-lensing data from KiDS-1000 and DES. This work establishes LDS-SF as a mathematically consistent and observationally viable extension of standard cosmology.

</details>


### [90] [Two-Loop Spacelike Splitting Amplitudes in Full-Color QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27123)
*Federico Buccioni,Hanyu Fang,Kai Yan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes full-color two-loop spacelike splitting amplitudes in QCD, confirms known universal collinear structures from N=4 sYM, discovers new collinear factorization-violating (CFV) effects, and shows CFV terms cancel in color-summed squared amplitudes—ensuring third-order universality of single-parton collinear factorization for jet cross sections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand QCD factorization in the collinear regime and test universality of splitting amplitudes beyond leading order, especially in light of recent findings in N=4 sYM.

Method: Direct computation of two-loop spacelike splitting amplitudes in full-color QCD using modern amplitude techniques (e.g., unitarity, integration-by-parts reduction, analytic regularization), covering all partonic channels and helicity configurations.

Result: First complete two-loop spacelike splitting amplitudes in full-color QCD; confirmation of known universal contributions from N=4 sYM; discovery of new CFV effects; cancellation of all CFV terms in color-summed |M|², up to third order in αₛ.

Conclusion: Single-parton collinear factorization remains universal for inclusive jet cross sections at third perturbative order in QCD, despite presence of CFV effects at the amplitude level.

Abstract: The study of QCD scattering amplitudes in the collinear regime provides crucial insight into the factorization properties of hadronic cross sections. In this paper, we present the first complete results for two-loop spacelike splitting amplitudes in full-color QCD, in all partonic channels and helicity configurations. We confirm the universality of a class of contributions already found in N=4 super Yang--Mills (sYM) theory, and identify previously unknown sources of collinear factorization-violating (CFV) effects. Consistent with recent observations in N=4 sYM, all CFV contributions cancel in color-summed squared amplitudes, implying the universality of single-parton collinear factorization for jet cross sections at third order in QCD.

</details>


### [91] [Light and Heavy $Z'$ from Flavored Chiral $U(1)_X$ Gauge Symmetries: Purely Axial and Mixed Vector-Axial Couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27157)
*Hemant Kumar Prajapati,Rahul Srivastava*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a new class of flavor-specific chiral U(1)_X gauge symmetries to generate non-universal, purely axial or mixed vector-axial Z′ couplings to Standard Model fermions—beyond simple vector-only interactions—while maintaining anomaly freedom via three right-handed neutrinos and minimal scalar content.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize model-independent effective interactions with purely axial (A) or mixed vector-axial (V,A) couplings—common in phenomenological analyses (e.g., neutrino/B-physics)—within a UV-complete, anomaly-free gauge theory, which is nontrivial in conventional gauged U(1)_X extensions like B−L.

Method: Systematic classification of anomaly-free U(1)_X charge assignments for SM fermions and three right-handed neutrinos; construction of minimal UV completions with only necessary scalars (no extra fermions); analysis of resulting Z′ couplings (vector/axial/chiral structure) and flavor violation patterns.

Result: Demonstration that chiral, flavor-specific U(1)_X models can naturally yield purely axial or mixed V–A Z′ couplings to SM fermions (both in heavy and light Z′ limits); emergence of tree-level flavor-changing neutral currents (FCNCs) in quark and lepton sectors due to non-universal charges; identification of several benchmark models with distinct flavor structures.

Conclusion: Flavor-specific chiral U(1)_X symmetries provide a theoretically consistent, minimal, and phenomenologically rich framework for generating beyond-vector Z′ interactions—enabling direct connection between UV charge assignments and low-energy effective operators relevant for precision flavor and collider studies.

Abstract: Model independent phenomenological studies, ranging from neutrino to B-physics, often consider effective interactions involving either purely vector (V), purely axial vector (A), or mixed vector and axial vector (V, A) couplings. While pure vector $Z'$ interactions can naturally emerge in gauged $U(1)_X$ extensions of the Standard Model, such as the $B-L$ model, generating other coupling structures from a UV complete theory is highly nontrivial. To realize such couplings, we propose a new class of flavor specific chiral $U(1)_X$ gauge symmetries. Gauge anomaly cancellation is achieved by introducing three right-handed neutrinos charged under the $U(1)_X$ symmetry. We systematically classify anomaly free charge assignments and analyze viable ultraviolet completions with minimal scalar content, requiring no additional fermions beyond the three necessary for anomaly cancellation. We present several benchmark models illustrating the range of possible charge assignments, under which the quark and lepton flavor structures can differ substantially, leading to distinct phenomenological signatures. In particular, such non universal charge configurations naturally give rise to $Z'$ mediated flavor changing neutral currents in both the quark and lepton sectors. We also demonstrate that, within this framework, the $Z'$ boson can naturally acquire purely axial vector or mixed vector-axial couplings to the SM fermions, both in the heavy and light $Z'$ regimes.

</details>


### [92] [Magnetic moments of open bottom--charm molecular pentaquark octets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27657)
*Halil Mutuk,Xian-Wei Kang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates magnetic moments of open heavy-flavor molecular pentaquarks (b̄cqqq and c̄bqqq) using a constituent quark model and S-wave molecular picture, distinguishing two SU(3)ₜ octets (8₁f and 8₂f) based on light-diquark symmetry; it finds universal magnetic moments in the antisymmetric (8₂f) case due to spin-singlet diquarks, contrasting with varied values in the symmetric (8₁f) case, thereby revealing heavy-quark flavor symmetry breaking and providing electromagnetic benchmarks for experimental identification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide theoretically robust electromagnetic observables—specifically magnetic moments—that can discriminate between competing internal structures (e.g., light-diquark symmetry) and spin configurations of newly discovered or yet-to-be-observed open heavy-flavor pentaquarks, and to probe heavy-quark flavor symmetry breaking in hadronic systems.

Method: Adopts a molecular picture where pentaquarks are S-wave bound states of a heavy baryon and a meson; constructs complete spin–flavor wavefunctions for the two SU(3)ₜ octet representations (8₁f from symmetric, 8₂f from antisymmetric light-diquark); computes magnetic moments within the constituent quark model for J^P = 1/2⁻ (from 1/2⁺⊗0⁻) and 1/2⁻, 3/2⁻ (from 1/2⁺⊗1⁻) configurations across both b̄c and c̄b families.

Result: In the 8₂f representation (antisymmetric light-diquark), magnetic moments are nearly universal: ≈ −0.062 μ_N (b̄cqqq) and ≈ +0.362 μ_N (c̄bqqq), due to suppression of light-quark contributions by the spin-singlet diquark; in contrast, the 8₁f representation shows large variation and sign changes; clear b̄c vs. c̄b differences confirm explicit heavy-quark flavor symmetry breaking in electromagnetic observables.

Conclusion: Magnetic moments serve as sensitive probes of light-diquark symmetry and heavy-quark flavor structure in open heavy-flavor pentaquarks; the predicted hierarchy and flavor asymmetry provide concrete, testable benchmarks for identifying internal configurations in experiments at LHCb and Belle II.

Abstract: We present a comprehensive theoretical investigation of the magnetic moments of open heavy-flavor molecular pentaquarks with quark compositions $b\bar{c}qqq$ and $c\bar{b}qqq$ (where $q=u,d,s$). Employing a molecular picture in which the pentaquarks are treated as S-wave bound states of a heavy baryon and a meson, we systematically construct the complete spin--flavor wavefunctions for the two distinct SU(3)$_f$ octet representations, $8_{1f}$ and $8_{2f}$, arising from symmetric and antisymmetric light-diquark configurations, respectively. Within the framework of the constituent quark model, we calculate the magnetic moments of spin-parity configurations, $J^P = \frac{1}{2}^-(\frac{1}{2}^+\otimes 0^-)$ and $J^P = \frac{1}{2}^-, \frac{3}{2}^-(\frac{1}{2}^+\otimes 1^-)$, for each member of the $b\bar{c}$ and $c\bar{b}$ octets. Our results reveal a striking hierarchy: in the $8_{2f}$ representation, the $\frac{1}{2}^+\otimes 0^-$ states exhibit near-universal magnetic moments ($μ\approx -0.062\,μ_N$ for $b\bar{c}qqq$ and $μ\approx +0.362\,μ_N$ for $c\bar{b}qqq$), as a direct consequence of the spin-singlet light-diquark that suppresses light-quark contributions. In contrast, the $8_{1f}$ representation shows a broad spectrum of values with frequent sign changes, reflecting the active role of the symmetric light-diquark. The clear differences between the $b\bar{c}$ and $c\bar{b}$ families demonstrate explicit heavy-quark flavor symmetry breaking in electromagnetic observables. These predictions provide a detailed set of electromagnetic benchmarks that can serve as discriminants for the internal flavor structure and spin configuration of future experimentally observed open heavy-flavor pentaquarks, offering valuable guidance for ongoing and future searches at facilities such as LHCb and Belle II.

</details>


### [93] [Late-time attractors in relativistic spin hydrodynamics in Gubser flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27182)
*Gen-Hui Li,Xiang Ren,Dong-Lin Wang,Shi Pu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the late-time behavior and attractor structure of spin density in minimal causal spin hydrodynamics under Gubser flow, identifying power-law decay regimes and showing that certain spin components can behave as slow hydrodynamic modes when system size ≫ proper time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how spin density evolves at late times in relativistic spin hydrodynamics, particularly whether it exhibits universal (attractor) behavior and can be treated as a hydrodynamic mode.

Method: Derivation of the spin density evolution equation in Gubser coordinates; analytical and numerical analysis of late-time asymptotics; identification of attractors/repellers; mapping back to Minkowski space; scaling analysis under condition L ≫ τ.

Result: Late-time asymptotic solutions are obtained; attractors and repellers are identified numerically; power-law decay of spin density is found in specific parameter regions; for L ≫ τ, some spin components decay as slowly as conventional hydrodynamic variables.

Conclusion: Spin density can exhibit hydrodynamic-mode behavior governed by flow-induced scaling laws, extending the applicability of hydrodynamic descriptions to spin degrees of freedom in certain late-time, large-system limits.

Abstract: We investigate the late-time asymptotic solutions and attractor structure of the spin density in minimal causal spin hydrodynamics in Gubser flow. After deriving the differential equation governing the spin density, we obtain its late-time asymptotic solutions and identify both attractors and repellers in the corresponding numerical solutions. We then map these solutions back to flat Minkowski space and find parameter regions where the spin density exhibits a power-law decay. We further show that, when the characteristic length scale of the system is much larger than the proper time, several components of the spin density can decay as slowly as conventional thermodynamic variables in relativistic hydrodynamics. In this regime, the spin density behaves as a hydrodynamic mode governed by the late-time scaling laws of the flow.

</details>


### [94] [Bag Parameters for Heavy Meson Lifetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28516)
*Matthew Black,Robert V. Harlander,Jonas T. Kohnen,Fabian Lange,Antonio Rago,Andrea Shindler,Oliver Witzel*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes dimension-six ΔQ=0 four-quark matrix elements for heavy-meson lifetime ratios using gradient-flow-based renormalization on six 2+1-flavor domain-wall fermion ensembles; it reports the first fully error-budgeted lattice-QCD determination of flowed bag parameters and evanescent-operator-dependent B_i and ε_i parameters in the MS-bar scheme at 3 GeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable high-precision predictions of heavy-meson lifetimes (e.g., τ(D_s)/τ(D^0)) by reliably computing ΔQ=0 four-quark matrix elements subject to operator mixing under renormalization—a long-standing challenge in lattice QCD.

Method: Uses gradient flow with short flow-time expansion as a renormalization scheme; performs nonperturbative computation on six RBC/UKQCD 2+1-flavor domain-wall fermion ensembles; matches flowed bag parameters for charm/strange quarks to the MS-bar scheme using NNLO perturbative coefficients; employs multi-scale matching with RG running to extrapolate to zero flow time.

Result: First full-error-budget lattice-QCD determination of ΔQ=0 four-quark matrix elements: B₁^MS̄(3 GeV) = 1.0524(97), B₂^MS̄(3 GeV) = 0.9621(70), ε₁^MS̄(3 GeV) = −0.2275(76), ε₂^MS̄(3 GeV) = −0.0005(8), at the SU(3)_F symmetric point using a specific evanescent operator basis.

Conclusion: The gradient-flow-based approach provides a robust, scale-independent, and systematically improvable framework for renormalizing ΔQ=0 four-quark operators; this work establishes a foundation for sub-percent precision in heavy-meson lifetime ratios and related weak decay observables.

Abstract: We calculate the dimension-six $ΔQ=0$ four-quark matrix elements describing heavy-meson lifetime ratios using the gradient flow with its short flow-time expansion as a renormalization procedure. On six RBC/UKQCD 2+1-flavor domain-wall fermion ensembles, we determine flowed bag parameters for physical charm and strange quarks and match to the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ scheme with perturbative short flow-time expansion coefficients through next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO). A multi-scale matching procedure using renormalization-group running improves the extrapolation to zero flow time. For the operators relevant to $τ(D_s)/τ(D^0)$ at the SU(3)$_{\rm F}$ symmetric point, we obtain $B_1^{\overline{\text{MS}}}(3\,{\rm GeV})=1.0524(97)$,$B_2^{\overline{\text{MS}}}(3\,{\rm GeV})=0.9621(70)$, $ε_1^{\overline{\text{MS}}}(3\,{\rm GeV})=-0.2275(76)$, and $ε_2^{\overline{\text{MS}}}(3\,{\rm GeV})=-0.0005(8)$ using a specific choice of evanescent operators. This is the first lattice-QCD determination of $ΔQ=0$ four-quark operators with a full error budget. It opens the path towards higher-precision predictions of heavy-meson lifetimes and similar quantities exhibiting operator mixing under renormalization.

</details>


### [95] [Vorticity-induced modifications of chemical freeze-out in heavy-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27267)
*Nandita Padhan,Kshitish Kumar Pradhan,Arghya Chatterjee,Raghunath Sahoo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how global rotation affects chemical freeze-out parameters in ultra-relativistic heavy-ion collisions using an extended hadron resonance gas (HRG) model; it finds rotation shifts the freeze-out curve to lower temperatures, alters electric charge and strangeness chemical potentials, and shows hadron yield ratios—more than cumulant ratios—are sensitive probes of rotational effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically investigate the influence of global rotation—a recently recognized feature in non-central heavy-ion collisions—on chemical freeze-out conditions and related observables within a theoretically consistent framework.

Method: Extended the hadron resonance gas (HRG) model to incorporate global rotation; applied modified freeze-out criteria (fixed energy per particle and scaled entropy density) to extract $T$ and $\mu_B$; computed rotational dependence of $\mu_Q$, $\mu_S$, hadron yield ratios, and susceptibility (cumulant) ratios.

Result: Rotation shifts the chemical freeze-out curve toward lower temperatures in the $T$--$\mu_B$ plane; induces nontrivial behavior in $\mu_Q$ and $\mu_S$; enhances sensitivity of hadron yield ratios to rotation, while cumulant ratios remain largely unchanged.

Conclusion: Hadron yield ratios are more effective than conventional cumulant ratios for constraining the magnitude of global rotation in heavy-ion collisions; this work provides the first systematic HRG-based analysis of rotational effects on all major chemical freeze-out parameters and observables.

Abstract: We investigate the influence of global rotation on the chemical freeze-out parameters in ultra-relativistic heavy-ion collisions. Within the framework of the hadron resonance gas (HRG) model, the freeze-out parameters are determined using commonly employed freeze-out criteria, namely the fixed energy per particle and the scaled entropy density, extended here to include rotational effects. We find that the presence of rotation leads to a systematic shift of the chemical freeze-out curve toward lower temperatures in the $T\text{--}μ_B$ phase diagram. The behavior of the electric charge and strangeness chemical potentials in the presence of rotation is also analyzed, providing the first systematic study of their rotational dependence within the HRG framework. Furthermore, we examine the impact of rotation on experimentally relevant observables, including hadron yield ratios and susceptibility ratios of conserved charges. Our results show that while particle yield ratios exhibit noticeable sensitivity to rotation, the conventional cumulant ratios remain comparatively less affected. This indicates that hadronic yield ratios may provide a more suitable observable for estimating the magnitude of rotational effects generated in heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [96] [Chiral Phase Transition in Rotating Quark Matter with Chiral Imbalance: A Medium Separation Scheme Regularized NJL Model Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27216)
*Huang-Jing Zheng,Peng Nan,Sheng-Qin Feng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how rotation and chiral imbalance affect the chiral phase transition in quark matter using the NJL model with Medium Separation Scheme (MSS) regularization. It finds that chiral chemical potential μ₅ enhances chiral symmetry breaking (raising T_pc), while angular velocity ω suppresses it (lowering T_pc); μ₅ mitigates ω’s suppression, and MSS resolves prior discrepancies with lattice QCD. Strong radius dependence of rotational effects is revealed, including possible abrupt drops in T_pc at high ω.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies between previous NJL model predictions and lattice QCD results on the chiral phase transition under rotation and chiral imbalance, and to clarify the interplay between angular velocity ω and chiral chemical potential μ₅—particularly how spacetime curvature and centrifugal effects influence the pseudocritical temperature T_pc.

Method: Two-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model regularized via the Medium Separation Scheme (MSS); numerical computation of chiral condensate, pseudocritical temperature T_pc, and phase transition strength as functions of μ₅, ω, and system radius; comparison with lattice QCD and analysis of curvature/centrifugal contributions.

Result: μ₅ increases and sharpens the chiral phase transition (↑T_pc), whereas ω decreases and smears it (↓T_pc); μ₅ progressively buffers ω-induced suppression of T_pc; MSS yields monotonic μ₅–T_pc dependence consistent with LQCD; rotational suppression of T_pc intensifies with radius and can cause abrupt drops at high ω due to enhanced spacetime curvature and centrifugal forces.

Conclusion: Rotation and chiral imbalance exert antagonistic, radius-dependent influences on chiral symmetry restoration; MSS is validated as a physically consistent regularization for rotating, chirally imbalanced QCD matter; findings provide a robust theoretical basis for interpreting heavy-ion collision and compact star observables involving vorticity and axial charge.

Abstract: We investigate the chiral phase transition in rotating quark matter with chiral imbalance using the two-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model regularized by the Medium Separation Scheme (MSS). Our numerical calculations demonstrate that the chiral chemical potential $μ_5$ and angular velocity $ω$ exert opposite effects on chiral symmetry breaking: $μ_5$ enhances the breaking, raising the pseudocritical temperature $T_{pc}$ and sharpening the phase transition, while $ω$ suppresses the breaking, lowering $T_{pc}$ and smearing the transition. Notably, chiral imbalance buffers the rotation-induced softening of the phase transition-the suppression of $T_{pc}$ by $ω$ weakens progressively as $μ_5$ increases. The MSS predicts a monotonic increase of $T_{pc}$ with $μ_5$, in qualitative agreement with LQCD, resolving the discrepancy found in traditional regularization. Furthermore, the rotational suppression of $T_{pc}$ exhibits strong radius dependence: larger rotation radii amplify the suppression due to enhanced spacetime curvature and centrifugal effects, and can even induce an abrupt drop in $T_{pc}$ in the high-rotation region. These findings clarify the interplay between rotation and chiral imbalance in modulating the QCD chiral phase transition and validate the MSS as a reliable regularization framework for such extreme systems.

</details>


### [97] [Isolation of photon-nuclear interaction backgrounds in the search for the chiral magnetic effect in relativistic heavy-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27473)
*Jing Gu,Jinhui Chen,Jie Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates coherent photon-nuclear interactions as a novel background to the chiral magnetic effect (CME) in heavy-ion collisions, showing they produce charge-dependent correlations mimicking CME but arising from electromagnetic field polarization—not chirality imbalance or flow—thereby complicating CME detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify and quantify previously unaccounted background sources—specifically coherent photon-nuclear interactions driven by strong electromagnetic fields—that can mimic the CME signal and thus hinder definitive CME observation.

Method: Quantitative estimation of coherent photon-nuclear interaction contributions, leveraging their dependence on electromagnetic field strength and polarization geometry (aligned with electric field, perpendicular to magnetic field), and comparing their charge-dependent correlation signatures to those expected from the CME and flow backgrounds.

Result: Coherent photon-nuclear interactions generate CME-like charge-dependent correlations distinct from both flow-related backgrounds and the genuine CME; their contribution is non-negligible and must be accounted for to improve CME signal extraction.

Conclusion: These electromagnetic-field-driven processes constitute an important, physically distinct background to the CME search; disentangling them is essential for high-precision CME measurements and robust tests of $CP$ violation in QCD matter.

Abstract: The chiral magnetic effect (CME) in relativistic heavy-ion collisions originates from a chirality imbalance among quarks within metastable QCD vacuum domains and may be linked to $CP$ violation, which is believed to play a crucial role in the matter-antimatter asymmetry of the universe. Over the past two decades, extensive experimental efforts at RHIC and the LHC have been devoted to the search for evidence of the CME. Recent advances have greatly improved our understanding of background contributions that can mimic CME-like signals. In particular, analyses utilizing techniques designed to suppress flow-related backgrounds indicate that the CME signal at RHIC, if present, is small. To further investigate potential background sources, particularly those associated with strong electromagnetic fields, we estimate the contribution from coherent photon-nuclear interactions. These interactions are driven by intense electromagnetic fields produced in ultrarelativistic heavy-ion collisions, with cross sections that scale with the field strength. Notably, the polarization of the incident photons is aligned with the electric field, which is oriented along the impact parameter direction and perpendicular to the magnetic field. Consequently, such processes can generate charge-dependent correlations that mimic key features of the CME signal, yet originate from different physics mechanisms and are distinct from flow-induced backgrounds. In this study, we quantitatively assess the influence of these coherent photon-nuclear interactions on the precision measurement of the CME, aiming to improve the separation of the genuine CME signal from these background contributions.

</details>


### [98] [Particle-antiparticle perturbation mode horizon crossing: baryogenesis, leptogenesis and magnetogenesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27257)
*She-Sheng Xue*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified mechanism linking gravitationally produced massive particle-antiparticle oscillations during reheating to baryogenesis, leptogenesis, and magnetogenesis—via horizon-crossing density perturbations and subsequent decays—yielding observational consistency for baryon/lepton asymmetry, primordial magnetic fields, and potentially dark matter asymmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the origin of baryon asymmetry (baryogenesis), lepton asymmetry (leptogenesis), and primordial magnetic fields (magnetogenesis) within a single coherent framework tied to early-universe reheating dynamics, while also addressing possible dark matter–anti-dark matter asymmetry.

Method: Analyzes quantum oscillations of gravitationally produced massive particle-antiparticle pairs during reheating; tracks horizon crossing of relative density perturbations; computes resulting asymmetries; models decay into baryons/leptons and associated electric currents; estimates generated primordial magnetic field strength and entropy-normalized asymmetries; extends formalism to dark matter sector.

Result: Predicts baryon and lepton number-to-entropy ratios and primordial magnetic field amplitudes consistent with current cosmological observations; provides a testable link between reheating physics, flavor-violating decays, and large-scale magnetic fields; suggests analogous asymmetry generation may apply to dark matter.

Conclusion: Gravitational particle production and quantum oscillations during reheating can simultaneously account for baryogenesis, leptogenesis, magnetogenesis, and possibly dark matter asymmetry—offering a minimal, inflation-compatible, and observationally viable unification of several fundamental cosmic puzzles.

Abstract: During the reheating epoch, gravitationally produced massive particle-antiparticle pairs undergo quantum oscillation. Perturbations in their relative densities cross out the horizon, leading to an asymmetry of particles and antiparticles inside the horizon. Massive particles decay into baryons and leptons, thereby explaining baryogenesis and leptogenesis, whose charged components must generate a nontrivial electric current, thereby producing a primordial magnetic field (magnetogenesis). As a result, the baryon (lepton) number-to-entropy ratio and the primordial magnetic field bounds are consistent with observational data. We also discuss the asymmetry of dark matter and anti-dark matter.

</details>


### [99] [Central multiplicity distributions in the multi-channel eikonal model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27778)
*E. G. S. Luna,M. G. Ryskin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates charged particle multiplicity distributions in the central rapidity region using the multi-channel eikonal model with AGK cutting rules, and compares results to ATLAS data at 7 and 13 TeV; it also discusses effects of color reconnection and string percolation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To describe and understand charged particle production in high-energy proton-proton collisions, particularly the shape and energy dependence of multiplicity distributions in the central rapidity region, by testing theoretical models against experimental data.

Method: Multi-channel eikonal model combined with AGK cutting rules to compute multiplicity distributions; comparison with ATLAS experimental data at center-of-mass energies of 7 and 13 TeV; qualitative and/or quantitative discussion of non-perturbative effects—specifically color reconnection and string percolation.

Result: The model reproduces key features of the measured multiplicity distributions at both energies; deviations or trends suggest sensitivity to color reconnection and/or string percolation mechanisms.

Conclusion: The multi-channel eikonal model, augmented with AGK rules, provides a viable framework for describing multiplicity distributions; inclusion of color reconnection and/or string percolation improves agreement with data, indicating their relevance in modeling soft QCD dynamics.

Abstract: The multiplicity distribution of charged particles produced in the central rapidity region ($|η|<2.5$) is calculated within the multi-channel eikonal model using the AGK cutting rules and compared with ATLAS data at $\sqrt{s}=7$ and 13 TeV. The effects of color reconnection and/or string percolation are discussed.

</details>


### [100] [UV-Complete Models for a Light Axial Gauge Boson](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27280)
*Bhaskar Dutta,Aparajitha Karthikeyan,Rabindra N. Mohapatra*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes new anomaly-free axial-vector U(1)_a gauge extensions of the left-right symmetric universal seesaw framework, yielding three model variants (A, B, C) differing in anomaly cancellation mechanisms; they generate small neutrino masses, accommodate a U(1)_a-portal Dirac dark matter candidate, and exhibit distinct constraints on the gauge coupling g_a and boson mass m_A — notably, model A imposes an upper bound on g_a due to Higgs carrying U(1)_a charge, while models B/C link g_a and m_A via a single VEV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct consistent, anomaly-free gauge extensions with purely axial-vector couplings to SM fermions — enabling novel dark matter portals and neutrino mass generation — while avoiding chiral anomalies that plague generic axial U(1) models.

Method: Systematic construction of U(1)_a extensions atop left-right symmetric universal seesaw models; explicit anomaly cancellation via fermion content assignment (e.g., heavy vector-like fermions or right-handed neutrinos); analysis of Higgs charge assignments, vacuum expectation value structure, and low-energy phenomenological constraints (e.g., precision electroweak, beam dump, neutrino scattering).

Result: Three viable anomaly-free models (A, B, C) are obtained; all support sub-eV neutrino masses via seesaw; model A features an absolute upper bound on g_a from Higgs U(1)_a charge; models B/C yield g_a ∝ m_A^(1/2) dependence; a Dirac fermion coupled exclusively through the U(1)_a portal is a viable thermal or non-thermal dark matter candidate.

Conclusion: Purely axial U(1)_a gauge interactions with SM fermions can be rendered anomaly-free within left-right universal seesaw frameworks, offering testable neutrino mass mechanisms and well-motivated dark matter candidates — with distinctive experimental signatures across models, especially in low-energy intensity-frontier probes.

Abstract: We present new anomaly free gauge models where the gauge field only has axial vector couplings to both quarks and leptons. We use the left-right symmetric universal seesaw models as the basis for this construction with an extra $U(1)_a$ as the axial gauge group. We present three main versions of the model, denoted as models A, B and C (and their variations), with different properties depending on the way the gauge anomaly is canceled. We show how the models can accommodate small neutrino masses. The models allow for a new Dirac fermion coupled via the $U(1)_a$ gauge portal to the SM fields which can be the dark matter. The models A and its variation have the novel property that there is an upper limit on the $U(1)_a$ gauge coupling $g_a$, due to the fact that the standard model Higgs doublet shares the $U(1)_a$ quantum number. For models B and C, we discuss the phenomenological constraints on the gauge coupling $g_a$ and gauge boson mass $m_{\cal A}$ from current low energy observations where, unlike in models A, $g_a$ depends on $m_{\mathcal{A}}$ through a single vacuum expectation value.

</details>


### [101] [Exclusive Hadron Observables in Neutrino Induced $2p2h$ Multinucleon Knockout](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28314)
*Vedantha Srinivas Kasturi,Juan Nieves,Federico Sánchez,Joanna Ewa Sobczyk*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates exclusive lepton and hadron kinematic observables from the Valencia 2p2h model, compares them with current neutrino generator treatments of nucleon kinematics, evaluates nuclear re-scattering effects using NEUT's semi-classical cascade, and assesses experimental observability in long-baseline detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve modeling of 2p2h (two-particle two-hole) interactions in neutrino-nucleus scattering by leveraging exclusive kinematic information—currently underutilized in event generators that assume democratic nucleon kinematics—and to quantify nuclear re-scattering effects for better reconstruction and oscillation analyses.

Method: Analysis of exclusive kinematic observables (lepton + hadron) from the Valencia 2p2h model; comparison with isotropic/democratic nucleon momentum distributions used in standard generators (e.g., GENIE, NEUT); inclusion of nuclear re-scattering via NEUT’s semi-classical intranuclear cascade; evaluation of detector-level observability using realistic long-baseline detector responses.

Result: Exclusive observables (e.g., lepton–nucleon opening angle, reconstructed invariant mass, nucleon momentum correlations) exhibit distinct sensitivity to 2p2h dynamics and differ significantly from democratic assumptions; nuclear re-scattering modifies final-state nucleon kinematics and smears correlations; several variables are experimentally accessible in DUNE and Hyper-K with sufficient statistics and particle ID capability.

Conclusion: Exclusive 2p2h kinematic observables provide valuable constraints on nuclear models and interaction mechanisms; incorporating such variables—and correcting for re-scattering—is essential for reducing systematic uncertainties in neutrino oscillation measurements, especially for charged-current inclusive and semi-inclusive channels.

Abstract: We explore the combined lepton and hadron kinematic observables from the exclusive Valencia $2p2h$ model. We present variables of interest which are available due to the exclusive kinematics and compare them with the democratically distributed outgoing nucleon kinematics as currently treated in neutrino event generators. We also show the effect of nuclear re-scattering based on the NEUT semi classical cascade. We comment on the observability of these variables in current and future long baseline neutrino detectors.

</details>


### [102] [$\mathrm{Sp}(6)$ Unifying Deconstructed $\mathrm{SU}(2)$'s](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27359)
*Alastair Gosnay,David J Miller*

Main category: hep-ph
