TL;DR: This paper proposes an Sp(6)_L flavour-unified extension of the SM where three left-handed doublet generations are embedded into a single fundamental representation; it predicts new gauge bosons inducing flavour-changing processes, and derives updated experimental bounds on the intermediate SU(2)_L^3 breaking scale v_{12} using precision flavour observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the unresolved origin of flavour hierarchies in the Standard Model by introducing a high-scale flavour symmetry — specifically, unifying three copies of SU(2)_L into Sp(6)_L — thereby providing a framework for generation-unified dynamics and testable flavour-violating signatures.

Method: Constructs a deconstructed grand-flavour model with Sp(6)_L gauge symmetry at scale v_S, spontaneously broken to SU(2)_L^3 at scale v_{12}, then to diagonal SU(2)_L; computes tree-level contributions of new gauge bosons (triplets and singlets) to FCNC processes; performs global analysis of constraints from K^0–K̄^0, D^0–D̄^0 mixing, μ→3e, and μ–e conversion; maps exclusion contours in the (v_{12}, r) plane with r = v_S²/v_{12}².

Result: Derives lower bounds on v_{12}: ≥ 550 TeV for r ≈ 1 (strong unification), relaxing to ≥ 150 TeV for r ≳ 100 (decoupled unification); identifies neutral meson mixing and charged lepton flavour violation as dominant constraints; projects improved sensitivity from upcoming Mu3e and COMET experiments.

Conclusion: The Sp(6)_L unification framework is phenomenologically viable only if the SU(2)_L^3 breaking scale lies well above current collider limits — at multi-hundred-TeV scales — and remains testable via next-generation intensity-frontier experiments probing charged lepton flavour violation.

Abstract: Understanding the origin of flavour hierarchies in the Standard Model remains an open problem, motivating extensions with non-trivial flavour symmetries. We unify deconstructed weak isospin $\mathrm{SU}(2)_\mathrm{L}^3$ into an $\mathrm{Sp}(6)_\mathrm{L}$ symmetry at a high scale $v_S$. The three generations of Standard Model (SM) left-handed doublets are unified into a single fundamental representation of $\mathrm{Sp}(6)_\mathrm{L}$. In addition to two BSM triplets from the breaking of $\mathrm{SU}(2)_\mathrm{L}^3$, the enlarged symmetry predicts six additional gauge bosons below the unification scale: three $\mathrm{SU}(2)_\mathrm{L}$ triplets and three singlets, which induce flavour transitions in both the quark and lepton sectors, even in the presence of mass-gauge alignment. We derive updated bounds on the intermediate breaking scale $v_{12}$ of $\mathrm{SU}(2)_\mathrm{L}^3$ in the presence of the unification scale $v_S$, mapping exclusions in the $(v_{12}, r)$ parameter space with $r = v_S^2 / v_{12}^2$. The most stringent constraints arise from precision flavour observables involving first- and second-generation transitions, including neutral meson mixing ($K^0 - \bar{K}^0$, $D^0 - \bar{D}^0$), $μ\to 3e$ and $μ\to e$ conversion in nuclei. These measurements probe scales well beyond direct collider reach and imply $v_{12} \gtrsim 550 ~\mathrm{TeV}$ for small $r \approx 1$, while for $r\gtrsim100$ the bound relaxes to $v_{12} \gtrsim 150~\mathrm{TeV}$, recovering the limits of the $\mathrm{SU}(2)_\mathrm{L}^3$ model. Additionally we include projections from Mu3e and COMET-I and -II experiments which show promising further reach into the parameter space.

</details>


### [103] [Blocking Mesogenesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28330)
*Chaja Baruch,Gilly Elor,Jared M. Goldberg,Omer Shtaif,Yotam Soreq*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel 'morphing' mechanism—via a late-time phase transition—to alter dark sector particle masses, thereby kinematically suppressing forbidden meson and proton decays in Mesogenesis, evading current experimental constraints on CP violation and proton decay.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve fatal tensions in Mesogenesis: (i) required CP violation exceeds observed $A_{CP}$ in meson systems; (ii) $D$-meson-based models conflict with stringent proton lifetime bounds.

Method: Introduce a late-time cosmological phase transition that dynamically increases the mass of dark sector final states post-meson production, rendering meson → baryon + dark matter and proton → meson decays kinematically forbidden.

Result: The morphing mechanism relaxes collider and proton decay constraints, opening viable parameter space for Mesogenesis—particularly with $D$ mesons—while preserving baryogenesis and dark matter genesis via late decays.

Conclusion: Late-time mass morphing provides a theoretically consistent and experimentally viable escape from existing exclusions, revitalizing Mesogenesis as a unified explanation for baryon asymmetry and dark matter.

Abstract: Mechanisms of Mesogenesis generate the baryon asymmetry and dark matter of the Universe through late-time decays of Standard Model mesons into baryons and dark matter states. Utilizing the CP violation in the meson systems themselves, the resulting baryon asymmetry is directly controlled by collider observables, the CP asymmetry $A_{CP}$, and the branching fraction for the meson decays. Experimental probes of these decays place strong constraints on the amount of $CP$ violation required, placing it well above observed limits in meson mixing. Additionally, strong lower bounds on the proton lifetime seemingly rule out Mesogenesis mechanisms which use $D$ mesons. In this work, we propose to circumvent these constraints by ``morphing'' the mass of the dark sector particles using a late-time phase transition. The change in mass of the final decay products kinematically excludes meson and proton decays, relaxing the constraints on the model parameter space.

</details>


### [104] [Probe charmonium-nucleon interactions in high energy proton-proton collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27391)
*Jiaxing Zhao,Taesoo Song,Joerg Aichelin,Elena Bratkovskaya,Pol Bernard Gossiaux,Klaus Werner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses EPOS4+CATS to study charmonium production and charmonium-nucleon correlations in pp collisions, revealing a non-Gaussian emission source for charmonium-proton pairs—enabling direct femtoscopic extraction of their interaction—and showing that excited charmonium states significantly affect (even invert) the observed prompt J/ψ–proton correlation via feed-down.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable direct experimental access to the charmonium–nucleon interaction via femtoscopy by dynamically modeling the emission source and accounting for feed-down contributions from excited charmonium states.

Method: Combined EPOS4 (event generator with collective dynamics) and CATS (correlation analysis tool for source and interaction reconstruction); dynamically generated non-Gaussian emission source for charmonium–proton pairs; included both ground (e.g., J/ψ) and excited (e.g., ψ', χc) charmonium states with feed-down modeling.

Result: The charmonium–proton emission source is non-Gaussian; femtoscopic extraction of the charmonium–proton interaction becomes feasible; excited charmonium states induce large (even negative) feed-down contributions to the prompt J/ψ–proton correlation, indicating stronger interactions than for ground states.

Conclusion: Feed-down from excited charmonium states must be rigorously accounted for in interpreting charmonium–nucleon correlation data; the non-Gaussian source enables model-independent constraints on charmonium–nucleon scattering parameters via femtoscopy.

Abstract: We investigate charmonium production and the charmonium-nucleon correlation function in pp collisions using the EPOS4+CATS framework. For the first time, the emission source of charmonium-proton pairs is dynamically generated and found to be non-Gaussian. This enables a femtoscopic extraction of the charmonium-proton interaction directly from experimental correlation functions. Both ground and excited charmonium states are included. We found that the excited states induce sizable uncertainties even negative in the observed prompt $J/ψ$-proton correlation through feed-down effects, reflecting their stronger interactions.

</details>


### [105] [Heavy-Flavor Fragmentation from HF-NRevo: Status, Prospects, and Intrinsic Charm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28389)
*Francesco Giovanni Celiberto,Francesca Lonigro*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: HF-NRevo is a novel NRQCD-based framework for heavy-hadron fragmentation at moderate-to-large pT, incorporating NLO short-distance coefficients, collinear evolution with variable-flavor-number scheme, and consistent heavy-flavor threshold treatment; it provides the NRFF1.0 (quarkonia), TQ4Q1.x/TQ4Q2.0 (tetraquarks) FF sets, enabling studies of medium-modified fragmentation in heavy-ion collisions and intrinsic charm in forward hadronic collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a consistent, perturbative description of heavy-hadron (especially quarkonium and exotic tetraquark) production via leading-power fragmentation across collider and heavy-ion environments, addressing limitations of fixed-flavor-number schemes and enabling precision probes of QCD dynamics and potential New Physics signatures.

Method: Combines next-to-leading-order (NLO) NRQCD short-distance coefficients with collinear factorization in a variable-flavor-number scheme (VFNS), enabling scale evolution across heavy-flavor thresholds; constructs fragmentation functions (FFs) for S-wave quarkonia (NRFF1.0) and fully heavy tetraquarks (TQ4Q1.x, TQ4Q2.0) in their leading NRQCD Fock states.

Result: Development of the HF-NRevo framework and associated FF families (NRFF1.0, TQ4Q1.x, TQ4Q2.0); enables first-principles, perturbative baseline for heavy-flavor fragmentation in pp, pA, and AA collisions; supports quantitative studies of jet quenching, medium-modified fragmentation, intrinsic charm, and exotic hadron formation.

Conclusion: HF-NRevo establishes a robust, threshold-resolved, evolution-compatible framework for heavy-flavor fragmentation that significantly extends the phenomenological scope of NRQCD beyond inclusive production—facilitating precision QCD tests at HL-LHC and future colliders, and offering new avenues to explore exotic hadrons and possible New Physics.

Abstract: We report on recent developments of the Heavy-Flavor Non-Relativistic evolution (HF-NRevo) scheme, a framework designed to describe heavy-hadron formation through leading-power fragmentation at moderate and large transverse momentum. The approach combines short-distance inputs obtained from next-to-leading-order NRQCD calculations with collinear scale evolution in a variable-flavor-number scheme, ensuring a consistent treatment of heavy-flavor thresholds and partonic hierarchies. Within this setup we have constructed the NRFF1.0 family of fragmentation functions for $S$-wave heavy quarkonia in their leading NRQCD Fock states. We discuss prospective applications of the HF-NRevo framework in the heavy-ion environment, where it can provide a perturbative baseline for investigating medium-induced modifications of heavy-flavor fragmentation. Its explicit treatment of partonic channels and heavy-flavor thresholds makes it particularly suitable for exploring jet-quenching sensitivity, energy-loss mechanisms, and the emergence of medium-modified fragmentation patterns in the quark-gluon plasma. The HF-NRevo scheme has also been extended to the exotic sector through the TQ4Q1.x and newly released TQ4Q2.0 fragmentation sets, which describe the formation of fully heavy tetraquarks in multiple quantum configurations. These developments open a novel pathway to study quarkoniumlike states and to probe the intrinsic charm content of the proton in forward hadron-collision environments. Altogether, this program broadens the phenomenological reach of heavy-flavor fragmentation studies at the HL-LHC and future collider facilities, opening access to previously unexplored aspects of QCD and potential portals to New Physics.

</details>


### [106] [News from Extended Scalar Sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27636)
*Tania Robens*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a concise overview of extended scalar sector phenomenology, focusing on light scalars at Higgs factories and the Inert Doublet Model's discovery prospects at lepton colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To review and highlight recent developments in phenomenology of extended scalar sectors—particularly models with light scalars relevant for Higgs factory experiments—and to assess the detectability of the Inert Doublet Model at future lepton colliders.

Method: Literature review and phenomenological analysis, emphasizing theoretical predictions and collider sensitivity studies for the Inert Doublet Model at lepton colliders.

Result: Summarizes current understanding and projected discovery reach for the Inert Doublet Model at upcoming lepton colliders; identifies key signatures and experimental constraints.

Conclusion: The Inert Doublet Model remains a viable and testable dark matter candidate at lepton colliders, especially in scenarios with light inert scalars accessible at Higgs factories.

Abstract: In this proceeding contribution, I give a short overview on selected topics regarding extended scalar sector phenomenology. After a short overview on extended scalar sectors with light scalars at Higgs factories, I concentrate on the Inert Doublet model and recent studies exploring its discovery potential at lepton colliders.

</details>


### [107] [Structure-dependent radiative corrections to $e^+ e^- \to π^+ π^- γ$ in the GVMD approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28621)
*Carlo M. Carloni Calame,Marco Ghilardi,Andrea Gurgone,Guido Montagna,Mauro Moretti,Oreste Nicrosini,Fulvio Piccinini,Francesco P. Ucci*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes next-to-leading-order radiative corrections to e+e−→π+π−γ using a generalized vector meson dominance model for the pion form factor in loop integrals, and compares results with scalar QED factorisation to assess model dependence in radiative return analyses at flavour factories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the uncertainty arising from the modeling of the pion-photon interaction in radiative return experiments—particularly for precision measurements of the hadronic cross section e+e−→π+π−—by consistently including non-perturbative pion structure in NLO radiative corrections to e+e−→π+π−γ.

Method: One-loop calculation incorporating the pion electromagnetic form factor via the generalized vector meson dominance (GVMD) model; treats final-state radiation and initial-final state interference at NLO; contrasts with naive factorised scalar QED.

Result: Predictions for experimentally relevant observables in e+e−→π+π−γ show deviations from the scalar QED factorisation approach, highlighting the importance of consistent inclusion of pion structure in radiative corrections.

Conclusion: The GVMD-based NLO calculation provides a more realistic estimate of radiative corrections than scalar QED factorisation, enabling improved control over systematic uncertainties in radiative return analyses at flavour factories.

Abstract: We compute the radiative corrections to the process of two-pion production in association with a hard photon in $e^+ e^-$ annihilation by taking into account the non-perturbative structure of the pion in the one-loop calculation. For this purpose, we adopt the generalised vector meson dominance model to insert the pion form factor in loop integrals for the treatment of final-state radiation and initial-final state interference at next-to-leading order. We compare our predictions with the results of the naive factorised scalar QED approach for experimentally relevant observables in the measurement of the $e^+ e^- \to π^+ π^- γ$ process. The computation extends previous results obtained for the energy scan process $e^+ e^- \to π^+ π^-$ and can be used to quantify the uncertainty due to the model describing the pion-photon interaction in radiative return experiments at flavour factories.

</details>


### [108] [Absence of Quadratic-Order Sensitivity to Small Neutrino Mass Splittings in Disappearance Measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27681)
*Sanjeev Kumar Verma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a limitation in neutrino disappearance analyses where small mass-squared splittings (Δm²) become statistically indistinguishable from smooth spectral uncertainties when modeled with profiled nuisance parameters—because the leading oscillatory distortion is quadratic in Δm² and can be absorbed by flexible systematic deformation functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and quantify the loss of sensitivity to small Δm² in binned neutrino oscillation analyses due to degeneracy between oscillation-induced spectral distortions and smooth systematic uncertainties.

Method: Analytical perturbative expansion in the small-phase limit; examination of how quadratic-order oscillation effects interact with profiled nuisance parameters representing smooth spectral shape uncertainties in χ² fits.

Result: Demonstrates that, under conditions where nuisance deformation functions span the energy dependence of the quadratic oscillation distortion, the profiled χ² becomes insensitive to Δm² at quadratic order—rendering small splittings unidentifiable without higher-order effects or constraints on spectral freedom.

Conclusion: Standard binned likelihood or χ² analyses with smooth, profiled systematics suffer an intrinsic sensitivity floor for small Δm²; robust extraction requires either higher-statistics data enabling higher-order effects, physically motivated regularization of nuisance space, or alternative analysis strategies less vulnerable to this degeneracy.

Abstract: Neutrino disappearance measurements using binned reconstructed-energy spectra exhibit a regime in which small mass-squared splittings become unidentifiable at quadratic order when smooth spectral shape uncertainties are represented by profiled nuisance parameters in the fit. In the small-phase limit, the oscillation-induced modification of the detected spectrum is quadratic in the mass-squared splitting and produces a smooth deformation of the reconstructed-energy distribution. If the nuisance deformation functions used in the fit can reproduce this energy dependence across the fitted bins, the quadratic oscillation-induced distortion can be absorbed by the systematic deformation space and the profiled chi-squared remains unchanged at this order. Sensitivity to the mass-squared splitting then arises only from higher-order oscillation effects or from restrictions imposed on the allowed smooth spectral freedom.

</details>


### [109] [Large Hadronic Effects in $B \to K^* μμ$?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27753)
*Wolfgang Altmannshofer,Samuel G. Christensen,Peter Stangl*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes recent LHCb results on the $B \to K^* \mu^+\mu^-$ decay, focusing on the $P_5^\prime$ anomaly and the newly hinted non-zero $S_7$ observable. It argues that a non-zero $S_7$ requires sizable hadronic effects with a strong phase, which—under plausible assumptions—can also explain $P_5^\prime$. The paper proposes the direct CP asymmetry as a clean probe of new physics and demonstrates via combined fits that strong constraints can be placed on the imaginary parts of new physics Wilson coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the persistent $P_5^\prime$ anomaly with the newly observed non-zero $S_7$ in $B \to K^* \mu^+\mu^-$ decays, and to assess whether hadronic effects alone can explain both, or whether new physics contributions—particularly their CP-violating (imaginary) components—are still required and testable.

Method: Theoretical analysis using angular observables in $B \to K^* \mu^+\mu^-$ decays; decomposition of amplitudes into short-distance (Wilson coefficients) and long-distance (hadronic) contributions; inclusion of strong phases from hadronic rescattering; global fit combining hadronic parameters and Wilson coefficients; use of direct CP asymmetry as a null-test observable sensitive only to weak-phase differences (i.e., Im[C_i]).

Result: A non-zero $S_7$ unambiguously signals nontrivial hadronic effects with a strong phase; under reasonable assumptions, these same effects quantitatively account for the $P_5^\prime$ anomaly; direct CP asymmetry remains a theoretically clean probe; combined fits yield stringent upper bounds on the imaginary parts of new physics Wilson coefficients (e.g., Im[C_9^\text{NP}], Im[C_7^\text{NP}]).

Conclusion: The $S_7$ observation strengthens the case for significant hadronic contributions in $B \to K^* \mu^+\mu^-$, potentially eliminating the need for new physics to explain $P_5^\prime$; however, CP-violating observables retain high sensitivity to imaginary NP Wilson coefficients, allowing robust discrimination between pure hadronic explanations and NP scenarios with CP violation.

Abstract: Recent results from LHCb have confirmed the long-standing $P_5^\prime$ anomaly, an intriguing discrepancy in the angular distribution of the $B \to K^* μ^+μ^-$ decay that might be a sign of new physics. In addition, the new results hint at a non-zero value for $S_7$, another observable that characterizes the $B \to K^* μ^+μ^-$ angular distribution. We stress that a non-zero $S_7$ cannot be explained by heavy new physics but instead necessarily requires a sizable hadronic effect that introduces a strong phase. We argue that, under plausible assumptions, the hadronic effect is of the correct size to also explain $P_5^\prime$. The direct CP asymmetry in $B \to K^* μ^+μ^-$ emerges in principle as a clean probe of new physics in such a scenario. We show that a combined fit of hadronic parameters and Wilson coefficients retains sensitivity to new physics and we find strong bounds on imaginary parts of new physics Wilson coefficients.

</details>


### [110] [Associated Higgs production in lepton-photon collisions at FCC-ee](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27822)
*Pankaj Agrawal,Biswajit Das,Tousik Samui*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a novel Higgs production channel e±γ → ν̄e(νe)HW± at lepton colliders, enabling clean measurement of the WWH coupling; shows observability at FCC-ee (365 GeV and 500 GeV) using an in-house Monte Carlo generator incorporating both photon and lepton PDFs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To precisely probe the SM Higgs sector—especially the WWH coupling—in the post-HL-LHC era, leveraging clean environments of future lepton colliders and avoiding dominant backgrounds plaguing conventional channels.

Method: Developed an in-house Monte Carlo event generator that consistently includes beamstrahlung-induced photon distributions and electron/positron parton distribution functions (PDFs); performed cross-section calculations and signal-background analysis for e±γ → ν̄e(νe)HW± at FCC-ee energies (365 GeV and 500 GeV).

Result: The process has sufficiently large cross-section and low background to be observable: detectable at 365 GeV with full FCC-ee luminosity, and within a few months at 500 GeV; enables direct extraction of the WWH coupling with high precision.

Conclusion: The e±γ → ν̄e(νe)HW± channel is a viable, background-suppressed probe of the WWH coupling at future lepton colliders; the developed simulation framework establishes a foundation for realistic modeling of mixed lepton–photon initiated processes.

Abstract: Following the HL-LHC era, proposed lepton colliders highlight the need to study various important Higgs boson production mechanisms to precisely probe the Standard Model Higgs sector. We propose a novel mechanism $e^\pm γ\rightarrow {\bar ν}_e (ν_e) H W^\pm$, which can be useful to study Higgs boson properties. This channel is relatively free from the background and can be used to measure the Higgs boson properties, in particular $WWH$ coupling. We examine the viability of this production mechanism. We show that the process can be observed at the planned FCC-ee with the center-of-mass energy of 365 GeV. At the center-of-mass energy of 500 GeV, the process can be observed within a few months of the operation. We use an in-house Monte Carlo event generator that simultaneously incorporates the photon distribution and electron/positron distribution. Our work is also a step towards realistic simulations of lepton- and photon-initiated processes at lepton colliders.

</details>


### [111] [Full energy fraction and angular dependence of medium-induced splittings in the large-$N_c$ limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27846)
*Carlota Andres,Fabio Dominguez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a semi-analytical framework for medium-induced 1→2 parton splittings in QGP, fully differential in energy fraction z and angle θ, using large-Nc and harmonic oscillator approximations; it also introduces and validates an improved semi-hard approximation (ISHA) that outperforms the standard SHA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the soft-gluon approximation and quantitatively describe jet substructure modifications in heavy-ion collisions by modeling medium-induced splittings with full z and θ dependence, incorporating multiple scatterings.

Method: Analytical evaluation of path integrals for medium-induced 1→2 splittings in the large-Nc limit under the harmonic oscillator approximation; derivation and extension of the semi-hard approximation to include leading 1/E corrections (ISHA); comparative validation against the exact large-Nc-HO result.

Result: A computationally efficient semi-analytical expression for double-differential splitting kernels; demonstration that the standard semi-hard approximation (SHA) fails across most of phase space, while the improved version (ISHA) is robust for sufficiently energetic partons.

Conclusion: Full double-differential treatment of medium-induced splittings is feasible analytically in the large-Nc-HO limit, and ISHA provides a practical, accurate alternative to SHA for phenomenological applications in jet quenching.

Abstract: Jets produced in relativistic heavy-ion collisions are modified by their interactions with the quark-gluon plasma (QGP), making jet substructure observables sensitive probes of QGP dynamics. A quantitative description of these modifications requires understanding how the medium affects elementary parton splittings with full dependence on both their energy fraction $z$ and splitting angle $θ$, beyond the widely used soft emitted-gluon approximation. Here, we study medium-induced $1 \to 2$ splittings double-differential in $z$ and $θ$, with full resummation of multiple scatterings, and show that in the large-$N_c$ limit and under the harmonic oscillator (HO) approximation, all path integrals can be evaluated analytically for any splitting channel, providing a computationally efficient semi-analytical result. We also revisit the semi-hard approximation (SHA), extending it to include leading corrections in inverse powers of the partons energies, which we denote the improved semi-hard approximation (ISHA), and assess its validity through a comparison with the large-$N_c$-HO results. Our analysis shows that while the SHA is found to be unreliable across most of phase space, even for high-energy emitters, the ISHA provides a robust approximation for splittings where all partons are sufficiently energetic.

</details>


### [112] [Study of neutrinophilic low-mass dark matter mediated by pseudoscalar](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28034)
*Zhuo Zhang,Lian-Bao Jia,Reyes J. F. Eduardo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies a neutrinophilic low-mass dark matter model mediated by a pseudoscalar, where DM annihilates predominantly into invisible neutrinos—evading conventional indirect detection. It imposes stringent constraints using Lyman-α forest data, astrophysical neutrinos (AGN, SNe), direct detection (nucleon/electron scattering), and invisible Higgs decays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard indirect detection fails when DM annihilates dominantly into neutrinos (invisible final states); thus, complementary probes are needed to constrain neutrinophilic low-mass DM models.

Method: The authors perform a multi-probe phenomenological analysis: compute constraints from Lyman-α forest (structure formation), high-energy astrophysical neutrinos (from AGN and core-collapse supernovae), direct detection experiments (scattering off nucleons and electrons), and LHC measurements of invisible Higgs decays.

Result: All considered observables yield stringent, complementary bounds on the model’s parameter space; in particular, neutrino-line searches, supernova neutrino self-interactions, collider invisible Higgs decays, and Lyman-α provide the most constraining limits.

Conclusion: Neutrinophilic low-mass DM is highly constrained; viable parameter space is narrow and testable via next-generation neutrino telescopes, direct detection experiments with electron recoil sensitivity, and precision Higgs physics at colliders.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate a neutrinophilic low-mass dark matter model mediated by a pseudoscalar particle. Since dark matter lacks Standard Model gauge charges, new interactions are required to connect it to the visible sector. Traditional indirect detection searches for annihilation products, such as cosmic rays, become ineffective when the annihilation predominantly yields invisible neutrinos. In our model, the present-day annihilation cross section into neutrinos (manifesting as a neutrino line) falls below current indirect detection limits. We therefore constrain the model using complementary probes: the Lyman-$α$ forest, high-energy astrophysical neutrinos from active galactic nuclei and supernovae, direct detection via nucleon and electron scattering, and invisible Higgs decays. These observables provide stringent and multifaceted constraints on neutrinophilic dark matter interactions in the low-mass regime. Our results indicate that searches for the neutrino line from dark matter annihilations, neutrino self-interactions from supernovae and collider signatures, and invisible Higgs decays offer critical tests for the model's parameter space.

</details>


### [113] [Implications of the muon anomalous magnetic moment in a Doublet Left-Right Symmetric Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28041)
*M. Zeleny-Mora,R. Gaitán-Lozano,R. Martinez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes one-loop contributions to the muon anomalous magnetic moment (a_μ) in the Doublet Left-Right Symmetric Model (DLRSM) with inverse seesaw neutrino masses, and derives lower mass bounds on new particles (W′, Z′, heavy neutrinos) from agreement with experimental Δa_μ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain the parameter space of the DLRSM — particularly the right-handed symmetry breaking scale v_R and masses of new gauge bosons, scalars, and heavy neutrinos — using precision measurement of the muon g−2 anomaly as a probe of beyond-Standard-Model physics.

Method: Systematic computation of all one-loop diagrams (VFF, SFF, FVV, FSS topologies) involving extended gauge bosons (W′, Z′), scalar sector (H₃⁰, A₁⁰, H_R^±, H_L^±), and ISS-generated heavy neutrinos; uses Casas–Ibarra parametrization to link neutrino mixing to physical observables; imposes experimental Δa_μ bound to derive exclusion limits.

Result: v_R ≲ 1 TeV is excluded; under manifest left-right symmetry (g_R = g_L): m_W′ ≳ 325 GeV, m_Z′ ≳ 385 GeV, m_N ≳ 700 GeV; with g_R ≠ g_L: m_W′ ≳ 1625 GeV, m_Z′ ≳ 1650 GeV.

Conclusion: The muon g−2 anomaly provides stringent, model-dependent lower bounds on new physics scales in the DLRSM, especially disfavoring low-scale left-right symmetry breaking unless coupling asymmetry is introduced.

Abstract: We compute the complete set of one-loop contributions to the muon anomalous magnetic moment, $a_μ=(g-2)_μ/2$, in the Doublet Left-Right Symmetric Model (DLRSM), based on the gauge group $SU(2)_{L}\otimes SU(2)_{R}\otimes U(1)_{B-L}$ with neutrino masses generated via the inverse seesaw (ISS) mechanism. We evaluate all four one-loop topologies VFF, SFF, FVV, and FSS arising from the extended gauge bosons ($W^{\prime}$, $Z^{\prime}$), the new scalar sector ($H_{3}^{0}$, $A_{1}^{0}$, $H_{R}^{\pm}$, $H_{L}^{\pm}$), and the heavy neutrino spectrum generated by the ISS mechanism, using the Casas--Ibarra parametrization to express the neutrino mixing in terms of physical observables. Imposing the experimental bound on $Δa_μ$, we establish that $v_{R}\lesssim1$ TeV is excluded, implying lower bounds $m_{W^{\prime}}\gtrsim325$ GeV, $m_{Z^{\prime}}\gtrsim385$ GeV, and $m_{N}\gtrsim700$ GeV under the manifest left-right symmetry condition $g_{R}=g_{L}$. Relaxing this condition to $g_{R}\neq g_{L}$ strengthens the gauge boson bounds to $m_{W^{\prime}}\gtrsim1625$ GeV and $m_{Z^{\prime}}\gtrsim1650$ GeV.

</details>


### [114] [On the predictivity of axion dark matter in the presence of Peccei-Quinn breaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28098)
*Michael Zantedeschi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that the QCD axion's viability as a dark matter candidate is undermined when small explicit Peccei-Quinn symmetry breaking—parameterized by a mass scale μ—becomes dynamically relevant before the QCD phase transition, altering axion string-wall network evolution and relic abundance dependence beyond just the decay constant f_a.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reassess the predictive power of the post-inflationary QCD axion as dark matter in light of potentially viable, subdominant Peccei-Quinn (PQ) symmetry breaking effects that are not ruled out by the strong CP constraint but may significantly affect early-universe dynamics.

Method: Analytical and semi-analytical treatment of axion field dynamics, including thermal suppression of the QCD axion mass at high temperatures, evolution of the axion string-wall network under modified early-time potential (with explicit PQ-breaking term ∝ μ²), and computation of relic abundance dependence on both f_a and μ.

Result: The axion string-wall network annihilates earlier due to the μ-induced mass term; relic abundance becomes sensitive to μ as well as f_a, eroding the standard QCD axion DM prediction band and potentially covering the entire conventional parameter space.

Conclusion: The post-inflationary QCD axion is not generically predictive for dark matter when dynamically relevant explicit PQ breaking exists—challenging the robustness of standard axion DM forecasts and calling for UV model-dependent scrutiny of PQ symmetry restoration and breaking history.

Abstract: It is shown that the post-inflationary quantum chromodynamics (QCD) axion need not be a predictive candidate for dark matter whenever small explicit Peccei-Quinn symmetry breaking becomes dynamically relevant before the QCD transition. Although strongly constrained by the strong CP bound, such breaking remains phenomenologically viable and introduces a mass scale $μ$ that can control the early-time dynamics, as the QCD contribution to the axion mass is thermally suppressed at high temperatures. In this case, the axion string-wall network annihilates earlier, and the relic abundance is no longer primarily set by QCD dynamics alone, but instead depends on $μ$, in addition to $f_a$, the axion decay constant. This effect overlaps with the parameter space relevant for QCD axion dark matter and, depending on ultraviolet parameters and initial conditions, can extend across it entirely.

</details>


### [115] [$f_0(980)$ production from $K\bar{K}$ coalescence in pp collisions at $\sqrt{s}=5.02$ TeV within UrQMD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28131)
*Phacharatouch Chaimongkon,Krittaporn Anukulkitch,Pornrad Srisawad,Natthaphat Thongyoo,Sukanya Sombun,Ayut Limphirat,Yu-Peng Yan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the UrQMD transport model with a $K\bar{K}$ coalescence afterburner to simulate $f_0(980)$ production in pp collisions at 5.02 TeV; it finds that a $\Delta p \approx 0.365$ GeV/c momentum window optimally reproduces ALICE data, supporting a late-stage $K\bar{K}$ molecular interpretation of $f_0(980)$. 


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the molecular $K\bar{K}$ nature of the $f_0(980)$ scalar meson by modeling its production via hadronic coalescence near kinetic freeze-out in small-system (pp) collisions, where quark-gluon plasma formation is unlikely.

Method: UrQMD transport model tuned to reproduce bulk charged-kaon $p_T$ spectra, coupled with a $K\bar{K}$ coalescence afterburner that assigns accepted $K\bar{K}$ pairs equally to $f_0(980)$ and $a_0(980)$ channels; systematic variation of coalescence momentum window $\Delta p$ and interpolation to determine optimal value.

Result: Best agreement with ALICE $f_0(980)$ $p_T$ spectrum and integrated yield achieved at interpolated $\Delta p^* \approx 0.365$ GeV/c; model reasonably describes data within constrained hadronic coalescence framework.

Conclusion: The $f_0(980)$ production in pp collisions is consistent with a late-stage, loosely bound $K\bar{K}$ molecular configuration formed near kinetic freeze-out—supporting a hadronic origin over a compact tetraquark or $q\bar{q}$ structure in this regime.

Abstract: We investigate the production of the scalar meson $f_0(980)$ in proton--proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}=5.02$~TeV using the Ultra-relativistic Quantum Molecular Dynamics (UrQMD) transport model supplemented with a $K\bar{K}$ coalescence afterburner. After conservatively tuning the UrQMD string-fragmentation parameters, the model reproduces the bulk charged-kaon production in the low-to-intermediate transverse-momentum region, providing the kaon phase-space distribution used as input for the coalescence calculation. In the present implementation, both charged and neutral kaon--antikaon pairs are considered, and each accepted $K\bar{K}$ pair is assigned to the isoscalar $f_0(980)$ and isovector $a_0(980)$ channels with an equal Monte Carlo probability. Using the updated integration analysis, we find that $Δp=0.4$~GeV/$c$ gives the best directly simulated agreement with the ALICE $p_T$ spectrum and integrated yield, while a linear interpolation between the neighboring points at $Δp=0.3$ and $0.4$~GeV/$c$ yields an interpolated optimum of $Δp^{\ast}\approx0.365$~GeV/$c$. Within this constrained hadronic coalescence framework, the measured $f_0(980)$ production is reasonably described, and the results are consistent with interpreting the $f_0(980)$ as a late-stage $K\bar{K}$ molecular configuration formed near kinetic freeze-out in small collision systems.

</details>


### [116] [Phenomenology developments in UPC: $γγ\to γγ$ scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28275)
*Paweł Jucha,Antoni Szczurek*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores extensions to current studies of γγ→γγ scattering in ultraperipheral heavy-ion collisions, including lower diphoton invariant masses, inelastic photon–nucleon couplings (contributing ~20–30% without cuts), associated neutron production as a probe for inelastic contributions, and single-photon production (AA → AAγ); it advocates dedicated analyses with future detectors (FOCAL, ALICE 3) and existing experimental cuts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend current theoretical and experimental understanding of light-by-light scattering beyond coherent γγ→γγ processes, by exploring lower-mass regimes, inelastic contributions (photon–nucleon vs. photon–nucleus coupling), and related signatures (neutron emission, single photons) that may reveal new dynamics or serve as diagnostics for background contamination.

Method: Theoretical cross section calculations for coherent and inelastic γγ→γγ scattering, associated neutron production, and single-photon emission (AA → AAγ) in ultraperipheral collisions; estimates of inelastic contribution fractions (~20–30%) and assessment of survival under existing experimental cuts; comparison of predictions with potential observables for discrimination.

Result: Inelastic γγ→γγ contributions are sizable (~20–30% before cuts) and may persist under current experimental selections; associated neutron emission provides a clean observable to constrain inelastic contamination; single-photon cross sections are quantified for key mechanisms; FOCAL and ALICE 3 are identified as promising for low-mass and high-sensitivity studies.

Conclusion: Inelastic γγ scattering—previously neglected—must be included in precision light-by-light analyses; associated neutrons and single photons offer complementary probes; future detector capabilities will enable systematic separation of coherent and inelastic components, improving tests of QED and potential BSM physics.

Abstract: We discuss several possible extensions of present studies of $γγ\to γγ$ scattering in ultraperipheral heavy ion collisions. One of the possible extensions are studies for lower diphoton invariant masses (smaller transverse momenta). There new mechanisms may show up. This includes possible studies with future FOCAL and ALICE 3 detectors. So far only coherent $γγ\to γγ$ processes, when initial photons couple to nuclei, were sudied theoretically. Recently we proposed to study also inelastic processes, when initial photons couple to individual nucleons. Without special cuts the corresponding processes are of the order of 20 - 30 \%. A study to which extent the inelastic processes survive with the present cuts used for the $γγ\to γγ$ studies is an interesting question. They can be of interest by itself and dedicated studies are suggested. We calculate also cross section for associated neutron production. Deviation from our predictions could signal presence of the inelastic contribution. Finally we discuss production of single photons in UPC ($A A \to A A γ$). Corresponding cross sections for selected mechanisms are presented.

</details>


### [117] [Missing energy signatures of inelastic magnetic dipole DM at NA64e](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28278)
*Sergei N. Gninenko,N. V. Krasnikov,I. V. Voronchikhin,D. V. Kirpichnikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the production rate and projected sensitivity of the NA64e experiment at CERN to inelastic dark matter (iDM) pairs via photon-mediated dipole interactions in electron–nucleus scattering, including both bremsstrahlung-like and heavy vector meson decay channels, extending reach to light iDM with small mass splittings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe sub-GeV thermally produced inelastic dark matter (iDM), an attractive candidate in beyond-Standard-Model theories, using high-intensity electron beams at NA64e—particularly in parameter regions inaccessible to prior experiments.

Method: Computes iDM pair production cross sections for $e^- N \to e^- N \chi_1 \bar\chi_0$ via magnetic dipole coupling to virtual photons; includes both direct bremsstrahlung-like emission ($e^- N \to e^- N \gamma^* (\to \chi_1 \bar\chi_0)$) and indirect production via off-shell heavy vector mesons ($\gamma^* N \to N V (\to \chi_1 \bar\chi_0)$); projects sensitivity assuming $\sim 10^{13}$ 100 GeV electrons on target.

Result: NA64e can explore new iDM parameter space—specifically for mass splittings $\Delta \simeq 5 \times 10^{-2}$ and light ground-state masses $m_{\chi_0} \lesssim 100~\text{MeV}$—by incorporating vector meson decays alongside standard bremsstrahlung.

Conclusion: The inclusion of heavy vector meson contributions significantly enhances NA64e’s discovery potential for light, inelastic dark matter, making it a powerful probe of previously unconstrained iDM scenarios with modest mass splitting and dipole-mediated couplings.

Abstract: Some extensions of the Standard Model consider inelastic dark matter (iDM) as an attractive candidate for sub-GeV DM of thermal origin that could be detected at modern accelerators. In the present paper, we calculate the production rate of iDM pairs $χ_{1} \barχ_0$ interacting with the ordinary photon via dipole magnetic moment in the reaction of high-energy electron scattering on nuclei, $e^- N \to e^- N χ_{1} \barχ_0$, in the NA64e experiment at the CERN SPS. We derive the projected sensitivity of NA64e to such particles assuming of $\simeq10^{13}$ 100 GeV electrons on target. We also show, that incorporating heavy vector meson decays, $γ^* N \to N V (\to χ_1 \barχ_0)$, alongside bremsstrahlung-like emission of inelastic dark matter pairs, $e^- N \to e^- N γ^* (\to χ_1 \barχ_0)$, will allow NA64e to probe previously unexplored regions of the iDM parameter space, in particular for modest mass splittings, $Δ\simeq 5 \times 10^{-2}$, and relatively light masses, $m_{χ_0} \lesssim 100~\mbox{MeV}$.

</details>


### [118] [Precise predictions for trilinear Higgs couplings and Higgs pair production in extended scalar sectors with anyH3 and anyHH](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28296)
*Henning Bahl,Johannes Braathen,Martin Gabelmann,Kateryna Radchenko,Georg Weiglein*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces anyBSM, a public tool for automated one-loop calculation of trilinear scalar couplings—including Higgs self-coupling—in arbitrary renormalisable BSM theories, and presents the new anyHH module for di-Higgs production in gluon fusion, with applications to multi-resonance scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precise determination of the Higgs trilinear self-coupling is critical for probing the Higgs potential structure and testing BSM physics (e.g., electroweak baryogenesis); radiative corrections from extended scalar sectors significantly alter these couplings, necessitating robust, scheme-flexible loop-level calculations.

Method: Development and implementation of the public code anyBSM for fully automated, momentum-dependent, one-loop computation of all trilinear scalar couplings in generic renormalisable quantum field theories; extension with the anyHH module for NLO QCD-inclusive di-Higgs production via gluon fusion in selected BSM models.

Result: anyBSM v2 enables consistent, user-defined renormalisation schemes and full momentum dependence for trilinear couplings; anyHH demonstrates enhanced sensitivity to BSM resonances (e.g., heavy scalars) in gg→hh cross sections, including interference effects beyond narrow-width approximation.

Conclusion: anyBSM and anyHH provide essential, publicly available infrastructure for precision Higgs phenomenology, bridging high-precision theory predictions with collider measurements to uncover indirect BSM signatures through trilinear scalar interactions.

Abstract: A central objective of future collider experiments is to probe the structure of the Higgs potential, which requires access to trilinear scalar couplings, in particular the self-coupling of the observed Higgs boson. While this coupling is fixed in the Standard Model (SM), it can receive sizable modifications in many Beyond the SM (BSM) scenarios, often connected to solutions of open problems such as the origin of the matter-antimatter asymmetry of the Universe. In theories with extended scalar sectors, radiative corrections involving additional scalar states can significantly affect both the Higgs self-coupling and other trilinear scalar interactions, with important consequences for predictions of physical observables. Precise theoretical calculations are therefore essential for the interpretation of precision Higgs measurements and for identifying indirect signatures of new physics. This contribution presents the latest version of the public tool anyBSM, which provides automated calculations of all trilinear scalar couplings at full one-loop order in arbitrary renormalisable theories, including full momentum dependence and flexible renormalisation-scheme choices. In addition, the new module anyHH for di-Higgs production in gluon fusion is discussed in several exemplary BSM models, including scenarios with multiple resonances.

</details>


### [119] [Combined analysis of the data on cross sections and spin density matrix elements for $K^*Σ$ photoproduction reactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28337)
*Aai-Chao Wang,Neng-Chang Wei,Fei Huang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends a prior effective Lagrangian analysis of γp → K*Σ reactions to include LEPS spin density matrix element data, finding two equally valid fits where Δ(1905)5/2⁺ is essential but κ exchange importance is ambiguous—contradicting prior claims of its dominance—and predicts P_σ at 8.5 GeV to resolve the ambiguity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To impose stricter constraints on the reaction mechanism of γp → K*Σ by incorporating high-precision spin density matrix element data from LEPS, beyond prior differential cross-section analyses, and to clarify the contested role of κ exchange in generating the parity spin asymmetry P_σ.

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach including s-channel resonances (N, Δ, Δ(1905)5/2⁺), t-channel exchanges (K, κ, K*), u-channel exchanges (Λ, Σ, Σ*), and a generalized contact term; simultaneous χ² fits to LEPS spin density matrix elements for γp → K*⁰Σ⁺ across E_γ = 1.85–2.96 GeV.

Result: Two statistically equivalent fits obtained: both require significant Δ(1905)5/2⁺ contribution, but κ exchange is large in one fit and negligible in the other—directly contradicting literature assertions that LEPS P_σ data uniquely favor dominant κ exchange; predictions for P_σ at E_γ = 8.5 GeV are provided to test this.

Conclusion: The role of κ exchange in γp → K*⁰Σ⁺ cannot be unambiguously determined from current LEPS data alone; the Δ(1905)5/2⁺ resonance is robustly required; future high-energy P_σ measurements (e.g., at 8.5 GeV) are critical to distinguish between competing dynamical interpretations.

Abstract: In our earlier work [Phys. Rev. C \textbf{98}, 045209 (2018)], we analyzed the differential cross-section data from the CLAS Collaboration for the reactions $γp \to K^{*+}Σ^0$ and $γp \to K^{*0} Σ^+$ using an effective Lagrangian approach. We found that a satisfactory description of the data required the inclusion of the $s$-channel $Δ(1905)5/2^+$ resonance, in addition to $t$-channel exchanges of $K$, $κ$, and $K^*$, $s$-channel contributions from nucleons ($N$) and $Δ$, $u$-channel exchanges of $Λ$, $Σ$, and $Σ^*$, and a generalized contact term. In the present work, we extend our analysis to incorporate the data on spin density matrix elements from the LEPS Collaboration for the $γp \to K^{*0}Σ^+$ reaction at photon energies $E_γ = 1.85$--$2.96$ GeV. Our goal is to impose more stringent constraints on the theoretical model and obtain a more reliable understanding of the reaction mechanisms. We obtain two fits that describe the experimental data equally well. In both fits, the $Δ(1905)5/2^+$ resonance plays an important role. However, the contribution from $t$-channel $κ$ exchange is significant in one fit but negligible in the other. This finding contradicts earlier claims in the literature that the LEPS parity spin asymmetry $P_σ$ data support a dominant role of $κ$ exchange in $γp \to K^{*0}Σ^+$. We also present predictions for $P_σ$ at $E_γ= 8.5$ GeV, which may help clarify whether $κ$ exchange is indeed dominant in this reaction.

</details>


### [120] [The force of attraction between nucleons due to vacuum fluctuation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28379)
*Anupam Ghosh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This letter calculates the quantum vacuum-induced interaction energy and force between two parallel metal plates using a meson field model, grounded in Quantum Hadrodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate Casimir-like interactions mediated by mesonic vacuum fluctuations—distinct from standard electromagnetic vacuum—within the framework of Quantum Hadrodynamics.

Method: Analytical derivation of interaction energy and force between two parallel metal plates based on quantum vacuum fluctuations of a meson field.

Result: Explicit expressions for the interaction energy and force arising from meson-field vacuum fluctuations between closely spaced parallel metal plates.

Conclusion: Meson-field vacuum fluctuations induce a measurable interaction between parallel metal plates, extending the Casimir effect paradigm beyond electromagnetism into nuclear-force-related quantum fields.

Abstract: In this letter, we derive the interaction energy and the force between two parallel metal plates, arising from quantum vacuum fluctuations when they are very close to each other. We consider the vacuum to be composed of a meson field. In Quantum Hadrodynamics, mesons are the carriers of the nuclear force.

</details>


### [121] [Removing the Cosmological Bound on the Axion Scale via Confinement During Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28620)
*Gia Dvali,Sophia Fitz,Lucy Komisel*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a mechanism for early axion relaxation via high-scale SU(5) confinement during inflation, eliminating cosmological upper bounds on the axion decay constant and enabling arbitrarily large f_a while preserving axion dark matter viability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the cosmological upper bound on the axion decay constant f_a — traditionally constrained by overproduction in the misalignment mechanism — by enabling early relaxation of the axion field before QCD confinement.

Method: Embeds the invisible axion within an SU(5) grand unified theory; analyzes two inflationary scenarios: (i) direct inflaton–gauge field coupling inducing early SU(5) confinement, and (ii) SU(5) symmetry restoration during inflation followed by RG-driven strong coupling and confinement; treats axion dynamics across PQ frameworks (DFSZ, KSVZ, two-form), including cases where the PQ scalar vanishes during inflation but the axion remains well-defined via the fermionic 't Hooft determinant phase.

Result: Demonstrates that early axion potential generation via SU(5) confinement dilutes axion energy density, lifting the f_a upper bound; confirms viability for all major axion models, including DFSZ (with quark-condensate–driven η′-like phase) and KSVZ/two-form variants; shows present-day axion remains a consistent cold dark matter candidate for arbitrarily large f_a.

Conclusion: Early axion relaxation through high-scale GUT confinement during inflation robustly decouples axion dark matter abundance from the decay constant, reconciling large-f_a axions with observational constraints and broadening the theoretically allowed parameter space.

Abstract: We implement the scenario of early relaxation of the axion via a high scale confinement within $SU(5)$ grand unified theory and study an epoch of strong QCD in inflationary cosmology. We consider scenarios in which, during inflation, the $SU(5)$ is either entirely or partially in the confining phase. This generates an early potential for the axion and dilutes its energy density removing any cosmological upper bound on the decay constant. We show that a phase of strong QCD can be realized by at least two mechanisms: 1) A direct coupling between the inflaton and the gauge fields and/or 2) by restoration of the $SU(5)$ symmetry during the inflationary epoch. In the latter case, strong coupling is already achieved via the RG running of the $SU(5)$ gauge coupling. We show that the mechanism works for all known realizations of the invisible axion idea: Peccei-Quinn (PQ) type formulations in which the anomalous global symmetry is realized via additional scalars (DFSZ) or heavy fermions (KSVZ) as well as the two-form gauge axion formulation based entirely on the QCD gauge redundancy without any anomalous global symmetry. Even if the expectation value of the PQ scalar vanishes during inflation, the axion is a well defined degree of freedom represented by the phase of the fermion 't Hooft determinant. For the DFSZ case, this phase is composed out of a condensate of the ordinary quarks, amounting to an early universe version of the $η'$-meson. In all considered scenarios, the present day axion can be a viable dark matter candidate for an arbitrarily large value of the decay constant.

</details>


### [122] [The $B^{(*)}\bar{K}^{(*)}$-coupled-channel system in the hidden-gauge approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28649)
*J. Sánchez-Illana,R. Molina,Pan-Pan Shi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper predicts bottom-strange molecular states using the Hidden Gauge Formalism, fitting one parameter to LHCb's excited $B_s^0$ mass and obtaining pole parameters for six states—including analogs of $D_{s0}(2317)$ and $D_{s1}(2460)$ at 5760 MeV ($0^+$) and 5802 MeV ($1^+$), and interpreting LHCb’s ~6100/6160 MeV states as $B\bar{K}^*$ and $B^*\bar{K}^*$ molecules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To predict and interpret bottom-strange hadronic molecular states—particularly flavor partners of known charmed-strange resonances ($D_{s0}(2317)$, $D_{s1}(2460)$) and recently observed LHCb states near 6100–6160 MeV—within a unified theoretical framework.

Method: Coupled-channel scattering analysis of $B^{(*)}\bar{K}^{(*)}$ systems within the Hidden Gauge Formalism; one free parameter is fixed to reproduce the mass of a newly observed excited $B_s^0$ state by LHCb; pole positions (masses and widths) are extracted from the resulting S-matrix.

Result: Predictions for six molecular poles: $B\bar{K}$ ($0^+$) at 5760 MeV, $B^*\bar{K}$ ($1^+$) at 5802 MeV; $B\bar{K}^*$ and $B^*\bar{K}^*$ states consistent with LHCb’s ~6100 MeV and ~6160 MeV structures, respectively, including quantitatively reasonable mass splitting and pole residues.

Conclusion: The $B^{(*)}\bar{K}^{(*)}$ sector supports a rich spectrum of hadronic molecules; the predicted masses and splittings strongly support interpreting the LHCb states near 6100 and 6160 MeV as $B\bar{K}^*$ and $B^*\bar{K}^*$ bound/scattering states, reinforcing the universality of heavy-light meson molecular dynamics across charm and bottom sectors.

Abstract: In this work we provide predictions for bottom-strange molecular states within the Hidden Gauge Formalism. We study the coupled-channel scattering of $B^{(*)}\bar{K}^{(*)}$ states and, by fixing only one free parameter to obtain the mass of a new excited $B_s^0$ state seen by the LHCb, we predict the pole parameters of six states in this sector. Concretely, we get that the masses of the flavor partners of the $D_{s0}(2317)$ and $D_{s1}(2460)$ states in the bottom sector are $5760$ and $5802$ MeV for the $B\bar{K}$ ($J^P=0^+$) and $B^{*}\bar{K}$ ($1^+$) states, respectively. Moreover, the recently seen states by the LHCb with masses around $6100$ and $6160$ MeV can be interpreted as $B\bar{K}^*$ and $B^*\bar{K}^*$ molecular states, according to reasonable values of the pole parameters and the splitting between these two states obtained in our calculation.

</details>


### [123] [Hadron spectra and thermodynamics for all quark flavors from a universal Hagedorn temperature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28668)
*Michał Marczenko,Larry McLerran,Krzysztof Redlich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that hadron spectra across all quark flavors—including heavy charm—follow universal string-like dynamics governed by the Hagedorn temperature and QCD string tension, consistent with lattice QCD thermodynamics and experimental spectra, without introducing new parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish universality of string-inspired hadron thermodynamics beyond light hadrons and glueballs, extending it to the heavy-flavor sector and testing consistency with lattice QCD and observed spectra.

Method: Separation of current quark mass contributions from hadron masses; mapping residual spectra onto a Hagedorn-type string spectrum characterized by the universal string tension; comparison with lattice QCD thermodynamic data for charmed hadrons and experimental hadron mass spectra.

Result: The mass- and flavor-independent string spectrum, parameterized solely by the QCD string tension, accurately reproduces lattice QCD thermodynamics for charmed hadrons and observed hadron spectra across light, strange, and charm sectors.

Conclusion: Heavy-flavor hadrons obey the same universal confining string dynamics as light hadrons and glueballs, confirming the string tension as the fundamental scale governing QCD confinement and hadron thermodynamics.

Abstract: We show that hadrons in QCD follow a spectrum determined by string dynamics characterized by a universal Hagedorn temperature linked to the string tension. While this behavior was recently established for light hadrons and glueballs, we demonstrate that the same dynamics describes the heavy-flavor sector. After separating the current quark masses, the resulting spectrum reproduces lattice QCD thermodynamics of charmed hadrons and the observed spectra of hadrons across quark flavors without additional parameters. These results reflect the universal confining dynamics of QCD through the string tension.

</details>


### [124] [Charge-Dependent Directed Flow in Symmetric Nuclear Collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28742)
*Vipul Bairathi,Kishora Nayak*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates directed flow (v1) of identified hadrons in symmetric nuclear collisions at 200 GeV using the string-melting AMPT model with improved quark coalescence, revealing system-size–dependent v1-slopes and a baryon-meson dichotomy in charge-dependent splitting (Δdv1/dy), with implications for partonic-phase dynamics and electromagnetic field contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the origin of charge-dependent directed flow splitting (Δdv1/dy) in heavy-ion collisions, disentangle contributions from partonic dynamics (e.g., quark transport, coalescence) versus final-state hadronic interactions and electromagnetic fields, and probe the role of system size and particle type (baryon vs. meson).

Method: String-melting AMPT model with improved quark coalescence; analysis of mid-rapidity v1-slope (dv1/dy) and its charge-dependent splitting (Δdv1/dy) for π±, K±, p, p̄, φ, Λ, Λ̄ across O+O to U+U collisions at √sNN = 200 GeV; separate examination in low-pT (0.2–2.0 GeV/c) and high-pT (2.0–5.0 GeV/c) ranges; centrality and nuclear mass number (A) dependence; assessment of hadronic cascade effects.

Result: At low-pT: weak system-size dependence of dv1/dy; at high-pT: strong A-dependent negative dv1/dy, indicating hard-soft asymmetry. Δdv1/dy shows clear baryon-meson dichotomy: large and A-growing splitting for p−p̄ and Λ−Λ̄, negligible for π+−π− and K+−K−. Hadronic interactions negligibly affect dv1/dy, confirming partonic/coalescence origin. AMPT baseline matches STAR Au+Au data, implying EM field effects must be added to explain full observed splitting.

Conclusion: The directed flow slope and its charge splitting are predominantly generated in the partonic phase and coalescence stage, not by hadronic rescattering. The baryon-meson dichotomy in Δdv1/dy strongly supports quark-level transport mechanisms and constrains the magnitude of electromagnetic field contributions—suggesting that transported quark charge asymmetry sets the baseline, with EM fields providing additional modulation.

Abstract: The directed flow ($v_1$) of identified hadrons ($π^{\pm}, K^{\pm}, p, \bar{p}, φ, Λ$, and $\barΛ$) is studied in symmetric nuclear collisions (O+O, Cu+Cu, Ru+Ru, Au+Au, and U+U) at $\sqrt{s_{NN}} = 200$ GeV using the string-melting version of a multiphase transport model with improved quark coalescence. The mid-rapidity $v_1$-slope ($dv_1/dy$) and its charge-dependent splitting ($Δdv_1/dy$) between particles and anti-particles are investigated as a function of nuclear mass number ($A$) and collision centrality in both low-$p_\mathrm{T}$ (0.2$-$2.0 GeV/$c$) and high-$p_\mathrm{T}$ (2.0$-$5.0 GeV/$c$) regions. At low-$p_\mathrm{T}$, the $v_1$-slope shows weak system-size dependence, while at high-$p_\mathrm{T}$ strong system-size dependence is found and it becomes negative with nuclear mass number, reflecting the hard-soft asymmetry in particle production. The charge-dependent splitting $Δdv_1/dy$ reveals a striking baryon-meson dichotomy: baryon pairs ($p-\bar{p}$ and $Λ-\barΛ$) exhibit significant splitting that grows with system size, whereas meson pairs ($π^+-π^-$ and $K^+-K^-$) show minimal splitting. The effect of final state hadronic interactions on the $v_1$-slope is found to be negligible confirming that it is primarily generated during the partonic phase and coalescence process. A comparison of the AMPT results with measurements from the STAR experiment at RHIC in Au+Au collisions establish the transported quark contribution as a baseline for the observed charge-dependent $v_1$ splitting, on top of which electromagnetic field effects must be considered.

</details>
